# DECISIONS.md

Log of non-obvious autonomous decisions made during the LifeOS build, per Section 1.3 of the build document. Append-only — never rewrite history here.

---

## D-001 | 2026-08-20 | Repo root and working directory
**Context:** Build doc says "paste into an empty directory" and gives working repo name `lifeos`. The actual directory on disk is `Life planner tool` (user-named folder, already empty).
**Decision:** Use the existing empty `Life planner tool` directory as the repo root rather than creating a nested `lifeos/` subfolder. `package.json` `name` field is set to `lifeos`.
**Rationale:** Directory was already created empty for this purpose; nesting would just add a redundant path segment with no benefit.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — a folder rename/move, not a code change.

---

## D-002 | 2026-08-20 | Local dev environment lacks Docker/Supabase CLI
**Context:** Section 3 mandates Supabase (Postgres) with mandatory RLS, and Section 12.7 requires a passing RLS test suite. This machine has Node v24 and git, but no Docker and no Supabase CLI installed, and I cannot install Docker Desktop myself (it needs an interactive installer/admin rights and a reboot-capable host).
**Decision:** Write full schema migrations, RLS policies, and the RLS isolation test suite as real, complete code targeting Supabase Postgres — but they will be unexecuted in this session. `PROGRESS.md` will state plainly that migrations/tests are written but not run, and give the exact commands to run them once Docker + `supabase` CLI are available locally.
**Rationale:** This is an environment capability gap, not a product ambiguity — it doesn't meet the bar for QUESTIONS.md (nothing here has "a real chance of requiring a rewrite" based on Richard's answer). Blocking all schema/backend work on it would violate the core autonomy rule. Building the real thing and documenting the gap is more useful than stubbing with a fake DB.
**Reversibility:** N/A — this is a statement of environment fact, not a design choice. Once Docker/Supabase CLI are installed, `supabase start` + `supabase db reset` + `pnpm test` execute exactly as written.

---

## D-003 | 2026-08-20 | Project structure — no `src/` directory
**Context:** Next.js App Router scaffolding offers an optional `src/` wrapper. The build doc references `lib/`, `app/` paths directly at repo root throughout (e.g. `lib/gifts/leadtime.ts`, `lib/ai/prompts/base.ts`).
**Decision:** No `src/` directory. `app/`, `lib/`, `components/`, `supabase/`, `docs/` all live at repo root.
**Rationale:** Matches the exact paths named throughout the spec; avoids a needless rewrite of every path reference.
**Reversibility:** Cheap but tedious — a mechanical find/replace if reversed later.

---

## D-004 | 2026-08-20 | shadcn/ui set up manually, not via `shadcn init` CLI
**Context:** `pnpm dlx shadcn@latest init` (v4.18.0) crashes consistently with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND: Cannot find package 'zod'` from inside its own bundled `dist/chunk-*.js`, reproducible on retry with cache cleared. This looks like a shadcn CLI bug/peer-dependency gap against zod v4 + strict pnpm linking, not a project misconfiguration.
**Decision:** Skip the CLI. Set up shadcn manually: `components.json`, `lib/utils.ts` (`cn()` helper), and install its peer libs directly (`class-variance-authority`, `clsx`, `tailwind-merge`, `lucide-react`, `tw-animate-css`). Individual components get added by hand-copying canonical shadcn source into `components/ui/` as each is needed during Phase 7 — this is literally what the CLI does under the hood, it just copies files.
**Rationale:** shadcn components are vendored source, not a runtime package — losing the CLI loses a convenience, not a capability. Not worth burning more time on a third-party CLI bug this early with the entire data layer still ahead.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — CLI can be retried later (e.g. after a shadcn patch release) without conflicting with hand-added components.

---

## D-005 | 2026-08-20 | "Household default" gift budget tier lives on `households`, not a new table
**Context:** Section 4.2's budget resolution order is "person + specific occasion → person + `default` → household default → hardcoded fallback," but only `person_gift_budgets` is defined in the table list — there is no `household_gift_budgets` table.
**Decision:** Added `default_gift_budget_min_cents` / `default_gift_budget_max_cents` nullable columns directly on `households` (migration `20260820000002_core_tables.sql`) rather than a new table.
**Rationale:** It's a single row of config per household, not a many-row relation — a whole table would be overkill for two nullable integers.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — could be split into its own table later via a backfill migration if it ever needs occasion-specific household defaults.

---

## D-006 | 2026-08-20 | `auth.users` → `public.users` sync via trigger; household creation stays in the app layer
**Context:** `people.user_id`, `household_members.user_id` etc. all FK to `public.users`, which extends `auth.users`. Something has to create that row the moment a person signs up, before onboarding UI runs.
**Decision:** A `security definer` trigger (`handle_new_auth_user`, migration `20260820000002`) inserts a minimal `public.users` row on every `auth.users` insert (display name from signup metadata or the email's local part). Household creation/joining is deliberately left to the app's onboarding flow, not automated in the trigger — a new user might create a household or eventually join an existing one (co-parent case), and the trigger can't know which.
**Rationale:** Keeps the DB layer minimal and correct (FK targets always exist) without hardcoding a business decision (auto-create-household) that belongs in application code.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — trigger logic is a few lines, easy to extend.

---

## D-007 | 2026-08-20 | `gifts` and `gift_suggestions` are readable only by `owner`/`adult` roles, not the full household
**Context:** Section 6.3's role table gives `child` general household read access, but a `child`-role person is very often the *recipient* on `gifts`/`gift_suggestions` rows. Letting them read the table would let a kid read their own surprise gift suggestions.
**Decision:** Unlike other person-scoped tables (which are household-readable, owner/adult-writable), `gifts` and `gift_suggestions` restrict SELECT to `owner`/`adult` roles as well (migration `20260820000008`).
**Rationale:** The spec's own product thesis is a gift/surprise engine — a hole here undermines the core differentiator the moment a second household member (a kid, once v2 co-parent/kid login lands) can log in. Doesn't affect v1 usage since Richard is the only account.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — one line change to the SELECT policy's role list.

---

## D-008 | 2026-08-20 | Added a `notifications` table (not listed in Section 4.2)
**Context:** Section 10.2 requires an "in-app — notification center, unread state" channel, but Section 4.2's table list has no table to back it — `briefs` covers the daily digest but not general notifications (e.g. an individual gift order-by alert).
**Decision:** Added `notifications` (migration `20260820000014_notifications.sql`): recipient, type, title/body, optional deep link, dispatched channels, `read_at` for unread state.
**Rationale:** Directly implied by an explicit v1 requirement (10.2); building the in-app channel without persisted, markable-read rows isn't possible.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — purely additive, no other table depends on it.

---

## D-009 | 2026-08-20 | Simplified RLS read scope: household-readable + owner/adult-writable, not a bespoke per-table visibility matrix
**Context:** Section 6.3's role table says `child`/`viewer` read scope is "own + household-visible" / "household-visible only," but "household-visible" is only ever defined precisely for one table — `calendar_events.visibility` (Section 6.4). Nothing defines an equivalent visibility flag for `people`, `person_interests`, `contact_cadences`, `user_activities`, `custody_blocks`, etc.
**Decision:** For every table without an explicit visibility model, SELECT is open to all household members regardless of role; INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE is restricted to `owner`/`adult`. The one deliberate exception is `gifts`/`gift_suggestions` (see [[D-007]]). `calendar_events` implements the full 3-tier model exactly as specified (see migration `20260820000011`).
**Rationale:** Inventing a bespoke row-visibility flag per table that the spec never defined would be guessing at product behavior with real rewrite risk if wrong — the classic case for QUESTIONS.md, except the fallback (household-readable) is safe, reversible, and doesn't block anything now, since v1 is single-user (Richard is `owner` in every household). Logged as a decision rather than a question because no plausible answer changes v1 behavior at all.
**Reversibility:** Medium — adding finer-grained visibility later means new policies per table, not a schema rewrite, since every table already carries (or can join to) `household_id`.

---

## D-010 | 2026-08-20 | `external_data_cache.source` and `ai_usage_log.feature` are `text`, not enums
**Context:** Section 4.2 shows example values (`usgs | nws | noaa_tides | odfw | solunar`; `gift_suggestion | daily_brief | weekend_plan`) but declares both columns as `text`.
**Decision:** Kept them as plain `text` rather than promoting to Postgres enums.
**Rationale:** Both sets are expected to grow (Section 4 lists more external adapters and AI features than v1 ships with — Google Maps travel, Expo push, etc.). An enum requires an `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` migration per new value; `text` doesn't. Matches the spec's literal column type.
**Reversibility:** Cheap either direction.

---

## D-011 | 2026-08-20 | RLS helper functions are `security definer`, bypassing RLS internally
**Context:** The baseline policy pattern in Section 6.2 subqueries `household_members` directly from within a policy on other tables. Doing this naively from *within `household_members`' own* policy is the classic self-referential-RLS trap in Postgres/Supabase.
**Decision:** All membership/role checks go through `security definer stable` functions (`is_household_member`, `household_role`, `is_linked_household_member`, `person_is_in_my_household`, etc. — migration `20260820000004` and subsequent table migrations) that query the underlying tables directly, bypassing RLS, rather than every policy repeating a raw subquery.
**Rationale:** This is Supabase's own documented pattern for this exact situation — avoids recursion edge cases and is materially faster (one function call vs. a repeated correlated subquery per row).
**Reversibility:** N/A — this is the standard-practice implementation of the spec's own stated policy pattern, not a deviation from it.

---

## D-012 | 2026-08-20 | Seed data uses date math relative to `current_date`, not hardcoded calendar dates
**Context:** Section 12.6 requires seed data that's "demonstrable without manual data entry" — in particular the gift engine's Phase 3 done-criteria needs "an upcoming birthday" and the brief engine needs an "overdue" contact cadence to actually exist relative to whenever the seed is loaded.
**Decision:** `supabase/seed.sql` computes birthdates, gift `occasion_date`s, and cadence `last_contact_date`s as offsets from `current_date` (e.g. Dave's birthday month/day is always `current_date + 18 days`) rather than fixed 2026 dates.
**Rationale:** A hardcoded "upcoming" birthday goes stale the moment the seed is loaded on a different day — which defeats the point of seed data meant to demonstrate the product on demand. Relative dates keep the demo scenarios (upcoming birthday within the 60-day gift-scan horizon, an overdue golf buddy) true regardless of when `supabase db reset` runs.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — purely a seed-file authoring choice, no schema impact.

---

## D-013 | 2026-08-20 | Supabase is not behind a "runs with zero keys" stub; other integrations are
**Context:** Section 12.9 says every external integration is behind an interface with a working stub, and `pnpm dev` must run with zero third-party keys configured. Section 3 also lists Supabase as the mandatory database with RLS "not optional."
**Decision:** Read 12.9 as covering the *optional* integrations it lists elsewhere by name (Anthropic, Resend, Google Maps/Mapbox, NWS/USGS/NOAA/ODFW, push, SMS) — all of which get a real stub/fallback path. Supabase itself is the persistence layer, not stubbed; `pnpm dev` requires a reachable Supabase instance (local via `supabase start`, or a hosted project's URL/keys in `.env.local`), but needs none of the other API keys.
**Rationale:** There's no such thing as "the app, but with an in-memory fake Postgres with real RLS semantics" — building that would be a bigger, riskier piece of throwaway infrastructure than the thing it's meant to unblock, and Section 3 calls RLS mandatory. The stub requirement reads as being about the *optional* integrations layered on top of the DB, not the DB itself.
**Reversibility:** N/A — a reading of intent, not a design choice with an alternative to revert to.

---

## D-014 | 2026-08-20 | AI cost-per-token rates in `lib/ai/pricing.ts` are a documented estimate
**Context:** Section 11.3 requires `ai_usage_log.estimated_cost_cents` and a per-household daily spend ceiling; Section 3 names the model as `claude-sonnet-4-6`, a model id newer than my training data, so I have no verified current pricing for it to hardcode with confidence.
**Decision:** Used `$3/MTok` input, `$15/MTok` output as placeholder rates (in line with recent Claude Sonnet-tier pricing), clearly commented as an estimate in `lib/ai/pricing.ts`, to be checked against Anthropic's actual pricing page when `docs/ai-costs.md` is written in Phase 8.
**Rationale:** The spend-ceiling *mechanism* (compare today's summed cost against `households.ai_daily_spend_ceiling_cents`, degrade gracefully) is correct and testable regardless of the exact rate; getting the rate itself right just needs a pricing-page check, not a design decision.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — one constant to update once real pricing is confirmed.

## D-021 | 2026-08-21 | Richard's answers to Q-001/Q-002/Q-003 — no code changes required
**Context:** Presented three questions at the end of the first autonomous session (QUESTIONS.md Q-001, Q-002, Q-003).
**Decision:** Richard confirmed all three as-built defaults: ODFW stays scrape-only with no manual override field; SMS/A2P 10DLC registration is not being started yet; the product keeps the "LifeOS" placeholder name for now.
**Rationale:** All three were already built to their now-confirmed answer (Option A/B/A respectively), so no code changed — this entry exists so the confirmation is on the record, not just the original recommendation.
**Reversibility:** N/A — a record of an explicit answer, not a design choice.

## D-022 | 2026-08-21 | Attempted a native-Postgres path around D-002; left PostgreSQL 17 installed but unconfigured
**Context:** Asked to keep working until genuinely blocked. D-002's Docker/Supabase-CLI gap is the single biggest unverified piece of this build, so before doing anything else I tried a workaround: install PostgreSQL natively via `winget` and hand-bootstrap a minimal Supabase-compatible `auth` schema/role shim, so the real migrations, seed data, and an RLS check could actually run against a live database without Docker.
**Decision:** The `winget install PostgreSQL.PostgreSQL.17` step succeeded and the Windows service is running. The next two steps — editing `pg_hba.conf` to a trust-auth method, or stopping the Windows service to reset the unknown auto-generated superuser password in single-user mode — were both blocked by this environment's permission classifier as system/security-configuration changes. I did not attempt further workarounds (per the tool's own guidance not to route around a denial), and left PostgreSQL installed rather than uninstall it without being asked, since removing software someone didn't approve installing is its own overreach.
**Rationale:** This is a genuine "cannot function without your input" point for this one sub-task specifically — recovering here needs either Windows admin action or a decision to just wait for Docker — but it doesn't block anything else, so the rest of the session continued rather than stopping entirely.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — `winget uninstall PostgreSQL.PostgreSQL.17` removes it cleanly; nothing in the repo depends on it (the app targets Supabase, not this instance).

---

## D-027 | 2026-08-22 | Brief cron downgraded from hourly to once-daily for Vercel Hobby-tier deployment
**Context:** Deploying to Vercel (Richard's free Hobby-tier account) failed outright: `vercel.json`'s brief cron was `0 * * * *` (hourly, by design — see the route's own comment, D-013-adjacent reasoning about per-household timezone precision), and Hobby accounts are restricted to at most one cron firing per day per job.
**Decision:** Changed the brief cron to `0 13 * * *` (once daily, ~6am Pacific during daylight saving). The route handler's per-household timezone-matching logic is unchanged — it still only generates a brief when it's that household's configured `brief_time` in their own timezone — it just gets one chance a day to catch that instead of 24. Documented the resulting precision loss (up to ~1 hour of drift across DST, and imprecise for households outside Pacific time) directly in the route's comment, along with the one-line fix (`0 * * * *`) for whenever the project is on Vercel Pro.
**Rationale:** This is a hosting-platform constraint discovered only at actual deploy time — exactly the kind of thing no amount of code review would surface. Downgrading the cron frequency is the correct fix for a free-tier deployment; the alternative (asking Richard to pay for Pro before ever seeing the app live) is a worse trade for a first look at a v1 product.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — one line in `vercel.json`, no application code changes.

---

## D-023 | 2026-08-21 | Fixed a real privacy bug: weekend planner wasn't redacting child names before the AI call
**Context:** While auditing the codebase for more unblocked work, grepped every use of `person.full_name` in `lib/` against docs/privacy.md's rule that every AI feature must build its person-facing context through `lib/ai/context.ts`'s child-token map. `lib/planner/generate.ts`'s `labelPeople()` helper — used to label `overdueCompanionLabels` fed into the weekend-plan AI prompt — used `person.full_name` directly, with no token substitution. `user_activities.preferred_companions` has no constraint against listing a child (a parent's own kid is a perfectly normal fishing/hiking companion), so this was a real path for a child's real name to reach the Anthropic API when the gift-suggestion and brief engines both correctly redact it.
**Decision:** Fixed: `generate.ts` now builds a `ChildTokenMap` from the full household roster once per call, `labelPeople()` takes and uses it, and the AI response (or the template-fallback content, which was built from the same tokenized labels) is restored to real names before rendering/storing — the same pattern already used in `lib/gifts/suggest.ts` and `lib/brief/generate.ts`.
**Rationale:** This wasn't a hypothetical — it's a straightforward miss (a third feature module re-implementing person-labeling instead of reusing the one already-correct helper) caught by systematically grepping for the unsafe pattern rather than by a specific bug report. Worth calling out here rather than folding silently into a commit message, since it's a privacy-relevant correction, not a feature.
**Reversibility:** N/A — this is a bug fix, not a design choice with an alternative.

---

## D-024 | 2026-08-21 | Fixed four integration gaps found by auditing Section 9 against what actually got wired up
**Context:** Phase 4 built and unit-tested all five weekend-planner external adapters (NWS, USGS, NOAA tides, ODFW, solunar) and Section 9.5's companion layer depends on `user_activities.preferred_companions`, but grepping `lib/planner/generate.ts` turned up that only NWS and USGS ever actually got connected. ODFW, NOAA tides, and solunar were built and tested in isolation but never called from the orchestration; the `/activities/new` form had no field to set `preferred_companions` at all — meaning Section 9.5 ("the entire product thesis in one feature... do not treat it as optional polish") could only ever be exercised by directly editing seed data, never through the app.
**Decision:**
  - Wired `getOdfwReport()` in for locations with an `odfw_zone_url` in `external_ids` (report text truncated to 400 chars before it enters the AI prompt — the scraped page can run to thousands).
  - Wired `getNoaaTidePredictions()` in for locations with a `noaa_station` id.
  - Wired `computeSolunarPeriods()` in, but *gated* on the location already being fishing-relevant (having a `usgs_gauge` or `odfw_zone_url` set) — major/minor feeding periods are meaningless noise on a golf or gym recommendation, so it doesn't run unconditionally just because it's a free local computation.
  - Added a companion multi-select (checkboxes over the household's people) and `usgsGauge`/`odfwZoneUrl`/`noaaStation` fields to the activity form, wired through to `activity_locations.external_ids`.
**Rationale:** Same category as [[D-023]] — built-and-tested components that never got connected to the feature that was supposed to use them. Worth its own entry because Section 9.5 explicitly flags the companion layer as core to the product thesis, not incidental, and because five adapters existing but only two being reachable is exactly the kind of gap that's invisible to a code review focused on any single file.
**Reversibility:** N/A — bug fixes / completing existing wiring, not new design decisions.

---

## D-025 | 2026-08-21 | Offline SQL syntax verification via `libpg-query`, to partially de-risk D-002
**Context:** D-002 established that every migration, `seed.sql`, and the RLS test suite are real but unexecuted — no Docker/Supabase CLI available. That's the single biggest unverified surface in the build. Wanted a way to reduce that risk without a live Postgres.
**Decision:** Installed `libpg-query` (the actual Postgres parser grammar, compiled to run standalone with no database connection) in an isolated scratchpad npm project — not added to this repo's `package.json`, since it's a one-time verification tool, not a runtime or dev dependency of the app. Parsed all 17 migrations, `seed.sql`, and the RLS test file: zero syntax errors. Separately, manually cross-referenced every custom SQL function definition against every call site across all migrations (11 functions, ~90 call sites) to confirm no orphaned or misspelled references and correct argument counts.
**Rationale:** Parsing isn't the same as running — it can't catch a wrong column name, a real RLS policy logic bug, or anything that needs the actual catalog/query planner. But hand-written SQL that's never executed most commonly fails on a typo or malformed statement, and that whole failure class is now ruled out. This meaningfully raises confidence in the unexecuted SQL without requiring the Docker/Postgres access this session doesn't have.
**Reversibility:** N/A — a verification step, not a code change. The scratchpad tool isn't part of the repo.

**Follow-up (same session):** used the same parser's AST output to mechanically extract every `CREATE TABLE` and `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` across all 17 migrations — the actual, ground-truth column list per table — and diffed it column-by-column against every interface in `lib/db/database.types.ts`. All 23 tables matched exactly; zero drift between the hand-written TypeScript types and the real schema. This is the check that would have caught the `gift_suggestions.category` / `calendar_events.related_activity_id` class of gap (D-015, D-018) if it had been missed — confirms both were added correctly and nothing else was missed.

---

## D-026 | 2026-08-21 | Ran the real migrations + seed + RLS end-to-end via PGlite; found and fixed a real cross-household privilege-escalation bug
**Context:** D-025's parser-based check proved every migration is syntactically valid and schema-consistent, but couldn't prove the RLS *policies* actually do what they're supposed to — that needs a real Postgres executing real queries as different simulated users. Went looking for a way to do that without Docker (still blocked per D-022) and found `@electric-sql/pglite` — Postgres compiled to WASM, running in-process, no system service, no admin permissions. Verified it supports custom schemas, roles, RLS policies, and `SET ROLE` + `set_config('request.jwt.claims', ...)` (the exact mechanism Supabase's PostgREST layer uses per-request) — everything needed to run the actual migrations and actual RLS policies for real.
**Decision:** Built a permanent harness (`supabase/tests/pglite/`) that runs all 18 migrations, the real `seed.sql`, and a suite of `authenticated`/`service_role` role-switching assertions against an in-memory PGlite database, wired into `pnpm test` as real Vitest tests (`pnpm test:rls` to run just this file). Two harness-only compatibility shims were needed (documented in `bootstrap-auth-shim.sql` and `harness.ts`): PGlite's WASM build lacks the `pgcrypto` extension, so the extension-creation line is stripped before running migration 0001 (`gen_random_uuid()` is core in PG13+ regardless, so nothing depends on the extension itself), and `crypt()`/`gen_salt()` are stubbed as no-op functions so `seed.sql` runs completely unmodified. Neither the real migrations nor `seed.sql` in the repo were changed for this.

**What it found:** the `household_members` INSERT policy's self-join bootstrap check —
```sql
not exists (select 1 from household_members existing where existing.household_id = household_members.household_id)
```
— is itself a plain SELECT against `household_members`, so it's subject to that table's own SELECT policy (visible only to existing members of that household). For a user who is NOT yet a member of the target household, that subquery always returns zero rows *regardless of whether the household already has an owner*, so the emptiness check was always true for an outsider. Net effect: **any authenticated user could add themselves as a member of any existing household**, including one that already had an owner — confirmed live against the seeded household before the fix, blocked after it. This is the exact self-referential-RLS trap D-011 already named and solved everywhere else with `security definer` helper functions; this one policy queried the table directly instead and was missed. Fixed in migration `20260820000018` with a new `household_member_count()` helper function, matching the pattern used everywhere else. Grepped for the same `not exists`-against-self pattern across all other policies — this was the only instance.

Also verified (all passing): full household isolation across every household-scoped table, the three-tier `calendar_events` visibility model (private/household/shared_with_coparent) including the pending-vs-active `household_links` transition, and the gift/gift_suggestions spoiler-safety rule (D-007) holding even for a same-household child-role member and across an active co-parent link.
**Rationale:** This is the single most valuable thing done in this follow-up pass — a real, confirmed, exploitable authorization bug, found and fixed before any live deployment, entirely because the never-executed SQL got executed. It directly validates the core premise of D-002/D-025: unexecuted SQL is a real risk, not a formality.
**Reversibility:** The bug fix is not optional — it closes a real hole. The PGlite harness itself is purely additive tooling; removing it would just mean losing this coverage, not breaking anything.

---

## D-015 | 2026-08-20 | Added `gift_suggestions.category` (migration `20260820000015`)
**Context:** Section 7.3's output requirement explicitly lists "category (used to look up shipping window)" as one of the three required fields per suggestion, but Section 4.2's `gift_suggestions` table list has no `category` column (unlike `gifts`, which has one).
**Decision:** Added it via a new migration rather than editing the already-committed `20260820000008_gifts_and_suggestions.sql` (Section 5: never edit a committed migration).
**Rationale:** 7.3's requirement is specific and functional (the category drives `order_by_date` math); losing it after generation would mean losing the ability to explain why an order-by date is what it is, or to carry the category over when a suggestion converts to a `gifts` row. Same category of gap as [[D-008]] (notifications table) — filled because a later, more specific section requires it.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — additive nullable column.

---

## D-016 | 2026-08-20 | Gift-feedback interest matching uses word-overlap between the gift description and existing interest text
**Context:** Section 7.7 requires "an explicit function" that feeds a gift's `reaction` back into interest strength, with the example "a `loved_it` on a fly-fishing item should raise the strength of the 'fly fishing' interest." Nothing in the schema directly links a `gifts` row to a `person_interests` row, and `gifts.category` (per the seed data and D-015-adjacent reasoning) holds a *shipping* category like "standard"/"handmade" — not a topical category like "fly fishing" — so it can't be used as the match key.
**Decision:** `lib/gifts/feedback.ts` matches a gift to existing interests by lowercased, stopword-filtered word overlap between `gift.description` and each `person_interests.interest` string (e.g. "Orvis fly rod combo" and "fly fishing" share the word "fly"). On `loved_it`, matched interests move up one strength tier (`casual`→`regular`→`passionate`); on `missed`, down one tier; `liked_it`/`neutral` leave strength unchanged. It does not auto-create a brand-new interest from an unmatched gift — that would need real extraction (a job for the AI layer, not a pure function) and is out of scope for this pass.
**Rationale:** This is the smallest deterministic, testable heuristic that satisfies the spec's literal example. A pure function can't reliably invent a new interest from free text; strengthening an *existing* interest on a match is the well-defined 80% case and is exactly what the spec's example describes.
**Reversibility:** Medium — the matching heuristic is isolated in one function (`interestsMatchingGift`) and easy to swap for an AI-assisted version later without touching callers.

---

## D-017 | 2026-08-20 | Solunar major/minor periods use a sampling-based transit/underfoot calculation, not a published solunar-calendar formula
**Context:** Section 9.2 says "compute locally from lat/lng + moon phase. Use `suncalc`." Suncalc gives moonrise/moonset and moon position (altitude/azimuth) directly, but not "major/minor period" — that's solunar theory layered on top, and there's no single canonical formula (published solunar calendars vary in method and don't publish their exact math).
**Decision:** `lib/external/solunar.ts` samples moon altitude every 10 minutes across the day to find moon transit (max altitude, "overhead") and moon underfoot (min altitude, opposite) for the two major periods (2h windows, centered), and uses `suncalc`'s moonrise/moonset directly for the two minor periods (1h windows, centered) — standard solunar-theory window widths.
**Rationale:** Sampling for transit/underfoot is more robust than assuming a fixed offset from solar noon (the moon's transit time shifts day to day relative to the sun), and gives a deterministic, testable result from `suncalc` primitives alone, matching the spec's "use suncalc, no external call" instruction. This is an approximation of a folk-astronomy heuristic feeding one input into weekend-planner activity scoring (Section 9.4) — not a component where a published-calendar mismatch has real product risk.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — isolated in one function; swappable for a different window-width convention or precision without touching callers.

---

## D-018 | 2026-08-20 | Added `calendar_events.related_activity_id` (migration `20260820000016`)
**Context:** Section 8.5's concrete requirement is that a `user_activity` with `requires_prep = true` gets a generated prep event before the real one ("pack fishing gear on Friday evening" ahead of a Saturday fishing block). But nothing in Section 4.2 links a `calendar_events` row to the `user_activities` row it's an instance of — `interactions.activity_id` exists for logging *past* contact, but there's no forward equivalent for *scheduled* events.
**Decision:** Added a nullable `related_activity_id` FK from `calendar_events` to `user_activities`, set when an event is created from/for a known activity (e.g. via the weekend planner, or manually in the UI). Prep-event generation (`lib/brief/prep.ts`, Phase 5) only fires for events that carry this link — it does not try to infer the connection from title text matching, which would be unreliable.
**Rationale:** Same category as [[D-008]] and [[D-015]] — a later section's concrete requirement needs a link the table list didn't include. Explicit FK over fuzzy matching because prep reminders are exactly the kind of feature that erodes trust in the brief (Section 8.4: "a brief that manufactures content trains the user to ignore it") if it fires on the wrong event or misses the right one.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — additive nullable column; UI/weekend-planner code sets it explicitly when creating an event from an activity.

---

## D-019 | 2026-08-20 | Added a `weekend_plans` table (migration `20260820000017`)
**Context:** Section 4.2's table list has no persistence for weekend planner output, but Section 11.3 lists `weekend_plan` as a first-class AI feature alongside `gift_suggestion` (-> `gift_suggestions`) and `daily_brief` (-> `briefs`) — both of which persist. The brief engine (Section 8.2) also needs to read "weekend planning output if today is Wednesday-Friday," which requires the plan to exist somewhere queryable, not be recomputed inline during brief generation.
**Decision:** Added `weekend_plans` (household_id, for_date [the covered Saturday], content_json, content_markdown, generated_at, model_version) — same shape convention as `briefs`. Unique on `(household_id, for_date)` so re-running the job for an already-planned weekend updates rather than duplicates.
**Rationale:** Same pattern as [[D-008]], [[D-015]], [[D-018]]. Without persistence, every Wed/Thu/Fri brief would re-invoke the weekend-plan AI call, multiplying cost against the very ceiling Section 11.3 asks for and making the "once a week" cost projection in Section 11.3 ("roughly... one weekend plan per week") impossible to actually hit.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — additive table, no other table depends on it.

---

## D-020 | 2026-08-20 | Condition-data activity score is left neutral (null) — no fishing-condition thresholds fabricated
**Context:** Section 9.4 weights "condition data (flow/temp/tide/solunar where applicable)" as one of five scoring components. Judging whether a given river flow/gauge-height/tide is actually *good* for an activity (e.g. "245 cfs is decent for fly fishing at Dexter Reservoir") requires domain-expert thresholds this build has no verified source for — this isn't in the spec, and guessing a plausible-looking range would be exactly the kind of unsupported claim Section 9.2 explicitly warns against ("never assert a condition it did not retrieve from a named source").
**Decision:** `lib/planner/generate.ts` fetches and displays real USGS/NWS condition data (so the narration can cite it), but `conditionDataScore` is always `null`, which `scoreActivity()` already treats as neutral (50) by design (see `lib/planner/scoring.ts`).
**Reversibility:** Cheap — swap in a real scorer per activity type later without touching the aggregation contract; likely a QUESTIONS.md-worthy follow-up once Richard can supply his own "good conditions" thresholds per spot.

---

## D-028 | 2026-08-22 | Root-caused the live login failure: seed.sql's manual `auth.users` insert left GoTrue token columns NULL instead of ''
**Context:** After deploying to Vercel and wiring up the real Supabase project, logging in as the seeded demo user (`richard@example.com`) failed on the live site. It first looked like a Next.js Server Action / cookie problem (a temporary `/api/debug/session` route showed zero cookies after a login attempt), but calling Supabase's `/auth/v1/token?grant_type=password` endpoint directly with `curl` — bypassing the Next.js app entirely — reproduced the identical `500 {"error_code":"unexpected_failure","msg":"Database error querying schema"}`, proving the failure was inside GoTrue/Postgres, not in cookie handling.
**Decision:** `seed.sql`'s `insert into auth.users (...)` only listed 11 columns, leaving `confirmation_token`, `recovery_token`, `email_change`, `email_change_token_new`, `email_change_token_current`, `phone_change`, `phone_change_token`, and `reauthentication_token` at their column default, which is `NULL`. GoTrue's Go client scans these as strings on every auth query (including plain password login) and errors on `NULL`. Fixed by explicitly inserting `''` for all eight columns in `seed.sql`, and ran the equivalent `update auth.users set ... = ''` against the already-seeded live database (SQL Editor) so the existing demo user is fixed without a full re-seed.
**Rationale:** This is a well-known Supabase footgun specific to inserting directly into `auth.users` via raw SQL instead of the Auth signup API — GoTrue's schema assumes empty string, not NULL, for these token columns. The direct-curl test against the Auth endpoint was the deciding piece of evidence: it ruled out the entire Next.js/cookie layer in one request, which is why the earlier debug-route investigation was on the wrong track.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — column-level data fix, no schema change; the `seed.sql` fix is purely additive to the existing insert statement.

---

## D-029 | 2026-08-22 | Fixed a real bug: the homepage's on-demand brief generation used the wrong Supabase client, tripping `briefs`' intentional no-insert-for-users RLS policy
**Context:** With login fixed (D-028), the live homepage still 500'd after signing in. Vercel logs showed `42501: new row violates row-level security policy for table "briefs"`. `briefs`' migration (`20260820000012_briefs_and_system.sql`) deliberately has no insert policy for regular authenticated users — by design, only the service-role-driven cron job (`app/api/cron/brief/route.ts`) is meant to write brief rows. But `app/(app)/page.tsx`'s fallback path (generate today's brief on the spot if the cron hasn't produced one yet, e.g. a user opening the app before their household's `brief_time`) was calling `generateDailyBrief()` with the request-scoped, RLS-bound `supabase` client from `requireHouseholdContext()`, not a service-role client — so its own insert tripped the same policy the cron job was designed to bypass. The same bug was also the (less obviously connected) cause of a logged `[nws] forecast unavailable ... [object Object]` error on the same request: `generateDailyBrief` also writes weather/tide/gauge lookups into `external_data_cache` (also service-role-only for writes), through the same client parameter.
**Decision:** `app/(app)/page.tsx` now calls `generateDailyBrief()` with a fresh `createSupabaseServiceRoleClient()` for the generation step specifically, while still using the request-scoped client for the RLS-respecting read (`getBriefForPersonAndDate`, `briefsRepo.getById`) right before and after it.
**Rationale:** The RLS policy itself is correct and intentional (regular users should never be able to fabricate their own brief rows client-side); the bug was in which client the on-demand fallback used to perform a privileged, system-initiated write. Matching the cron route's own pattern (service-role client for generation) was the minimal fix that preserves the RLS design instead of loosening it.
**Reversibility:** Cheap — one client swap in one call site, no schema or policy change.

---

## D-030 | 2026-08-22 | Added a cross-app quick-capture feature (floating button, voice or text, AI-routed with clarifying questions)
**Context:** Richard asked for a button on every page to dictate or type a freeform note that gets stored "where appropriate," asking clarifying questions when the target is ambiguous — plus a real calendar month-grid (previously an agenda list) and fuller CRM-style person pages. No section of the original build spec covers freeform capture; this is new scope.
**Decision:**
- **Speech-to-text**: the browser's native Web Speech API (`SpeechRecognition`/`webkitSpeechRecognition`), not a server-side transcription service. It's free, requires no additional API key, and needs no new dependency — but it's Chrome/Edge/Safari-only and unavailable in, e.g., Firefox. The capture panel (`components/capture/capture-button.tsx`) always shows a text-input fallback and only shows the mic button when `window.SpeechRecognition` is actually present, so the feature never blocks on browser support.
- **Routing/clarification**: one new AI feature, `quick_capture` (`lib/ai/prompts/capture.ts`), following the same system-prompt + Zod-validated-JSON pattern as `brief`/`gift-suggestion`/`weekend-plan`. The model resolves the note against the household's real people (through the same `buildChildTokenMap` redaction every other AI call uses — [[D-023]]) and returns either one clarifying question, an "unrecognized" note, or a structured action (`add_interest`, `log_interaction`, `record_gift`, `create_calendar_event`, `append_person_note`, `add_gift_budget`). The client (`components/capture/capture-button.tsx`) resends the full turn history each request rather than the app maintaining server-side conversation state — `callAi()` is a single-turn wrapper by design (one system prompt, one user prompt, logged once to `ai_usage_log`), and this keeps the capture endpoint (`app/api/capture/route.ts`) stateless like every other route in the app.
- **Writes go through the normal RLS-bound client**, not the service role — unlike `briefs` ([[D-029]]), every table this feature writes to already has an insert policy for a regular owner/adult household member, so no privilege escalation is needed; the route also re-validates the model's chosen `personId` against the household's actual people list before writing (defense in depth beyond RLS).
- **`record_gift` always records a gift *idea*** (`status: "idea"`), never a completed gift — recording something already given stays on the person page's existing "Record gift" form, which captures a reaction. Keeps the capture schema from needing a full status/reaction sub-flow for what should be a fast, low-friction action.
- **Calendar month grid**: `app/(app)/calendar/page.tsx` rewritten as a server-rendered month grid (`month`/`day` search params drive navigation and selection — no client JS needed for the grid itself), with the selected day's agenda listed below it. Event/custody indicator dots per day; the previous 14-day agenda-list view is gone rather than kept alongside it, since the grid's dots already give the same at-a-glance density plus a full month's context.
- **CRM person page**: added phone/email display, upcoming events (new `listUpcomingEventsForPerson` in `lib/db/repositories/calendar.ts`, joining `event_attendees` → `calendar_events`), a recent-interaction-history list (`listInteractionsForPerson`, already existed but wasn't surfaced), and a 14-day custody summary for children — all additive to the existing interests/budgets/gifts/cadence sections already there.
**Rationale:** Matches the existing codebase's established patterns (prompt/schema/parse-defensively for AI; server-rendered search-param state for navigation) rather than introducing a new one, keeping this large a feature additive rather than a parallel architecture.
**Reversibility:** Medium — the capture feature is fully additive (one new route, one new prompt file, one new component, one `<CaptureButton />` mount in the layout) and can be removed by deleting those and the layout line. The calendar rewrite replaced the old list view outright; reverting means restoring the previous file from git history.

## D-031 | 2026-08-23 | Root-caused and fixed "Invalid UUID" on every create/update — two independent bugs, not one
**Context:** A QA pass against production reported every create Server Action failing with an inline "Invalid UUID" error and the form wiped, plus a hard crash on "Log contact today." Reproducing this live surfaced something worse first: nearly every mutation — including ones the QA brief said worked (Settings save, Edit person) — actually redirected to `/login` with the session cookie cleared and *no server-side error logged at all*, discarding the write silently. That symptom and the QA brief's literal "Invalid UUID" text turned out to be two separate, independent bugs layered on top of each other.

**Bug 1 — a real Next.js 16.3.1 framework issue, not application code:** A Server Action bound natively via `<form action={fn}>` (or invoked as the `useActionState` dispatch function through that binding) reliably fails when the current route sits under `app/(app)/layout.tsx` — an async layout that itself calls a redirect-capable auth check (`requireHouseholdContext()`). The action's own code never runs (confirmed with a completely trivial no-op action carrying zero Supabase calls — it still failed identically); the response embeds `/login`'s RSC payload instead. Proved this was structural, not an app bug, by: (a) the identical trivial action succeeding when moved to a route with no parent route group at all, (b) a plain `POST` **Route Handler** (`/api/capture`) working reliably throughout with the exact same session cookie, and (c) a direct "use server" function call via `startTransition()` (no form binding — `LogInteractionButton`'s existing pattern) working fine even nested under the same layout. The fix has two parts: flattened `app/(auth)/{login,signup}` out of their own route group into plain `app/login`, `app/signup` folders (they never needed the grouping — no shared layout beyond the root one — and having two sibling route groups was part of what triggered this), and, for every remaining form under `(app)`, replaced `<form action={dispatch}>` with a plain `<form>` whose button manually calls `dispatch(new FormData(formRef.current))` on click (`reportValidity()` first, to keep native HTML validation). Calling the exact same `useActionState`-managed dispatch function works fine — only binding it to the form's `action` prop is broken.
**Bug 2 — the QA brief's literal "Invalid UUID" text:** `lib/db/schemas.ts`'s shared `uuid` helper was `z.uuid()`, which enforces strict RFC 4122 version/variant nibbles. The seed data's hand-typed, readable ids (`20000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001` and similar) don't have a valid version nibble, so every create against the seeded demo household failed Zod validation with the message `"Invalid UUID"` — before ever reaching Postgres, which has no such constraint on its own `uuid` column type and accepts these ids fine. This is why the bug was specific to the demo account: a real signup's household id comes from `gen_random_uuid()`, which is RFC-4122-valid and passes either validator. Fixed by switching the shared helper to `z.guid()` (same 8-4-4-4-12 hex shape Postgres enforces, no version/variant constraint) — confirmed via `node -e` against both ids directly before touching the schema.
**Why bug 1 wasn't the QA brief's own description:** unclear — possibly timing-dependent on this Next.js version, or the tester's specific action ID/build state at that moment. Both bugs are independently confirmed and independently fixed regardless; regression tests added in `lib/db/schemas.test.ts` (D-031 regression: the seed household id now parses; a genuinely invalid string still doesn't) and every fixed flow (create person/event/custody/activity, settings save, edit person, add interest, set cadence, sign out) was re-verified end-to-end against the live deployment, including a direct database check that each write actually persisted.
**Rationale:** Framework-level bugs need framework-level evidence, not application-level guesses — the trivial-action isolation test (zero app code, still fails) was what finally separated "something is wrong with my auth code" from "something is wrong with how Next.js dispatches this class of request," after two plausible-but-wrong hypotheses (a `getUser()` refresh-token race, a CSRF Origin/Host mismatch) were each tried, deployed, and disproved by direct evidence rather than assumed correct.
**Reversibility:** The route-group flattening is a plain folder move (one import path updated) and safe to revert if a future Next.js release fixes the underlying issue. The manual-dispatch pattern is a mechanical, low-risk change per form. The `z.guid()` swap is a one-line, low-risk loosening that matches Postgres's actual constraint rather than an arbitrary stricter one.

---

## D-032 | 2026-08-24 | The React #441 pattern: no mutation ever caught a database error, and nothing bounded the blast radius when one escaped
**Context:** Round 2 QA reproduced a third distinct full-app crash (React error #441) — adding a duplicate interest — after two prior passes each found and patched a different instance (D-026's RLS bug, and whatever produced the original "Log contact today" crash). The brief asked for the pattern, not another instance-level patch, since patching instances without the pattern is exactly why a third one appeared.
**Root cause, in one paragraph:** every mutating Server Action in this codebase validated its input with Zod, then called straight into a repository method (`personInterestsRepo.create`, etc.) with no `try/catch` around it. `lib/db/repository.ts`'s `create`/`update`/`remove` all `throw error` on any Postgres failure — a unique-constraint violation, a check-constraint violation, an RLS denial, a dropped connection — by design, so the caller could handle it. Nothing did. An uncaught throw inside a Server Action propagates as an unhandled server error, and until this pass there was no `error.tsx` anywhere in the tree to catch it, so Next's default behavior took over: blank the page, show a digest id, done. Adding an interest a person already has hits `person_interests_person_interest_unique` (added in migration `20260820000007`, present since the very first build) — this bug has been reproducible since day one; it just hadn't been tried by a duplicate-add until this pass.
**Decision — two-part, matching "instance vs. pattern":**
1. **The pattern fix:** `lib/db/errors.ts`'s `friendlyMutationError(error, messages)` is now the one place that turns a caught Postgres/Zod error into UI copy, mapping `23505` (unique violation) / `23514` (check violation) to a caller-supplied message and everything else to a safe fallback — never the raw `error.message`, which is exactly what was leaking the schema (`max_cents must be >= min_cents`, `ends_at must be >= starts_at`) and the config detail (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is not configured`) straight into the UI. Every action that writes to the database (`app/(app)/people/[id]/actions.ts`, `app/(app)/settings/actions.ts`, `app/api/{people,capture,calendar/events,calendar/custody,activities}/*`) now wraps its write in `try/catch` and routes through this helper. `createRepository` gained a fourth primitive, `upsert(client, values, conflictColumns)`, and `add_interest`/`add_budget` use it instead of `create` — re-adding an interest or re-setting a budget for an occasion that already has one is a normal thing to do, not an error, so there's nothing left to catch for those two specifically.
2. **The backstop:** `app/(app)/error.tsx` and `app/global-error.tsx` — so the *next* uncaught error (a genuinely new cause this pass didn't anticipate) degrades to a retry screen instead of a blank page. This is the part that actually satisfies "no user action may ever blank the app": the pattern fix closes the causes found this pass, the boundary closes every cause not yet found.
**Next.js 16.3 gotcha hit while building the boundary:** the error-boundary recovery callback is `retry` (stable as of 16.3.0), not `reset` — `reset` is a separate, rarely-needed function that clears error state without re-fetching. Training data / muscle memory says `reset`; this build's docs (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/.../error.md`) say `retry`. Using the wrong name compiles fine and produces a silently-dead button.
**User-facing error strings changed this pass** (raw → friendly): `max_cents must be >= min_cents` → "Max must be at least the minimum."; `ends_at must be >= starts_at` → "End time must be after the start time." (custody's own copy, "End date can't be before the start date.", was already right and is now also the custody *schema's* message, not just the route handler's ad hoc check); `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is not configured` / "AI capture isn't set up yet — it needs an Anthropic API key configured on the server." → "Gift ideas are temporarily unavailable. Try again in a few minutes." / "Weekend planning is temporarily unavailable. Try again in a few minutes." / "Quick Capture is temporarily unavailable. Try again in a few minutes." (each surface gets its own copy, all equally silent about *why*); the capture route's catch-all used to interpolate `error.message` directly into "Couldn't save that: …" — now routed through `friendlyMutationError` like everything else.
**Also fixed alongside this, same investigation:** `addInterestAction`'s input wasn't cleared after a successful add (the thing that made a second, crashing click so easy to trigger by accident) — now resets via `form.reset()` on success; the Add/Save buttons already disabled while pending from the D-031 pass. Contact cadence status ("Overdue") never reflected same-day contact because `logInteractionAction` wrote to the `interactions` log table but nothing ever updated `contact_cadences.last_contact_date` — the two tables were simply never wired together (`lib/db/repositories/contact.ts`'s new `recordContactForCadence`, called from both `logInteractionAction` and the capture route's `log_interaction` case). Future birthdates now rejected server-side (create and edit) and `estimateAgeYears` floors at `null` instead of returning a negative number, in case any bad data predates this validation. `/people/[id]` gained a back link to `/people` — one line, noticed while already in the file for the cadence fix.
**Rationale:** A per-instance patch (catch this one constraint violation) would have shipped a fourth crash the next time someone hit an RLS denial, a foreign-key violation on a delete, or a dropped connection mid-write — all of which go through the exact same unguarded `repository.create/update/remove` calls this pass audited. Fixing the shared helper plus the boundary means the next uncaught cause is a UX regression to fix at leisure, not a P0 blank-page incident to firefight.
**Reversibility:** Cheap. `friendlyMutationError` and the two error boundaries are additive; removing them reverts to the pre-D-032 (broken) behavior with no other side effects. `upsert` is additive to the repository factory.

---

## D-033 | 2026-08-25 | Custody schedules: a generic N-day cycle engine + a unified calendar with a custody layer
**Context:** Round 2's Phase 2 named custody "the differentiator, and it is the weakest module": multi-day blocks only rendered on their start date, nothing showed who-with-whom, every handover claimed a hardcoded 5pm regardless of what was entered, there was no way to express a recurring pattern at all (every custody block was hand-typed one at a time), and the responsible-parent picker offered grandparents alongside actual co-parents. Presented two explicit trade-off choices before building anything (recurrence model: named presets only vs. a generic cycle engine; schedule view: a dedicated custody calendar vs. a layer on the existing unified calendar) — user chose the generic engine and the unified-calendar layer.
**Recurrence model:** a `custody_schedules` row stores a repeating cycle as explicit data — `cycle_length_days` (1-90) plus `cycle_assignments`, an array of `{dayIndex, responsiblePersonId}` — projected onto real dates from an `anchor_date`. `lib/custody/schedule.ts` is the pure, DB-free engine (`cycleAssignmentForDate`, `projectCustodySchedule`, `findGaps`); `CUSTODY_PRESET_LABELS`/`buildPresetCycle` are just pre-filled cycle definitions for the four named real-world patterns (week-on/week-off, alternating weekends, 2-2-3, 2-2-5-5) — the engine itself doesn't know a preset was used. This is what makes "2-2-3" (whose true period is 14 days, not 7) and any family's actual non-named arrangement both representable as data instead of needing new code per pattern. A schedule is a *generator*, not the thing every reader queries: `lib/custody/materialize.ts` projects a 90-day rolling window, merges consecutive same-parent days into runs, and writes them as ordinary `custody_blocks` rows tagged with `custody_schedule_id`. Calendar rendering, the person page's custody card, and brief generation all keep querying `custody_blocks` exactly as before and are untouched by this — regenerating a schedule (or deleting it) only ever touches blocks it produced; a hand-created one-off block is never affected.
**Schedule view:** no new calendar surface. `app/(app)/calendar/page.tsx` gained a `view=custody` search-param toggle showing custody blocks only, with a deterministic per-child color legend (`lib/custody/colors.ts`) reused on every custody item's dot regardless of view. `/calendar/custody` is a management hub (list schedules with a friendly detected-preset label, delete with confirm; list the next 14 days of actual blocks) — `/calendar/custody/new` is the recurrence editor (preset picker with a live 14-day who's-responsible preview and gap warnings via `findGaps`, or a custom per-day cycle grid) and `/calendar/custody/one-off` (moved from the old `/calendar/custody/new`) is unchanged in purpose but gained real `handoverTime`/`location` fields.
**Two real bugs fixed along the way, caught before/without a separate report:**
1. `listCustodyBlocksForChildInRange`/`listCustodyBlocksForHouseholdInRange` filtered only `starts_at >= X AND starts_at < Y` — a multi-day block starting before the query window was never fetched at all for days after its start, not merely mis-rendered. This was the actual root cause behind "multi-day custody blocks only render on the start date," deeper than the calendar page's own day-spanning logic (which also needed a fix — building `byDay` now iterates `eachDayOfInterval` per item instead of indexing only the start day). Fixed to a proper overlap condition (`starts_at < endWindow AND ends_at > startWindow`).
2. The phantom-5pm complaint wasn't actually a rendering bug — no form ever collected a real handover time, so every block was written with a literal hardcoded `"17:00:00"` regardless of input. Both the one-off form and the schedule materializer now use a real `handoverTime`/`handover_time` field end to end (default `17:00`, but an actual default a user can change, not a hardcoded constant).
**Responsible-parent picker fix:** `"parent"` as a `relationship_type` means "this person is the household self-person's own parent" (a grandparent to the kids), not "a co-parent of the kids" — `lib/custody/eligible-parents.ts`'s `filterEligibleResponsibleAdults` restricts the picker to `["self", "co_parent", "spouse", "partner"]`, used by both the one-off form and the schedule editor. Household membership (deferred, Phase 4) will eventually make this precise; this is the closest approximation available without it.
**Verification:** `lib/custody/schedule.test.ts` (17 tests, pure engine) plus full `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` (244/244 tests passing) before deploy. Live-verified end to end against production after the new migration was applied: created a 2-2-3 schedule for a real child, confirmed the live 14-day preview matched the engine's own math, confirmed materialized blocks merged into correct multi-day runs with real location/time, confirmed an interior day of a multi-day span (not just the start day) shows the block on the main calendar, confirmed the `view=custody` toggle and color legend, confirmed the responsible-parent picker on both forms excludes the seeded grandparent and offers only the two real parents, and confirmed schedule delete removes its future blocks while leaving hand-created blocks untouched.
**Rationale:** A fixed enum of named patterns would have covered the four common cases but hit the same wall as before the moment a family's real arrangement didn't match a name — exactly what "the differentiator" needs not to do. A dedicated custody calendar would have fragmented where a parent looks for "what's happening this week" into two places; a layer on the existing calendar keeps one surface with an optional filter.
**Reversibility:** Medium. The migration adds two new tables and two new nullable/FK columns on `custody_blocks` — additive, no data loss on revert. The generator/materializer split means deleting the custody-schedules feature entirely still leaves every previously materialized block intact as an ordinary custody block (just orphaned from its schedule via `ON DELETE SET NULL`), so reverting the UI doesn't blank anyone's calendar.

---

## D-034 | 2026-08-26 | The Anthropic key going live exposed two more instances of the D-029 wrong-client bug — gift suggestions and weekend planning, not just briefs

**Context:** With `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` finally set in Vercel, this was the first time any of the three AI features had actually run against a live model in production — every prior pass could only verify the health-gating and error-copy around them, never the features themselves. The very first real click ("Get gift ideas") crashed to the global error boundary. Logs showed `42501: new row violates row-level security policy for table "ai_usage_log"` — the exact same bug class D-029 found and fixed for `briefs`, just never caught there because nothing had exercised this code path with a working key before.

**Root cause:** `ai_usage_log`'s migration (`20260820000012`) has, by explicit design, no insert policy for regular authenticated users — only a service-role client can write it, matching `briefs`, `weekend_plans`, `external_data_cache`, and `notifications`, all documented the same way. But `lib/ai/client.ts`'s `callAi()` — the single shared wrapper every AI feature calls through — logged every call by writing to `ai_usage_log` (and checked the day's spend by reading it) using whatever `dbClient` its caller happened to pass in, which in every real call site is the request-scoped, user-authenticated client. This made the bug systemic rather than local to one feature: gift suggestions, weekend planning, and Quick Capture all route through `callAi`, so all three were one real API call away from hitting it, but it could only surface once a key existed to make a real call in the first place.

Fixing `callAi` immediately surfaced the *second* instance: generating a weekend plan crashed the same way, this time on `weekend_plans` — a table with the identical "service-role-only, no insert policy for regular users" design, hit by `lib/planner/generate.ts`'s `weekendPlansRepo.create(client, …)` where `client` was, again, the on-demand Server Action's request-scoped client (`app/(app)/calendar/actions.ts`'s `generateWeekendPlanAction`). This is the exact same shape of bug D-029 fixed for the homepage's on-demand brief fallback, just in a sibling feature nobody had re-audited for it.

**Decision — two separate fixes, matched to where each bug actually lives:**
1. **`lib/ai/client.ts`**: `callAi` now creates its own `createSupabaseServiceRoleClient()` internally and uses it for both `sumAiSpendToday` and the post-call `aiUsageLogRepo.create`, regardless of what client the caller passed for its own reads/writes. Fixed once, in the one shared function, rather than requiring all four call sites (gifts, weekend plan, brief, capture) to each remember to inject a service-role client just for logging — an internal implementation detail of `callAi` that callers shouldn't need to know about.
2. **`app/(app)/calendar/actions.ts`**: `generateWeekendPlanAction` now calls `generateWeekendPlan()` with a fresh service-role client for the whole generation step, matching D-029's exact precedent for `briefs` — `household.id` is already resolved and RLS-checked by `requireHouseholdContext()` before this point, so handing the rest of generation a privileged client doesn't broaden which household's data it can touch. This also incidentally fixes the `[nws] forecast unavailable … [object Object]`-style log noise from the same request: `generateWeekendPlan`'s weather/tide/gauge lookups cache through `external_data_cache`, itself service-role-write-only, through the same client parameter — so those external calls were silently degrading (treated as "unavailable," not fatal) every time this path ran, not just failing loudly like the final `weekend_plans` insert did.
3. **Backstop, matching D-032's own pattern**: `generateWeekendPlanAction` had no `try/catch` at all — the RLS error was propagating as a fully uncaught Server Action exception straight to the global error boundary. Wrapped the call in `try/catch` with `friendlyMutationError`, so a future, still-unanticipated failure in this path degrades to a friendly inline message instead of blanking the calendar page. `generateGiftSuggestions`/`generateWeekendPlan` themselves already catch `AiUnavailableError`/`AiBudgetExceededError` explicitly (by design, per `callAi`'s own doc comment) — this backstop is for everything else, the same gap D-032 closed everywhere else it was found.

**Why this wasn't caught during Phase 1/Phase 2 verification:** every AI-feature check run so far (D-032's pass, this session's Phase 2 work) could only confirm the *health-gating* — that buttons correctly disabled themselves and showed the right unavailable copy — because `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` was never set in Vercel until now. The code path from "key exists" to "AI call succeeds" to "result gets logged/persisted" had literally never executed in production before this session. `AiUnavailableError` (missing key) short-circuits `callAi` before either RLS-guarded operation runs, so a correctly-gated-but-broken feature and a correctly-working feature look identical from the outside until the key is live.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test` (244/244) before each of the two deploys. Live-verified all three AI features end to end against production after both fixes: gift suggestions for Emma Smith produced three genuinely personalized, context-aware suggestions (correctly referencing her actual interests and gift history); weekend plan generation produced a real scored recommendation incorporating live weather, travel time, USGS gauge data, and solunar timing, and correctly flagged an overdue companion; Quick Capture correctly asked a clarifying question for an ambiguous name ("Emma" — two kids on file), then correctly resolved and persisted the interest once answered. Test data from this verification (a fabricated "drawing" interest used only to exercise the capture round-trip) was removed afterward; the real gift suggestions and weekend plan generated are left in place as genuinely useful output, not test artifacts.

**Rationale:** Matching D-029's established pattern (service-role client for the generation step, RLS-respecting client for surrounding reads) instead of inventing a new approach keeps this codebase's now-repeated "on-demand job invocation" shape consistent across `briefs` and `weekend_plans`. Fixing the shared `ai_usage_log` write inside `callAi` itself, rather than at each of its four call sites, prevents this exact bug from reappearing the next time a new AI feature is added and its author reasonably assumes `callAi` handles its own logging correctly (which, now, it does).

**Reversibility:** Cheap. Both fixes are client-selection changes at existing call sites — no schema or RLS policy changes, no data migration. Reverting either restores the pre-D-034 (broken) behavior with no other side effects.

---

## D-035 | 2026-08-26 | Onboarding was completely broken for every new signup — a chicken-and-egg RLS bug, not a policy bug

**Context:** Flagged in KNOWN-ISSUES.md as "unverified live with a fresh signup" — turned out to be the single biggest blocker in the app: creating a household during onboarding failed 100% of the time with `42501: new row violates row-level security policy for table "households"`, meaning no new user (including a fresh real signup) could ever get past the very first onboarding step.

**Root cause:** `createHouseholdWithOwner` did two separate client-side inserts — `households` then `household_members` — and, like every `repository.create()` call in this codebase, each one used `.select("*").single()`, i.e. requested `RETURNING`. Postgres enforces RLS on `RETURNING` output using the table's *SELECT* policy, not just the statement's own INSERT policy. `households`' SELECT policy is `is_household_member(id)` — but at the exact moment the household row is inserted, the current user is *not yet* a household member; that only happens in the very next statement. So the SELECT-policy check on the `RETURNING` clause failed every single time, and Postgres reported it as an RLS violation on `households` — even though the actual INSERT policy (`auth.uid() is not null`) was trivially satisfied the whole time. This is why an extended live investigation that replaced the INSERT policy with a maximally permissive `WITH CHECK (true)` still reproduced the identical failure: the INSERT policy was never the problem.

**Fix:** Added a `SECURITY DEFINER` function, `create_household_with_owner(household_name)`, that performs both inserts atomically as the table owner — the same bypass-RLS-safely pattern this schema already uses for `is_household_member()`/`household_role()`. It resolves `auth.uid()` internally and only ever adds the calling user as `owner`, so it can't be used to bootstrap a household on someone else's behalf. `createHouseholdWithOwner` now calls this RPC instead of doing the two inserts directly (`supabase/migrations/20260826000001_fix_household_bootstrap_returning.sql`).

**Verification:** Reproduced identically via three independent methods before the fix (direct SQL simulation with `SET LOCAL role`/claims, a real curl POST to the live production PostgREST API using a genuine password-login access token, and a temporary debug function) — all confirmed `auth.uid()` resolved correctly and the INSERT check expression evaluated `true`, yet the insert still failed, which is what pointed at the `RETURNING`/SELECT-policy interaction rather than the policy text itself. After the fix: `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (244/244 tests). Live-verified end to end against production with a real account that had never completed onboarding: household creation succeeded with no error, onboarding redirected to the home dashboard, the household name appeared correctly in the header, and the "self" person record was confirmed created in the database (correctly excluded from the People list, which by design only lists people other than yourself).

**Rationale:** A narrower fix (e.g. dropping the household SELECT policy's dependency on membership, or having the client insert without requesting `RETURNING` and looking the row up separately) would have either weakened a real security boundary or just relocated the same ordering problem to a second round-trip. A single atomic, security-definer bootstrap function is the standard, minimal-surface-area way to solve a "you need to already be a member to read the thing you're creating membership for" ordering problem, and matches this schema's own established pattern for similar cases.

**Reversibility:** Cheap. Additive migration (one new function, no schema/column changes); the households table's policies themselves are unchanged from their original design. Reverting the repository function call would restore the pre-fix (broken) two-insert behavior with no other side effects.

---

## D-036 | 2026-08-26 | Every instant delete across the app got the same two-click confirm already built for custody schedules

**Context:** KNOWN-ISSUES.md 1.5 (flagged high priority, data-safety): activities, gift history, interests, gift budgets, and calendar events/custody blocks all deleted the instant their button was clicked, with no confirmation and no undo — a single misclick permanently destroyed data. Person archiving had the same instant-action shape. Custody *schedule* deletion already had a correct two-click "click → Confirm delete → click again" pattern (`app/(app)/calendar/custody/delete-schedule-button.tsx`), it just hadn't been generalized to anywhere else.

**Fix:** Extracted the confirm-arm-then-execute behavior into a reusable hook (`lib/hooks/use-confirm-delete.ts`) and a shared component (`components/ui/confirm-delete-button.tsx`, with a `variant="icon"` mode for the compact inline "×" style used on chips like interests). First click arms the button (visibly switches to "Confirm delete" / destructive styling); a second click within 4 seconds executes; the arm auto-resets otherwise so a stray later click can't slip through as a confirm. Wired into every previously-instant delete: calendar events and custody blocks (`app/(app)/calendar/delete-item-button.tsx`), interests/budgets/gifts (`app/(app)/people/[id]/person-forms.tsx`), activity removal (`app/(app)/activities/deactivate-button.tsx`), and person archiving (`app/(app)/people/[id]/edit/edit-person-form.tsx`).

**Error handling folded in for free:** every underlying server action this pass touched (`deleteCalendarEventAction`, `deleteCustodyBlockAction`, `deleteInterestAction`, `deleteBudgetAction`, `deleteGiftAction`, `deactivateActivityAction`, `archivePersonAction`) previously called its repository `remove()`/`update()` completely unguarded — a repeat of the exact D-032 gap (uncaught mutation error → global error boundary) just never caught for these seven actions specifically because a `remove()` rarely fails and no one had exercised the failure path. All seven now wrap the mutation in `try/catch` routed through the existing `friendlyMutationError` helper and return `{ error: string | null }` instead of `void`, so a delete that fails (network blip, an unexpected FK constraint) surfaces an inline message on the button instead of crashing the page.

**One real subtlety handled explicitly:** `archivePersonAction` calls `redirect("/people")` on success — Next.js implements `redirect()` as a thrown, digest-tagged (`NEXT_REDIRECT`) signal that its own client runtime intercepts to perform navigation, not a normal application error. Generic try/catch in the new shared hook would otherwise swallow that signal and show a bogus "couldn't delete" message instead of navigating. `useConfirmDelete` explicitly detects `NEXT_REDIRECT`/`NEXT_NOT_FOUND` digests and rethrows them unchanged, leaving Next's own handling intact while still catching genuine failures.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (244/244 tests, zero lint/type errors). Live-verified after deploy: armed-then-confirmed delete on a calendar event, an interest chip, a gift budget, and an activity all required the second click to actually remove the row (single click alone left the record untouched and showed "Confirm delete"), and the 4-second auto-reset was confirmed by waiting past the window and observing the button revert to its unarmed label.

**Rationale:** A one-off confirm dialog per delete button would have meant seven near-identical implementations to keep in sync (and an eighth, ninth… every time a future delete button is added). Generalizing the pattern already proven correct for custody schedules into one hook + component means every future "delete this" button gets the safety behavior for free by construction, and the redirect-signal handling only needs to be correct once.

**Reversibility:** Cheap. Purely additive (`use-confirm-delete.ts`, `confirm-delete-button.tsx`) plus call-site swaps and a `void` → `{error}` return-type change on seven actions — no schema or RLS changes. Reverting restores the pre-D-036 instant-delete behavior with no other side effects.

## D-037 | 2026-08-26 | Field-level error placement extended to every remaining form (interests, cadence, activities, settings)

**Context:** KNOWN-ISSUES.md 1.3 was only half-closed by D-032: gift budgets and the calendar event start/end pair got field-level error placement + clear-on-edit, but interests, contact cadence, activities, and settings still showed one generic message near the submit control regardless of which field actually failed validation.

**Fix, matched to how many plausibly-invalid fields each form has:**
- **Interests and cadence** (`app/(app)/people/[id]/person-forms.tsx`): each form has exactly one meaningfully-validated field (the interest text; the check-in interval number) — no disambiguation needed, just moved the existing message under that field with an `errorDismissed` flag cleared on `onChange`, matching the gift-budget form's own established pattern exactly.
- **Settings** (`app/(app)/settings/settings-form.tsx`): the one cross-field error this form produces ("Max must be at least the minimum.") concerns `budgetMax` specifically — same min/max shape as the gift-budget form it mirrors. A simple text match (`/budget|max must be/i`) separates it from the rare, generic errors (bad household name) that still render near Save.
- **Activities** (`app/(app)/activities/new/new-activity-form.tsx`): this form has *four* plausibly-invalid fields (activity type, enjoyment rank, duration, prep lead time), so text-matching on the raw zod message wasn't reliable enough — extended `app/api/activities/route.ts` to return the zod issue's own `path` (mapped to the form's input names) as a `field` property alongside `error`, and extended the shared `useFormPost` hook (`lib/hooks/use-form-post.ts`) with an `errorField`/`clearErrorField` pair so any future form using that hook gets the same precise field-tagging for free rather than needing its own regex heuristic.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (244/244 tests, zero lint/type errors).

**Rationale:** Reusing the exact `errorDismissed` pattern already proven in D-032 for the two single-field forms kept those changes minimal and consistent. Activities needed a genuinely different mechanism (server-supplied field path vs. client-side text matching) because it's the first form in this codebase with more than two fields that can independently fail — solving it at the shared `useFormPost` hook means the next multi-field form doesn't need to reinvent either the regex-matching approach or a bespoke field-tagging scheme.

**Reversibility:** Cheap. Purely additive to `useFormPost` (new optional return values, unused by existing callers that don't destructure them) and to the activities route's error response shape (`field` is optional and ignored by anything not reading it). Reverting any of the four form changes restores the pre-D-037 generic-message placement with no other side effects.
