## D-038 | 2026-08-26 | Three bugs found by live-verifying D-037; people-list double-name + hidden-sort fixed too

**Context:** Live browser verification of D-037's field-level error placement surfaced three real bugs that had nothing to do with error placement itself — the validation underneath some of those fields had its own gaps. Rather than close out 1.3 with known-bad validation still live, fixed all three in the same pass. Also picked up two quick Phase-3 items from the backlog (people-list double-name display and sort-by-hidden-field) while the browser verification was running.

**Bugs found and fixed:**
1. **Whitespace-only interest silently saved as a permanently blank, unremovable chip.** `personInterestInsertSchema` ran `.min(1)` *before* its `.transform(trim + lowercase)`, so a string of only spaces (length ≥ 1) passed the length check and was only reduced to `""` afterward, at insert time. Reordered to `.trim().toLowerCase().min(1, ...)` so the length check runs against the normalized value.
2. **Activities: no real minimum on typical duration.** The form's own input declares `min={15}`, but `userActivityInsertSchema` only required `.positive()` server-side — any client bypassing the native HTML attribute (different browser, direct API call, JS) could persist e.g. a 5-minute "typical duration" with no error anywhere. Tightened the schema to `.min(15, "Typical duration must be at least 15 minutes.")`, matching what the UI already told the user was the floor.
3. **Stale duplicate error banner after fixing a field-level error (activities form).** `useFormPost`'s `clearErrorField` only nulled `errorField`, leaving `error` itself set — so the generic fallback banner (`error && !errorField`) flipped back on the instant the field-level message cleared, showing the same resolved error a second time in the wrong place. Fixed by clearing `error` and `errorField` together, atomically, whenever the field matches.
4. **People list showed the full name twice** when a person had no nickname (`nickname || full_name` as the title, then `full_name` again as the subtitle even when identical). Now the subtitle only renders when the nickname differs from the full name.
5. **People list sorted by a hidden field.** The repository query orders by the DB column `full_name`, but the visible primary label is `nickname || full_name` — a household with nicknames set would see an on-screen order that didn't match what was displayed. Re-sorted client-side by the same name shown.
6. **Raw enum chips on the calendar.** `event_type`/`block_type` chips (e.g. `kid_activity`) rendered via a bare `.replace(/_/g, " ")`, producing lowercase, unstyled text ("kid activity") instead of a real label. Added `EVENT_TYPE_LABELS`/`BLOCK_TYPE_LABELS` maps (falling back to a capitalized humanization for anything unmapped) so every chip reads as a proper label ("Kid activity").

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (244/244 tests). Not yet independently re-verified live in the browser for these six fixes specifically — the original D-037 four scenarios themselves were confirmed working correctly (settings, interests, cadence, and the *enjoyment* half of activities all placed and cleared errors correctly on the first pass).

**Rationale:** All six fixes are narrow and mechanical (reorder two schema calls, tighten one bound to match a UI value that already existed, clear two pieces of state together instead of one, two label/sort tweaks on the people list, one label map for calendar chips) — none required a design decision, so fixing them immediately kept the backlog moving rather than filing them for a separate pass.

**Reversibility:** Cheap. Each fix is a small, independent, self-contained change (one schema field, one hook function, one page's render/sort logic) with no migration or data implications — the whitespace-interest fix only changes what new submissions accept, it doesn't touch any already-stored row.

## D-039 | 2026-08-26 | Calendar "Add" link now prefills and redirects back to the right day

**Context:** KNOWN-ISSUES.md's Phase 3 backlog flagged "no post-create redirect" for the calendar. The day view's own "Add" link already builds `/calendar/new?date=YYYY-MM-DD` for whichever day is selected, but the new-event page never read that query param — the date input always started blank — and after saving, the form unconditionally redirected to plain `/calendar`, which resolves to today's date regardless of which day (or month) you'd actually navigated to and added the event for.

**Fix:** `app/(app)/calendar/new/page.tsx` now reads `searchParams.date`, validates it's a `YYYY-MM-DD` string, and passes it to `NewEventForm` as `defaultDate` (used as the date input's `defaultValue`). On save, the form reads the actual submitted `date` field from its own `FormData` (no server round-trip needed — the value is already known client-side) and redirects to `/calendar?month=<YYYY-MM>&day=<YYYY-MM-DD>` instead of a bare `/calendar`.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (244/244 tests). Not yet independently re-verified live in the browser this pass.

**Rationale:** Scoped to the event-creation flow specifically, since that's what the calendar day view's own "Add" link targets — the one-off custody-block form (`/calendar/custody/one-off`) has a separate hub page (`/calendar/custody`) it correctly returns to and isn't reachable from a day-cell click, so it wasn't in scope here.

**Reversibility:** Cheap. Purely additive (`defaultDate` is an optional prop; the redirect target falls back to `/calendar` if no date is present) with no schema or data changes.

## D-040 | 2026-08-26 | Settings timezone is now a real IANA select; calendar auto-scrolls to the selected day

**Context:** Two independent Phase 3 backlog items, both quick and self-contained enough to do together.

**Timezone select:** `app/(app)/settings/settings-form.tsx`'s timezone field was free text — a typo like "Amercia/Los_Angeles" or any made-up string saved silently and would quietly break every time-based feature (brief timing, custody-block times) with zero indication why. Replaced with a `<select>` populated from `Intl.supportedValuesOf("timeZone")` (new `lib/timezones.ts`, with a small hardcoded fallback for the rare runtime missing that API), and tightened `userInsertSchema.timezone` server-side with a `.refine(isValidTimezone)` so a direct API call can't bypass the UI's constraint either. If a household's already-saved value somehow isn't in the list, it's prepended to the options so the select still shows the true saved value instead of silently defaulting to the first option.

**Calendar auto-scroll:** Clicking a day in the month grid always left the viewport at the top of the page (next/link's default post-navigation scroll-to-top), so the newly selected day's event list — rendered below the whole month grid card — was off-screen until the user scrolled down manually, every single time. Added `id="selected-day"` to that list's wrapper and a `#selected-day` hash fragment to every day-cell's `href` (and to the D-039 post-create redirect target) — `next/link`'s built-in hash-scroll behavior takes it from there, no client JS needed.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (244/244 tests). Live-verified in production: the timezone select shows all 418 IANA zones, saves and persists correctly across a page reload, then reverted cleanly back to the original value. Calendar auto-scroll confirmed on a 375×667 mobile viewport — clicking an unselected day cell moves `scrollY` from 0 to 183 and brings the selected day's heading into view; the D-039 post-create redirect also lands already scrolled to the newly created event.

**Rationale:** Both are narrow, config-shaped fixes (a data source swap for one field; a URL fragment for scroll targeting) rather than new features, so no product decision was needed for either.

**Reversibility:** Cheap. Timezone: reverting drops back to the free-text input with no schema/data implications (already-saved valid IANA strings, which is everything real users could have saved via the old UI in practice, still validate fine). Auto-scroll: the `#selected-day` fragments are purely additive routing sugar with no effect on any browser that doesn't support hash-scroll (it just falls back to the old scroll-to-top behavior).

## D-041 | 2026-08-26 | Custom 404 page; forgot-password flow

**Custom 404:** `app/not-found.tsx` was missing entirely, so any bad or stale link fell through to Next's bare unstyled default page — no branding, no navigation, no way back into the app except the browser's back button. Added a branded 404 that checks auth state directly (via `supabase.auth.getUser()`, not `requireHouseholdContext()` — that helper *redirects* on no session/household, the opposite of a 404's job) and links "Go to calendar" or "Go to sign in" depending on whether the visitor is logged in.

**Forgot password:** No self-service path existed for a locked-out user beyond asking someone to reset it by hand in Supabase. Added the standard two-step flow:
- `/forgot-password` — email form, calls new `sendPasswordResetEmail` action (`app/actions.ts`) which wraps `supabase.auth.resetPasswordForEmail(email, { redirectTo: "<origin>/auth/callback?next=/reset-password" })`. Always shows the same "if an account exists…" message regardless of whether the email matched anything, matching Supabase's own non-enumerating behavior.
- `app/auth/callback/route.ts` — extended to read an optional `next` query param (allow-listed to `/onboarding` or `/reset-password`) instead of hardcoding the onboarding redirect, so the existing magic-link/signup-confirmation links (which never set `next`) keep working exactly as before.
- `/reset-password` — new password + confirm-password form (client-side match check), calls new `updatePasswordAfterReset` action, which requires the caller to already hold the short-lived recovery session the callback just established (`supabase.auth.getUser()` returning a user is proof enough — no separate "current password" field, since reaching this page at all already proves control of the account's inbox), then `supabase.auth.updateUser({ password })` and redirects to `/calendar`.
- `/login` gets a "Forgot password?" link next to the password sign-in form.
- `getSiteOrigin()` helper reads `x-forwarded-host`/`x-forwarded-proto` via `next/headers` since Server Actions have no `request` object the way Route Handlers do.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (244/244 tests). Live verification pending — needs an actual reset email round-trip (send → receive → click → set new password) checked against the real inbox before calling this done.

**Rationale:** Both are standard, expected auth/error-page affordances with an obvious one correct shape — no product decision needed.

**Reversibility:** Cheap. 404 page is purely additive. Forgot-password touches `app/auth/callback/route.ts`'s redirect target logic, but the change is backward compatible (defaults to the exact prior behavior for any link without `next` set) — reverting drops the three new pages/actions and callback change with no schema or data implications.

**Context:** Phase 3 backlog: "Gifts tab has poor empty-state copy and doesn't expose the scan-horizon setting." `gift_scan_horizon_days` was already a real household column (set once at signup, read by `lib/gifts/scan.ts`), but nothing in the UI let a household change it afterward, and the empty state on `/gifts` was one vague sentence ("...within the scan horizon") that never said what the horizon actually was or what to do if suggestions weren't showing up.

Rewrote the Gifts empty state (`app/(app)/gifts/page.tsx`) to name the household's actual configured horizon in days, and to link directly to `/settings` for adjusting it, alongside a reminder to check that people have occasion dates set.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (244/244 tests). Not yet independently re-verified live in the browser this pass.

**Rationale:** Straightforward "expose an existing setting" + copy fix \u2014 no design decision needed since the field, its validation, and its consumer (`scanUpcomingOccasions`) already existed.

**Reversibility:** Cheap. Additive settings-form field with existing schema validation; the empty-state copy change has no data or schema implications.

## D-043 | 2026-08-26 | Custody exception-date UI + gap detection for existing schedules

**Context:** Phase 2 backlog item, deferred since the round-2 brief: the `custody_schedule_exceptions` table and its repository/materialization support (`lib/custody/materialize.ts` already merges exceptions into generated blocks and marks them `block_type: "holiday"`) existed end-to-end on the pure-engine and DB side, but there was no UI for a user to actually add, view, or remove a one-day override on a recurring schedule — the only place `findGaps` ever ran was the schedule-creation preview, so a gap introduced later (e.g. an edited `end_date`, or a custom cycle missing a day index) was never flagged anywhere for an existing schedule.

**Fix:**
- New page `app/(app)/calendar/custody/[id]/page.tsx` — a schedule detail/management view, linked from a new "Manage" button on each schedule card in `/calendar/custody`. Shows the schedule summary, a gap-detection banner (running `projectCustodySchedule` + `findGaps` over the same `MATERIALIZE_WINDOW_DAYS`-day window `materializeCustodySchedule` actually fills, with existing exceptions applied), the list of exceptions with delete buttons, and an add-exception form.
- New API routes: `POST /api/calendar/custody/schedules/[id]/exceptions` (validates via the pre-existing `custodyScheduleExceptionInsertSchema`, inserts, then calls `materializeCustodySchedule` so the calendar picks up the change immediately) and `DELETE /api/calendar/custody/schedules/[id]/exceptions/[exceptionId]` (removes the override and re-materializes so the cycle's default assignment takes back over).
- New client components `exception-form.tsx` and `delete-exception-button.tsx`, following the same direct-`fetch`-with-JSON pattern as the existing schedule create/delete flows (not `useFormPost`, which posts `FormData` — the custody schedule API routes have always taken JSON bodies).

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (244/244 tests, two new API routes appear in the build's route list). **Live-verified PASS**: since the real household had no child or co-parent to test against, created temporary "ZZ Test Child"/"ZZ Test CoParent" people and a temporary weekly schedule, then confirmed on `/calendar/custody/[id]`: summary header, "No exceptions yet" empty state, add-exception form (date, responsible-that-day dropdown, optional reason) all render; adding an exception (date 10 days out, overriding to the non-default parent, with a reason) saved and displayed correctly; the two-click confirm-delete pattern removed it cleanly, reverting to the empty state. No gap-detection banner appeared for this schedule (expected — a plain ongoing weekly cycle has no gaps to flag). All temporary test data (schedule, both people, and one orphaned custody block that survived schedule/child deletion — see "Found during this pass" in KNOWN-ISSUES.md) was deleted afterward; the real household is confirmed back to just "Richard Smith" with nothing else.

**Rationale:** The hard part (recurrence engine + exception merge + materialization) was already built and tested in Phase 2; this was purely the missing UI layer. Folding gap-detection into the same page (rather than a separate backlog item) was a low-cost add-on since `findGaps` and the exact materialize window were already one import away.

**Reversibility:** Cheap. Purely additive UI and API routes; no schema or migration changes (the `custody_schedule_exceptions` table and its Zod schema already existed unused).

## D-044 | 2026-08-26 | SECURITY: /reset-password no longer accepts any logged-in session

**Context:** Found during live verification of D-041's forgot-password flow. `updatePasswordAfterReset` only checked that `supabase.auth.getUser()` returned *some* user — true for any normal, already-logged-in session, not just one freshly established from clicking the emailed recovery link. Visiting `/reset-password` directly while already logged in silently changed the real account password with no error message at all, instead of the intended "That reset link has expired" behavior. This was reproduced and immediately reverted during verification (see D-042's live-verification run) — the account's password was momentarily changed and restored, no other data was touched.

**Fix:** Added `RESET_FLOW_COOKIE` (`lib/constants.ts`) — a short-lived (15 min), httpOnly, single-use marker cookie. `app/auth/callback/route.ts` sets it only when the callback's `next` param is `/reset-password` (i.e. only on the actual password-reset redirect path, right after exchanging the emailed link's code for a session). `updatePasswordAfterReset` (`app/actions.ts`) now requires this cookie to be present *in addition to* a valid session, and deletes it immediately so it can't be replayed for a second change later in the same login. A normal login session, lacking this cookie, now correctly hits the "reset link has expired" error instead of silently succeeding.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (244/244 tests). Live-verification pending — will re-run the browser_task that originally caught this, checking specifically: (a) direct `/reset-password` visit while logged in now shows the expired-link error and does NOT change the password, (b) the genuine email-link round trip still works once gcal reauth is available.

**Rationale:** This is a real security gap (session confusion between "authenticated" and "authorized via password-reset flow specifically"), so it jumped the backlog queue ahead of other Phase 2/3 items despite not being in the original brief.

**Reversibility:** Cheap, additive-only (a new cookie and a length check); no schema or migration changes. Rolling back means one logged-in session could again silently reset its own password via direct URL visit — not recommended.

---

## D-045 | 2026-08-26 | PWA manifest, app icons, and install support

**Context:** Phase 3 backlog: no web app manifest, no app icons beyond the existing `favicon.ico`, and no service worker — the app couldn't be "installed" as a PWA on desktop or mobile home screens.

**Fix:**
- Generated a brand icon set (black circle / white triangle, matching the existing `favicon.ico` mark) at 192×192 and 512×512 (`any` purpose) plus a 512×512 `maskable` variant with a proper safe-zone margin, saved under `public/`. Added `app/icon.png` (512×512) and `app/apple-icon.png` (180×180) using Next.js's App Router icon file convention, so the browser tab favicon and iOS home-screen icon are both covered automatically with no manual `<link>` tags needed.
- `app/manifest.ts` — Next.js's native manifest route, auto-served at `/manifest.webmanifest` with the correct content-type. `start_url: "/calendar"`, `display: "standalone"`, theme/background colors matching the app's existing neutral palette.
- `app/layout.tsx` — added `metadata.manifest`, `metadata.appleWebApp` (iOS "Add to Home Screen" standalone look), `metadata.formatDetection.telephone: false`, and a `viewport` export with `themeColor` (Next 16 requires viewport config as a separate export from `metadata`).
- `public/sw.js` + `app/service-worker-registration.tsx` — a deliberately minimal service worker, registered client-side. It only cache-first-serves the three static icon files; every page and API request passes straight through to the network untouched. This was a deliberate, explicit choice: this app is entirely session-dependent (`force-dynamic` on every route, per the comment already in `app/layout.tsx`), and D-031 already documented one real production bug from a *cached* auth-dependent response being replayed to the wrong session at Vercel's edge — caching HTML/API responses in a service worker would risk the same class of bug one layer further down, in the browser itself. The service worker exists purely to satisfy PWA installability criteria (manifest + active service worker), not to add offline support.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (244/244 tests); build output confirms `/manifest.webmanifest`, `/icon.png`, and `/apple-icon.png` are all generated as static routes. **Live-verified PASS** in production: `GET /manifest.webmanifest` returns `200` with `content-type: application/manifest+json` and the expected name/icons/theme_color JSON; `/icon-192.png`, `/icon-512.png`, `/icon-512-maskable.png`, `/sw.js`, `/icon.png`, and `/apple-icon.png` all return `200`. Full "Add to Home Screen" browser-chrome install-prompt UX not exercised (that's a Chrome/Android-specific affordance outside what can be checked headlessly), but every criterion the browser uses to decide installability (valid manifest, valid icons, registered service worker, HTTPS) is confirmed present.

**Rationale:** Manifest + icons + a real (if intentionally inert) service worker is the standard, low-risk way to make a Next.js app installable without touching any of its actual data-fetching behavior.

**Reversibility:** Cheap. All additive (new files only); no existing route or component behavior changed. The service worker's cache-versioning (`lifeos-static-v1`) makes future icon updates safe — bumping that string invalidates the old cache.

## D-046 | 2026-08-26 | Dark mode support

**Context:** Phase 3 backlog: no dark mode. `app/globals.css` already had a complete `.dark` class with oklch tokens (shadcn scaffolding default) and every component was already built against `bg-background`/`text-foreground`/`bg-card`/`text-muted-foreground`-style CSS variable tokens rather than hardcoded colors — but nothing ever added the `.dark` class to `<html>`, so dark mode was structurally ready but functionally inert.

**Fix:**
- Installed `next-themes`. `components/theme-provider.tsx` wraps its `ThemeProvider`, mounted in `app/layout.tsx` with `attribute="class" defaultTheme="system" enableSystem` — this is what actually toggles `.dark` on `<html>`, persists the choice to `localStorage`, and injects a pre-hydration inline script so a dark-preferring device doesn't flash light mode before React mounts. `<html suppressHydrationWarning>` added since that injected script intentionally sets the class before hydration.
- `components/theme-toggle.tsx` — a three-way Light/Dark/System segmented control using `useTheme()`. Renders a neutral (non-selected) state until mounted, since the real resolved theme is only knowable client-side.
- Added an "Appearance" card to `/settings` hosting the toggle. This is a device-local UI preference (not a household setting), so it's deliberately separate from `SettingsForm`'s server-action-backed household fields.
- Audited every component for hardcoded (non-token) colors before assuming dark mode would "just work": found only `text-white` on the fixed-red `destructive` button/badge variant (correct in both themes — destructive stays red-ish either way) and a `bg-black/40` modal backdrop (also correct in both themes). The two amber gap-detection/warning banners already had explicit `dark:text-amber-400` variants from when they were built. No changes needed to any of these.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (244/244 tests). **Live-verified PASS**: toggling to Dark instantly switches the whole page (html gains the `dark` class) with no unstyled flash; an automated WCAG contrast scan across Brief, Calendar, People, Gifts, and Settings found zero text/background pairs below 3.5:1 and zero stray bright/white leftover boxes; a hard refresh while in Dark stayed dark with no light-mode flash and the toggle still showed "Dark" selected after reload (confirms localStorage persistence); "System" correctly matched the test browser's OS preference; switching back to Light and reloading again persisted symmetrically. No visual defects found on any page checked.

**Rationale:** The CSS foundation already existed; this was purely wiring next-themes on top of it, which is the standard, well-tested approach for Next.js dark mode and avoids hand-rolling class toggling, persistence, and hydration-safe SSR handling.

**Reversibility:** Cheap. Additive only — no existing component's markup or class names changed (the tokens they already used simply now respond to a class that gets toggled). Removing the `ThemeProvider` wrapper would just leave the app permanently in light mode (the `:root` tokens), matching pre-D-046 behavior exactly.

## D-047 | 2026-08-26 | Custody schedule ICS calendar export

**Context:** Last open item from the round-2 Phase 2 custody backlog (KNOWN-ISSUES.md 2.x): no way to get a custody schedule into an external calendar app — relevant for e.g. a co-parent with no LifeOS account of their own.

**Fix:**
- `lib/custody/ics.ts` — pure, DB-free RFC 5545 (iCalendar) builder. `mergeCustodyRuns` merges consecutive same-parent days into single spans (mirrors `materialize.ts`'s private `mergeConsecutiveDays`, kept as a separate copy since this module is intentionally DB-free like the rest of `lib/custody/*`). `buildCustodyIcs` emits one all-day `VEVENT` per run, with an RFC-5545-correct *exclusive* `DTEND` (the day after the run's last covered day — every calendar app expects all-day multi-day events expressed this way), escaped text fields, 75-octet line folding, and a UID stable per `scheduleId` + run start date so re-downloading and re-importing an updated export updates existing events in the destination calendar rather than duplicating them.
- `GET /api/calendar/custody/schedules/[id]/ics` — projects the schedule 365 days forward using the same `projectCustodySchedule` engine (with saved exceptions applied) the schedule detail page already uses for gap detection, then returns `text/calendar` with a `Content-Disposition: attachment` filename derived from the child's name.
- New "Export .ics" button on the schedule detail page (`/calendar/custody/[id]`), next to the schedule summary header.
- 9 new unit tests (`lib/custody/ics.test.ts`) covering merge-run boundaries (including the same-parent-but-date-gap non-merge case), exception-day `DESCRIPTION` lines, RFC 5545 text escaping, UID stability across regenerations, and the empty-schedule edge case.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (253/253 tests, 9 new). Build output confirms the new route. **Live-verified PASS** against production (commit `c5cb448`, deployed as `https://lifeos-ijra76x7w-rwsmith964s-projects.vercel.app`): created a temporary child + co-parent + a week-on/week-off custody schedule, clicked "Export .ics" on the schedule's manage page, and got a 378-line, 11,953-byte download (`zz-export-child-custody-schedule.ics`) that the sandbox's own `file` utility identified natively as a valid iCalendar file — 53 matched `BEGIN:VEVENT`/`END:VEVENT` pairs, correct `SUMMARY` lines alternating between the two parents, `DTSTART`/`DTEND` as `VALUE=DATE` 8-digit dates, and correct RFC 5545 CRLF line endings. All temporary data (schedule, both people, and the orphaned custody-block rows the schedule delete intentionally left behind as history) was fully cleaned up afterward; real household confirmed back to zero people/schedules and Richard Smith still logged in.

**Rationale:** A one-time authenticated download (rather than an unauthenticated subscribable webcal:// feed with its own token-based auth scheme) is the lower-risk MVP that still satisfies the actual backlog wording ("custody ICS calendar export") — a subscribable feed would need new public-endpoint auth design that's out of scope for a quick backlog item.

**Reversibility:** Cheap, purely additive — new files and one new route only, no schema/migration changes, no existing route touched.

## D-048 | 2026-08-26 | Brief markdown rendering, "seeing them today" contact-nudge suppression, People-item tappability

**Context:** Round-2 backlog line item "brief Markdown rendering / tappability / 'seeing them today' suppression / look-ahead," carried forward with only a short paraphrase (the original detailed brief text for this line isn't preserved in the repo or recoverable session history at the point this was picked up). Rather than block on that, re-derived concrete, verifiable sub-issues directly from the code:

1. **Raw markdown rendering bug (confirmed in code):** `lib/planner/generate.ts`'s `renderWeekendPlanMarkdown` emits real markdown (`## `, `**bold**`, `- ` lists), and the weekend-plan card on `/calendar` (`app/(app)/calendar/page.tsx`) was dumping that string into a `whitespace-pre-line` div with zero parsing — users would see literal `**Recommendation:**` asterisks and `- ` dashes instead of styled text. Fixed with a new, intentionally narrow component, `components/ui/rendered-markdown.tsx` — handles only the exact subset our own two generators (`lib/brief/render.ts`, `lib/planner/generate.ts`) produce (`## ` headline, blank-line paragraphs, `- ` bullets, inline `**bold**`). Not a general markdown library and never fed arbitrary/AI-echoed text directly (still our own deterministic renderer's output).
2. **"Seeing them today" contact-nudge suppression (confirmed gap in code):** the daily brief's overdue-contact-cadence check (`lib/brief/generate.ts`) already fetched today's calendar events and custody blocks for other purposes but never cross-referenced them against the overdue-contact list — so a co-parent with a custody handover *this afternoon*, or a friend already on today's calendar via a shared activity, could still get "you haven't talked to X in N days" in the same brief. Added a new pure, unit-tested filter, `suppressCadencesSeenToday` (`lib/contact/cadence.ts`), and wired it in: a person is considered "seen today" if they're the child or responsible parent on a custody block starting today, or a `preferred_companions` match on an activity linked to an event starting today. Deliberately scoped to *today only* (not tomorrow) — a reminder about someone you're not seeing until tomorrow is still useful to act on today.
3. **People-item tappability (best-effort, given the brief stores names not IDs):** the brief's `content_json` only ever stores each person by their real `full_name` string (`lib/ai/context.ts`'s `labelFor`) — it has no foreign key back to a person id, since the brief is deterministically rendered text, not structured data with references. `app/(app)/page.tsx` now resolves each `People` section item to a real `/people/[id]` link by exact-name match against the household's own people, but only when that name is unique in the household (a same-first-name duplicate falls back to plain text rather than risk a wrong link).
4. **"Look-ahead"** — left as an open item. Nothing in the current code or recoverable history pins down what specific look-ahead behavior was being asked for (e.g., extending the brief's 2-day event window, a weekly preview, or something else), and guessing further risked building the wrong thing. Flagged in KNOWN-ISSUES for clarification rather than implemented speculatively.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (256/256 tests, +3 new for `suppressCadencesSeenToday`). Not yet independently live-verified in a real browser session — next step.

**Rationale:** Splitting an underspecified backlog line into what's concretely provable from the code (and building only those pieces) is safer than guessing at an entire vague feature; the one genuinely ambiguous sub-item ("look-ahead") is called out explicitly rather than silently invented.

**Reversibility:** Cheap. New component + new pure function + one new import/usage each in two existing pages, plus a filter added to one existing computation in `generate.ts`. No schema/migration changes.

## D-049 | 2026-08-26 | Responsive shell audit + AI-assistant FAB positioning fix

**Context:** Backlog item "responsive shell audit." Ran a structured visual audit (login as the real account, DevTools device-metrics overrides + `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth` checks + `elementFromPoint` hit-testing, not just screenshots) across Brief, Calendar, People, and Settings at 390px (mobile), 820px (tablet), and 1440px (desktop).

**Findings:**
- **No functional bugs at any width.** Zero horizontal scrollbars, zero text truncation/overlap, zero unclickable nav/header controls across all 12 page×width combinations tested. The app's shell (`app/(app)/layout.tsx`) is intentionally `max-w-md mx-auto` (~448px), so at 820px/1440px it renders as a narrow centered column with large unused margins on both sides — a deliberate, working consequence of the mobile-first design, not a bug. Whether LifeOS should eventually get a real multi-column desktop layout is a product/design decision, not something to unilaterally build without direction — flagged in KNOWN-ISSUES rather than redesigned.
- **One genuine CSS bug found and fixed:** the floating AI-assistant "Quick capture" button (`components/capture/capture-button.tsx`) used bare `fixed bottom-20 right-4`, which anchors to the real viewport edge rather than the app's `max-w-md` column edge every other fixed element (the bottom nav) respects. At 820px it drifted ~150px outside the app's right edge; at 1440px, ~430px outside — visually a stray element disconnected from the rest of the "phone frame" UI, though it never overlapped or blocked any other control at any width. Fixed by wrapping it in a `pointer-events-none fixed inset-x-0 mx-auto max-w-md justify-end` strip (mirroring the bottom nav's own centering pattern) with the button itself `pointer-events-auto`.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (256/256 tests, no new tests needed — this is a pure CSS/layout fix with no new logic). Live re-verification of the FAB fix at 820px/1440px pending for next round.

**Rationale:** An "audit" backlog item is a request to characterize the current state and fix what's actually broken, not a license to redesign the whole shell without user sign-off on a real multi-column desktop layout. Fixing the one concrete regression (FAB drift) while leaving the larger design question open matches that scope.

**Reversibility:** Trivial — one component's positioning wrapper, no logic or schema changes.

## D-050 | 2026-08-26 | Home address field — unblocks weekend planning + brief weather

**Context:** Live-verification of D-048 surfaced that weekend-plan generation always fails with "No activities with a home location configured yet — add one under Activities," even after creating an activity with lat/lng. Traced the actual logic in `generateWeekendPlan` (`lib/planner/generate.ts`): the "home" location it needs comes from the **household owner's `home_lat`/`home_lng`** (columns that have existed on `users` since the initial schema and are already validated in `lib/db/schemas.ts`), not from any activity row. The error message was actively misdirecting people to the wrong screen. Worse: there was **no UI anywhere in the app** — not Settings, not Activities, not onboarding — that could ever set `home_lat`/`home_lng`/`home_address`. This also silently degraded `lib/brief/generate.ts`'s weather section, which reads the exact same `owner.home_lat/home_lng` pair via `getNwsForecast`. This closes out the backlog's "weather provider + location field" item — the weather provider itself (NWS, `lib/external/nws.ts`) was already fully implemented and needs no API key; the location field was the only missing piece.

**What shipped:**
- New `lib/external/geocode.ts`: a small forward-geocoding adapter using OpenStreetMap's Nominatim (`nominatim.openstreetmap.org/search`) — free, no API key, so this doesn't need a `custom-credentials` round trip. Only called on an explicit "Save settings" click (not a hot/repeated path), respecting Nominatim's usage policy via a descriptive `User-Agent` header. Returns a discriminated `{status: "ok" | "not_found" | "error"}` result rather than throwing, so a typo'd address becomes a clear inline validation message instead of a generic save failure.
- Settings page/form (`app/(app)/settings/`) gained a "Home address" text field. On save, if the text changed, it's geocoded and `home_address`/`home_lat`/`home_lng` are written to the user's row; clearing the field clears all three. Address-lookup failures surface under the field itself (mirrors the existing budget-min/max inline-error pattern), not as a generic top-of-form error.
- Fixed the misleading `generateWeekendPlanAction` error message in `app/(app)/calendar/actions.ts` to point at Settings instead of Activities.
- `revalidatePath` added for `/calendar` and `/` (Brief) alongside the existing `/settings`, since both read `home_lat`/`home_lng` and should reflect a freshly-saved address without a manual reload.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (262/262 tests, +6 new for the geocoding adapter covering blank input, a successful match, no-match, HTTP failure, network failure, and the required User-Agent header). Live re-verification (save a real address, confirm weekend-plan generation and brief weather both start working) still pending for next round.

**Reversibility:** Additive — new nullable columns are already in the schema and were simply unreachable before; no migration needed. The geocode adapter is a new, isolated file.

## D-051 | 2026-08-26 | Fix stale UI after delete (router.refresh() in useConfirmDelete)

**Context:** D-048's live-verification flagged an apparent "calendar event delete doesn't persist" bug. Ran a dedicated diagnostic pass with full network/response instrumentation against production to pin down the exact mechanism before touching any code. Findings, in order:
1. The two-click arm/confirm UI works exactly as designed (first click arms, no network call; second click within 4s fires the real action).
2. The second click's request **does** reach the server: `POST /calendar` with the `Next-Action` header for `deleteCalendarEventAction`, response `200 OK` with `x-action-revalidated: 1`.
3. The row is **genuinely deleted in the database** — captured the raw response body of that exact 200, and it's already the fresh, event-free RSC payload. The server did its job correctly before responding.
4. The already-open tab's DOM still showed the deleted event for 5+ seconds with zero console errors, only correcting itself on the next real navigation or reload. A brand-new session confirmed the row was already gone server-side the whole time.

**Root cause:** not a database bug, not a caching/CDN bug, not a business-logic bug — the Server Action + `revalidatePath` round trip is completing correctly, but the already-mounted client component tree isn't reliably reapplying that revalidation signal to the live DOM in this app's setup. This is a known class of Next.js App Router issue: a bare `await someServerAction()` call from a client component is not guaranteed to force the invoking route segment to re-render just because the action revalidated a path — the framework applies the update to the *router cache*, but the mounted tree doesn't always pick it up without an explicit `router.refresh()`.

**Fix:** added a single `router.refresh()` call (`next/navigation`) inside `useConfirmDelete`'s success path, right after every successful two-click delete. This hook already backs every delete flow across activities, gifts, interests, budgets, calendar events, custody schedules/blocks, and archiving people — one change covers all of them, rather than patching `/calendar` specifically. It's very likely the exact same "ghost row until reload" symptom was present on those other pages too and simply wasn't caught, since the only prior verification method was "reload afterward and check the final state" rather than watching the in-place transition.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (262/262 tests — no new tests added; this is a client-rendering-freshness fix with no new branching logic to unit test, and is best verified live/visually). Live re-verification (delete something and confirm the row disappears immediately, without a reload) pending for next round — should specifically re-test on `/calendar` where the bug was originally caught.

**Reversibility:** Trivial, one hook, one added call.

---

## D-052 | 2026-08-26 | "Refresh brief" button + cleanup of a stale production brief row

**Context:** D-048's re-verification surfaced Richard's real, live Brief page showing a "Heads up" reminder for "Carol Smith" — a person who does not exist in People. Traced this to the `briefs` table directly: today's brief row (`generated_at` 14:38 UTC) had that content baked into its stored `content_json` from whenever it was first generated, and since `briefs` has no update/delete policy for regular users (service-role-write-only by design, alongside `weekend_plans`/`ai_usage_log`/etc. — see earlier pitfalls), there was no way for Richard to ever force a fresh one. This was flagged in KNOWN-ISSUES.md as a real quality-of-life gap, not dismissed as a one-off fluke.

**What shipped:**
- `regenerateBriefAction` (`app/(app)/actions.ts`): deletes today's existing brief row for the current person (via the service-role client, same justification as `generateWeekendPlanAction`) and calls `generateDailyBrief` fresh.
- A small "Refresh brief" button (`app/(app)/regenerate-brief-button.tsx`) next to the Brief page's headline, using the `startTransition` + `router.refresh()` pattern (per D-051's finding that a bare await + `revalidatePath` isn't reliably picked up by the already-mounted tree in this app).
- Deleted the one stale, live "Carol Smith" brief row directly (production data cleanup, not a code change) so Richard's actual Brief page stops showing it; his next real brief generation (today, via this new button, or tomorrow's normal cron) will reflect his real current data (zero people, zero pending gifts).

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (262/262 tests — no new tests; this reuses already-tested `generateDailyBrief`/`briefsRepo` and adds no new branching logic worth isolating). Live re-verification (click Refresh brief, confirm the stale Carol Smith reminder is gone and the button doesn't error) pending for next round.

**Reversibility:** Additive UI + one action; the production data deletion only removed a single already-stale cached row that gets regenerated every day regardless.

## D-053 | 2026-08-26 | SECURITY: fixed cross-household gift-suggestion data leak in daily brief

**Severity:** High — real user-facing cross-tenant data leak, caught live in production on Richard's own real account.

**Context:** Live re-verification of D-052's "Refresh brief" button kept showing a "Carol Smith gift — order by Monday, Aug 31" reminder on Richard's real Brief page, even immediately after confirming (a) `/people` showed zero people in his household, (b) `/gifts` showed zero open suggestions, and (c) the underlying `briefs` row had just been freshly regenerated (not stale/cached — confirmed via `generated_at` timestamp and by inspecting the row's own `content_json` directly in the database). Since the content kept reappearing with consistent facts (a person named "Carol Smith," birthday Sep 9, order-by Aug 31) despite zero real matching data anywhere in Richard's own household, the AI had to be getting fed this from somewhere — the working theory shifted from "hallucination" to "the AI is being given real data, just from the wrong household."

**Root cause:** `lib/brief/generate.ts`'s gift-reminder query (`generateDailyBrief`'s "Gift order-by reminders within 14 days" section) called `giftSuggestionsRepo.list(client, (q) => q.in("status", [...]).lte(...).gte(...))` — filtering only by status and date range, with **no `household_id` filter of any kind**. `generateDailyBrief` always runs on the **service-role Supabase client** (both its callers — the Brief page's on-demand fallback and D-052's new regenerate action — explicitly use `createSupabaseServiceRoleClient()` because `briefs` has no insert policy for regular users). The service-role client bypasses RLS entirely, so this unscoped query silently returned every household's due gift suggestions, not just the current one. Confirmed directly in the database: "Carol Smith" is a real person in the project's seeded demo household (`richard@example.com` / `20000000-...-001`, used for local dev/testing throughout this project) with three real `gift_suggestions` rows, one of which has `order_by_date = 2026-08-31` — inside the 14-day window checked against Richard's *today*. Every time Richard's brief regenerated, this demo household's data was being pulled into his real brief, worded slightly differently each time because the AI was genuinely (correctly) summarizing whatever the query handed it.

**Why this one query and not others:** audited every other data-fetching call in `generateDailyBrief` (events, custody blocks, cadences, people, activities, weather, weekend plan) — all correctly pass and filter by `householdId`. Also confirmed a correctly-scoped equivalent helper, `listSuggestionsDueForOrder(client, householdId, withinDays)` (inner-joins `people.household_id`), already existed in `lib/db/repositories/gifts.ts` and is used correctly elsewhere (e.g. `listActiveSuggestionsForHousehold`) — this one call site in the brief generator just wasn't using it.

**Fix:** switched the brief generator to call `listSuggestionsDueForOrder(client, householdId, 14)` and re-applied the original `>= today` lower bound as a local `.filter()` afterward (the helper only takes an upper-bound `withinDays` parameter). No schema or migration change needed — this was a query-construction bug, not an RLS policy gap.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build` all pass (262/262 — no new automated test added; this is a straightforward query-scoping fix with an existing, already-tested-elsewhere helper, and the realistic regression risk (two real households both having a gift suggestion due in the same 14-day window) isn't easily reproducible in a unit test without a lot of fixture setup for low marginal value). **Live-verified PASS** post-deploy: logged into production, confirmed no "Carol Smith" text anywhere on the Brief page, clicked "Refresh brief" twice in a row (regenerating the brief from scratch each time) and confirmed the leak did not reappear either time, and confirmed `/gifts` now correctly shows "No open gift suggestions right now" instead of the leaked reminder. All other pages (`/people`, `/activities`, `/calendar`, `/settings`) sanity-checked as loading normally with no regressions.

**Second instance found during the same audit — more severe:** while sweeping every `createSupabaseServiceRoleClient()` call site and every unscoped repository `.list()` call in the codebase for the same pattern, found a second, worse copy of the identical bug in `lib/gifts/scan.ts`'s `scanHouseholdForGiftOccasions` (the daily occasion-scan cron job, per Section 7.1/7.2): its "due suggestions" query (`giftSuggestionsRepo.list(client, (q) => q.in("status", [...]))`) also had no `household_id` filter, and this function runs once per household in a loop from the cron job/script, always on the service-role client. Because the notification-dispatch step right after used `dueSuggestions` to decide who to notify and for what, this could have sent a household's shopper an "Order by..." notification about a completely different household's gift suggestion — a worse outcome than the brief case (which only *displayed* leaked data) since this one could reach email/in-app notifications. Fixed by switching to the existing correctly-scoped `listActiveSuggestionsForHousehold(client, householdId)` helper. Also added a defensive `household_id` filter to the adjacent `alreadyNotified` dedup check in the same function, even though its `link_path` already embeds a suggestion-specific UUID and wasn't a practical leak vector on its own.

**Full audit performed:** manually reviewed every repository `.list()` call site across `lib/` and `app/` (grepped for the `Repo.list(client, (q) =>` pattern) — every other call site was confirmed to filter by `household_id` directly, or by a `person_id`/`schedule_id`/etc. that was itself already derived from a household-scoped call upstream. These two (brief gift reminders, gift-scan due suggestions) were the only two missing the filter.

**Blast radius / what to double check going forward:** both fixed instances were in gift-related, service-role-executed queries — nothing else in the codebase had the same gap as of this audit. See KNOWN-ISSUES.md for a standing process note to re-check this pattern whenever a new service-role query is added.

## D-054 | 2026-08-26 | VERIFIED: Activities USGS/ODFW/NOAA/lat-lng fields work end-to-end with real live data

**Context:** The Activities form has five fields (latitude, longitude, USGS gauge ID, ODFW recreation report URL, NOAA tide station ID) that feed `lib/planner/generate.ts`'s weekend-plan generation, wiring real river-flow (`lib/external/usgs.ts`), fishing-report (ODFW), and tide-prediction (NOAA) data into the AI-written weekend plan for locations that have them set. Before D-050, this could never be tested end-to-end because weekend-plan generation failed outright for any household without a home address (the geocoding gap fixed in D-050). This was previously verified by code inspection only, never with a real activity and real external data.

**What was tested:** Created a real activity, "Willamette River Fishing," on Richard's production account at location "Willamette River at Sellwood" (lat 45.4720, lng -122.6640) with USGS gauge `14211720` (a real, active USGS streamflow gauge on the Willamette River at Portland/Sellwood) and the real ODFW Willamette Zone fishing-report URL (`https://myodfw.com/recreation-report/fishing-report/willamette-zone`); NOAA station left blank (not a coastal spot, correctly not applicable).

**Result — PASS, confirmed against a live independent source:**
- All fields persisted correctly in the database (`external_ids: {"usgs_gauge": "14211720", "odfw_zone_url": "..."}`, NOAA key correctly absent rather than an empty string).
- Clicking "Generate weekend plan" on `/calendar` completed in ~8 seconds with zero errors.
- The generated plan selected "Fishing at Willamette River at Sellwood" as its #1 recommendation (score 83/100) and stated the river was "running at 18,500 cfs with a gauge height of 3.24 ft" with an ODFW report confirming the fishery open.
- Independently queried the live USGS Water Services API for gauge 14211720 at the same moment: the real-time reading was exactly 3.24 ft gauge height and ~18,500 cfs — an exact match to the app's plan. This is conclusive: the planner is pulling genuine live USGS data through `lib/external/usgs.ts`, not AI-fabricated numbers. The ODFW URL was also confirmed live and reachable (HTTP 200).

**Conclusion:** the USGS/ODFW/lat-lng portion of the Activities form → weekend-plan pipeline is fully working in production with real external data. NOAA tide-station integration was not exercised in this pass (no coastal test location created) — this is the one remaining untested sub-path; low priority since it uses the same code pattern as the now-confirmed USGS/ODFW paths (`lib/external/noaa-tides.ts`, wired identically in `lib/planner/generate.ts`).

**Verification:** live production test only (external API behavior, not something to unit-test meaningfully beyond the existing mocked tests already in the suite). No code changes required — this was a verification-only pass, closing out the open item from D-050's discovery.

## D-055 | 2026-08-27 | Household membership / multi-user (Phase 4.1): invite flow, 3 bugs found and fixed, household switching added

**Context:** Phase 4.1 added the ability for a household owner to invite other people (adult/viewer roles) via email, backed by a `household_invites` table and an `accept_household_invite()` SECURITY DEFINER RPC. Full live production testing of the invite lifecycle (send → accept → use) surfaced three separate bugs, each fixed, verified, and deployed in this pass.

**Bug 1 — invite-send 500 crash.** `app/(app)/settings/household-invite-actions.ts` (a `"use server"` file) exported a plain object constant (`initialInviteState`) alongside its Server Actions. Next.js/Turbopack requires every runtime export of a `"use server"` file to be an async function — a non-function export crashes the whole module the instant it's evaluated, as an uncaught 500, invisible to typecheck/lint/build and only surfacing when the route is actually hit. **Fix:** moved the constant into the client component file that uses it (`household-members.tsx`), keeping only the type import from the actions file. Deployed as `dpl_9VWcz2ng7YZfpA7tQhXG36rdfAFZ`.

**Bug 2 — fake/garbage invite token crash + no copy-link fallback.** Two related issues: (a) `get_household_invite_preview`'s `p_token` param is typed `uuid` in Postgres, so a syntactically-invalid token in a `/invite/<token>` URL threw an uncaught `22P02` (invalid input syntax for uuid) instead of showing "invite not found"; (b) when `RESEND_API_KEY` isn't configured, invite emails silently stub to a server console log the owner can never see, with no fallback UI, making the whole accept flow untestable and practically unusable in that config. **Fix:** `getHouseholdInvitePreview()` now catches `22P02` and returns `null` (graceful "not found" card); `sendHouseholdInviteAction` now returns the accept URL when email delivery didn't happen, and the invite form shows a read-only link + "Copy link" button in that case. Deployed as `dpl_7i1gqQq9p7nHViV2RiJhJBZKC4XD`.

**Bug 3 (the significant one) — accepted invite was invisible to the invitee.** `accept_household_invite()` correctly inserted the `household_members` row but never created a matching `people` "self" record in the newly-joined household — and `requireHouseholdContext()` resolves "your household" by finding exactly that: a `people` row with `user_id = you AND relationship_type = 'self'` inside whichever household happened to be `households[0]` in an *unordered* list. An invitee with 2+ households had no way to choose between them, and even the "right" household lacked the self-person row needed to resolve into it at all. Confirmed against real production state: `rwsmith964+invitee3@gmail.com` had a genuine `household_members` row (role `adult`) in Smith Household and an `accepted` invite, but zero `people` presence there — their own client could never show it, while the owner's Settings page correctly showed them as a member the whole time.

**Fix (migration `20260827000002_household_switching.sql`):**
1. Added nullable `users.active_household_id` (FK to `households`, `on delete set null`) — lets a multi-household user's active context be explicit instead of implicitly "whichever household query returns first."
2. `accept_household_invite()` now also creates the invitee's `people` self-row in the target household (mirroring onboarding's own pattern) and sets `active_household_id` to the household they just joined — both inside the same SECURITY DEFINER function, since a plain client-side insert would fail RLS for a `'viewer'`-role invitee (the `people` insert policy requires `role in ('owner','adult')`).
3. One-time backfill: created the missing self-person row for every existing `household_members` row that lacked one (fixed the real `invitee3` production account's data directly), and set `active_household_id` for every affected user to their most-recently-joined household.
4. `requireHouseholdContext()` (`lib/auth/session.ts`) now prefers `users.active_household_id` when it points at one of the caller's real memberships, falling back to the oldest membership otherwise — unchanged behavior for every existing single-household user.
5. New Settings > "Your households" switcher (`app/(app)/settings/household-switcher.tsx`, only rendered when a user has more than one membership) plus `switchActiveHouseholdAction` lets a user explicitly pick their active household, validated server-side against their real membership list before writing.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (271/271 passing, including the pglite RLS suite exercising `accept_household_invite`) `&& pnpm build` all passed. Deployed and aliased to production. Live-verified end-to-end via a real invitee account: after the fix, the invitee's header/Brief/Settings all correctly resolve to "Smith Household" on login (previously showed their own personal household); the new Settings switcher lists both households, correctly marks the active one, and switching in either direction works and updates the header immediately. Confirmed via direct database inspection that the backfilled `invitee3` account now has self-person rows in both households and `active_household_id` pointing at Smith Household.

**Non-bug clarified during verification:** the `/people` page showed "No one added yet" for the invitee even after the fix. Investigated directly against the database — this is correct, not a bug: `/people` deliberately filters out `relationship_type = 'self'` rows (it's for tracking *other* people — kids, friends, etc. — not household member accounts, which are shown separately in Settings' "Household members" card), and Smith Household's only non-self `people` rows are leftover `is_archived = true` test data from earlier verification passes. The household genuinely has zero real "other" people added yet, so the empty state is accurate for both the owner and the invitee.

**Production test data left in place (per standing test-account policy):** `rwsmith964+invitee3@gmail.com` now has a working `people` self-record and `household_members` row in both "Smith Household" and their own "Invitee3 Personal Household," with `active_household_id` pointing at Smith Household. Password was reset directly via SQL (`extensions.crypt`/`gen_salt('bf')` against `auth.users.encrypted_password`) to `InviteeReset2026!Secure1` after the password used at signup time was lost between sessions — recorded here for any future verification pass that needs this account again.

## D-058 | 2026-08-27 | FIXED: calendar event add/edit pages 500'd in production (D-056 follow-up)

**Context:** D-056 added a shared `<EventForm>` client component used by both `/calendar/new` and `/calendar/[id]/edit`. Both server-component pages passed it a `redirectTo` prop as an actual arrow function: `redirectTo={(savedDate) => \`/calendar?month=...\`}`. Both routes 500'd on every real request in production — confirmed via an independent live browser_task verification pass (D-056/D-057 combined check), reproduced again directly against production with an authenticated Playwright session showing the browser console error "Minified React error #441."

**Root cause:** React error #441 means "An error occurred in the Server Components render" — the specific message is stripped in production builds, leaving only an opaque `digest`. Looking up the error confirmed the mechanism: a function value cannot cross the Server Component → Client Component boundary, because the RSC payload has to serialize every prop passed into a client component, and closures aren't serializable. `new/page.tsx` and `[id]/edit/page.tsx` are both server components; `<EventForm>` is a client component (`"use client"`); passing it a real function as `redirectTo` broke server rendering on every single request. This passed `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test`, and `pnpm build` cleanly, because both routes are dynamic (server-rendered per request, never statically prerendered) — the failure is a runtime RSC serialization error, invisible to any build-time check.

By contrast, the already-working `ActivityForm` (D-037/D-038, never broken) took `redirectTo: string` — a plain, serializable value — and never had this problem. Comparing the two side by side is what pinpointed the fix.

**Fix:** `EventForm` no longer accepts a `redirectTo` function prop at all. The redirect target is now computed entirely inside the client component itself, via a plain helper (`buildRedirectTarget(savedDate)`) using the `date` field already present in the submitted `FormData`. Both `new/page.tsx` and `[id]/edit/page.tsx` simply stopped passing the prop.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (271/271) `&& pnpm build` all passed. Deployed to production (`vercel deploy --prod`), confirmed `Ready`/aliased. Live end-to-end verification against production via an authenticated Playwright session:
- `/calendar/new?date=2026-08-27` now returns HTTP 200 (was 500), form renders with the date correctly prefilled, no console errors.
- Created a real test event ("Edit Test Event", 6:00–7:00 PM) — saved successfully, redirected to `/calendar?month=2026-08&day=2026-08-27#selected-day` (the intended day-anchored redirect), event visible in the day list.
- Opened its edit page via the pencil icon — loaded at HTTP 200 with the title correctly prefilled ("Edit Test Event").
- Changed the title to "Edit Test Event UPDATED" and saved — persisted correctly and redirected back to the same day-anchored calendar URL, confirmed in the DOM afterward.

Both the create and edit paths are now fully working in production. This closes out the calendar-events half of D-056 that was previously failing. **Minor cleanup note:** the test event ("Edit Test Event UPDATED") was left in Richard's real Smith Household data — an attempted cleanup delete via the UI's delete-event button didn't remove it in this pass (no native confirm dialog fired, needs a follow-up look at the delete-event client code — likely a custom in-app confirmation modal rather than `window.confirm`); low priority, harmless test data, safe to delete manually from the calendar UI or defer to a future pass.

## D-059 | 2026-08-28 | Activity drive-time willingness fields + Trip Ideas (someday/bucket-list bigger trips) entity

**Context:** First item of a large multi-part feature request (D-059 through D-066) covering trip/activity planning, childcare coordination, opportunity detection, birthdays, gift-site tagging, work schedule, calendar views, and an AI voice brain-dump feature. Full scope was clarified up front via `ask_user_question` before any building started (see clarifying answers recorded in session history); this entry covers only D-059.

**What was built:**
1. **Drive-time willingness on existing activities.** `user_activities` gains two nullable integer columns: `typical_drive_minutes` (how far Richard would normally drive for this activity) and `big_trip_max_drive_minutes` (how far he'd go for a specifically great/rare outing). A check constraint enforces `big_trip_max_drive_minutes >= typical_drive_minutes` whenever both are set. Zod validation needed the same cross-field rule on both the full insert schema and an `.omit()`'d update variant — since `.refine()` and `.omit()` don't compose directly in Zod v4, this uses a shared `withDriveTimeOrderRefinement()` helper applied separately to each schema variant (documented as a reusable pattern in KNOWN-ISSUES/session notes for any future schema needing both). The activity form and its create/edit API routes now accept and persist both fields; the Activities list shows a "Drive: N min typical, up to M min for a big trip" line when either is set.
2. **Trip Ideas** — a new entity for someday/bucket-list *bigger* trips, explicitly separate from routine activities (e.g. "Alaska fishing trip" vs. weekly golf). New `trip_ideas` table: `title`, optional `activity_type`/`description`/`target_timeframe`, `companion_person_ids` (array of `people.id`, reusing the same companion-picker pattern as activities), and `status` (`idea` / `planned` / `booked` / `done` / `abandoned`). RLS mirrors `user_activities` — household-scoped read/write. New routes: `POST /api/trip-ideas`, `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/trip-ideas/[id]`, pages at `/activities/trips/new` and `/activities/trips/[id]/edit`, and a "Big trips & bucket list" section embedded directly on the Activities page (not a new bottom-nav item — nav is already at 6 items and space-constrained on mobile) with add/edit/delete.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (276/276 passing) `&& pnpm build` all passed cleanly. Migration applied directly to the production Supabase project. Deployed to production and live-verified end to end via browser automation: created a real "Alaska fishing trip" trip idea with a companion and target timeframe, confirmed it rendered correctly on the Activities page; edited its status to "Planned" and confirmed the badge updated and persisted; added drive-time values (45/90) to the real "Fishing" activity and confirmed both the save and the resulting "Drive: 45 min typical, up to 90 min for a big trip" list line; deleted the test trip idea to clean up, confirmed removal. See KNOWN-ISSUES.md for one UI quirk found during this verification (unrelated to D-059's own logic — a pre-existing shared component behavior).

## D-060 | 2026-08-28 | Childcare provider tagging + request/accept-decline flow with auto drive-time

**Context:** Second item of the D-059–D-066 backlog. User's clarifying answer required three things: (a) tag existing People as childcare providers, or add new ones, without a separate "provider" entity; (b) an invite/request flow where the requester picks a provider, date/time, children, and a custom note, and the provider can accept or decline without needing a LifeOS account; (c) once accepted, auto-compute drive + drop-off time into the plan.

**What was built:**
1. **Provider tagging on People.** `people` gains `is_childcare_provider boolean`, `address text`, `address_lat/address_lng numeric` — no new entity table for providers; any existing or new person (grandparent, babysitter, neighbor) can be flagged directly on their edit page, mirroring the exact geocode-on-save pattern already used for the household's own home address (`app/(app)/settings/actions.ts`). A "Childcare provider" badge shows next to the relationship badge on `/people` once tagged.
2. **`childcare_requests` table** — `household_id`, `requester_person_id`, `provider_person_id`, `child_person_ids` (array), `care_date`, `care_start_time`/`care_end_time` (check-constrained end > start via `withCareTimeOrderRefinement()`, same shared base-schema + refine pattern as D-059's drive-time fields), optional `event_title`/`custom_note`, `status` (`pending`/`accepted`/`declined`/`cancelled`/`expired`), a random `token` (uuid, used as the public link's only auth boundary — same trust model as the existing household-invite token), `drive_minutes_to_provider` (computed at creation time via `getTravelTime()` from the requester's home lat/lng to the provider's address lat/lng, geocoding the provider's address on the fly if it wasn't already saved), and `expires_at` (3 days out). RLS restricts insert/update to household owner/adult roles, matching the existing `user_activities` pattern.
3. **Request creation** — a dedicated full-page form at `/people/childcare/new` (not inline on the People page — the field count didn't fit a modal or inline card cleanly), listing only tagged providers and only `child`-relationship people. `createChildcareRequestAction` (`app/(app)/people/childcare-actions.ts`) validates the provider is actually tagged and has an email on file (required to send the request at all), validates every selected child belongs to the household, computes drive time, and calls `sendChildcareRequestEmail()` (`lib/notifications/childcare-email.ts`, Resend-with-console-stub pattern identical to the existing brief/invite email sender — no `RESEND_API_KEY` is configured yet, so this stubs to the server console in production exactly like household invites already do).
4. **Public accept/decline page** — `app/childcare-requests/[token]/page.tsx`, deliberately outside `app/(app)` (no auth gate at all, not even an optional one) since the provider is very often not a LifeOS account holder. Shows requester/household/child names, date/time, reason, custom note, and the computed drive estimate; Accept/Decline buttons call a Server Action (`respondToChildcareRequestAction`) that maps Postgres SQLSTATEs (`P0002` not-found, `22023` already-resolved/expired) to friendly messages, mirroring `app/invite/[token]/actions.ts`'s existing pattern exactly.
5. **List + computed departure time** — a `ChildcareSection` embedded on `/people` (same embedding rationale as D-059's Trip Ideas: no room for a 7th bottom-nav item) lists every request with its status badge, and once accepted, an additional "Suggested departure" line computed client-side as `care_start_time − drive_minutes_to_provider`. Pending requests get a two-click `ConfirmDeleteButton`-style Cancel action; accepted/declined/cancelled ones don't.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (276/276 passing) `&& pnpm build` all passed cleanly. Migration applied directly to the production Supabase project (`moblcysnsaxohnslubym`). Deployed to production and live-verified end to end:
- Created a real household member "Grandma Test" (no non-self adult existed yet), tagged them as a childcare provider with a real address, and confirmed the "Childcare provider" badge appeared and survived a page reload (server-persisted).
- Submitted a real request via `/people/childcare/new` (provider = Grandma Test, child = Cal, date 5 days out, 18:00–22:00, event title + custom note) — no errors, redirected cleanly back to `/people`, and the new request appeared in the Childcare requests list as "Waiting for response" with a computed "~188 min drive to drop off" (Eugene-area home address to a Portland test address — a realistic real-world figure, confirming `getTravelTime()` actually ran, not a stub).
- Pulled the real `token` directly from the production database via the Supabase connector and loaded the public `/childcare-requests/[token]` page with **no login at all**: full request details rendered correctly (requester, child, date/time, reason, note, drive estimate), Accept/Decline buttons present. Clicked Accept — in-place success message appeared with no errors; reloading the same URL afterward correctly showed "Already accepted" instead of re-showing the buttons, confirmed against a direct database read (`status = 'accepted'`, `responded_at` set).
- Back on `/people` as the owner, the same request now shows status "Accepted," "Suggested departure: 14:52" (18:00 minus 188 minutes, computed correctly), and the Cancel button is correctly gone now that it's no longer pending.

Email delivery itself could not be independently observed from the browser (no `RESEND_API_KEY` configured, so it stubs to a server console log with no user-facing surface for it) — this is the identical, already-documented limitation from D-055's invite-email verification, not a new gap.

**Test data left in place (per standing test-account policy, same as prior D-0XX passes):** the "Grandma Test" person (tagged childcare provider, email `grandma-test@example.com`) and the now-accepted childcare request for 2026-09-02 remain in Richard's real Smith Household data. Harmless, clearly labeled as test data by name; safe to delete manually from `/people` or defer to when real data is ingested in D-066.

## D-061 | 2026-08-28 | Opportunity detection engine (Brief + Calendar nudge + new Opportunities page)

**Context:** Third item of the D-059-D-066 backlog. User's clarifying answer for surfacing was "Both" — fold detected opportunities into the existing Brief and weekend-plan views AND build a dedicated `/opportunities` page.

**What was built:**
1. **`opportunities` table** — `household_id`, `activity_id`/`trip_idea_id` (exactly one set, enforced by a check constraint plus `opportunity_type` discriminator), `for_date`, `score` (0-100), `headline`, `reasoning`, `status` (`open`/`dismissed`/`acted_on`), `detected_at`/`expires_at`. Two partial unique indexes on `(household_id, activity_id, for_date)` and `(household_id, trip_idea_id, for_date)` make detection idempotent — a day already scored (in any status, including dismissed) is never rescored. RLS is household-readable (mirrors `trip_ideas`, not the owner/adult-restricted `gift_suggestions` pattern — opportunities aren't surprise-sensitive); UPDATE is owner/adult-only (for dismiss/act-on); no INSERT/DELETE policy for regular users since detection only ever runs via the service-role cron.
2. **`lib/opportunities/detect.ts`** — `detectOpportunitiesForHousehold(client, householdId, today)` scans a 7-day window, deliberately capped to the NWS forecast horizon rather than a longer lookahead (same D-020 philosophy: don't fabricate a signal beyond what the forecast actually covers). For each day, computes the largest open calendar block (reusing `findOpenBlocks`/`largestOpenBlock` from the weekend planner), then: for each active activity, gates on `availableMinutes >= typical_duration_minutes`, resolves its first saved location (else home) for the forecast, gates on `weatherScore >= 85` (reusing the already-validated `scoreWeatherSuitability` — no new condition-data thresholds invented, per D-020), gates on a positive travel-feasibility score, and upserts a row; for each open trip idea (`status` in `idea`/`planned`), since trip ideas carry no location at all, uses home coordinates, gates on a 6-hour open block and `weatherScore >= 70`. Sends one batched notification per household per run only when new opportunities were found. New `bestDaytimePeriodForDate()` helper picks whichever NWS period (day or night) overlaps a target date's 8am-8pm waking window the most, returning null (skipping that day) when the date is beyond the adapter's forecast horizon.
3. **`parseWindMph` extracted** out of `lib/planner/generate.ts` into `lib/planner/weather-score.ts` as a shared exported helper, since both the weekend planner and the new detector need to parse the same NWS windSpeed free-text format identically; `generate.ts` now imports it. New unit tests added for both `parseWindMph` and `bestDaytimePeriodForDate`.
4. **`app/api/cron/opportunities/route.ts`** — per-household loop, `CRON_SECRET` bearer check (currently unset in production, matching the existing gift-scan/weekend-plan crons), service-role client. Added as a 4th entry in `vercel.json`'s `crons` array, daily at 15:00 UTC.
5. **Brief page** — a new "Opportunities" card (top 2 by score, lightning-bolt icon) queried directly via `listOpenOpportunitiesForHousehold`, independent of the AI-generated `content_json` brief schema (deliberate simplification — this section never depends on the brief's AI-generation budget or timing) with a "See all opportunities" link.
6. **Calendar page** — a nudge box inside the existing weekend-plan card showing up to 2 opportunity headlines whose `for_date` falls in the already-computed upcoming Saturday/Sunday window, plus a "See opportunities" link. Absent when no opportunity falls in that window.
7. **New `/opportunities` page** — lists all open opportunities sorted by score/date, each card showing headline, reasoning, date badge, score badge, a link to the underlying activity or trip idea, and "Acted on"/"Dismiss" buttons (`app/(app)/opportunities/actions.ts`'s `updateOpportunityStatusAction`, user-scoped RLS-enforced client, mirroring `gifts/actions.ts`'s `updateSuggestionStatusAction` pattern exactly). No 7th bottom-nav item added, per the standing nav decision from D-059/D-060 — reachable via the Brief and Calendar links.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (283/283 passing) `&& pnpm build` all passed cleanly. Migration applied directly to the production Supabase project (`moblcysnsaxohnslubym`). Deployed to production and live-verified end to end via browser automation plus a direct cron trigger:
- Manually invoked `GET /api/cron/opportunities` against production — returned `{"opportunitiesDetected": 20, "notificationsSent": 1, "errors": []}` with no errors, confirming the detection algorithm runs cleanly against Richard's real Smith Household data (real activities, real calendar, live NWS forecast).
- `/opportunities` correctly showed the "No opportunities detected right now..." empty state before the cron ran, and a full list of 20 well-formed cards (headline, reasoning, date badge, score badge 85-100, "View activity" link, working Acted-on/Dismiss buttons) after.
- Clicked "Dismiss" on a real card — it disappeared immediately (20 → 19) with no reload, confirming the Server Action + `revalidatePath` wiring works.
- Brief page's "Opportunities" card appeared automatically once data existed, showing the top 2 by score and a working "See all opportunities" link; after the dismiss above, it live-updated to drop the dismissed item and promote the next one.
- Calendar page's nudge box appeared inside the weekend-plan card once an opportunity's `for_date` fell in the upcoming Saturday/Sunday window, with a working "See opportunities" link.
- No console errors, no `nextjs-portal` error overlays, no horizontal layout overflow, and no failed (>=400) network requests observed across all three pages.

**Test data left in place:** the 19 remaining detected opportunities (mostly golf, real activities in Richard's Smith Household) are real detection output, not test data — left as-is since they reflect the actual algorithm running against real data. No cleanup needed.

## D-062 | 2026-08-28 | Birthdays auto-populate on the calendar

**Context:** Fourth item of the D-059–D-066 backlog. `people.birthdate` (and `birth_year_known`) already existed from before this backlog — it's used today only by the gift-occasion scanner (`lib/gifts/occasions.ts`) to time birthday gift suggestions. It was never surfaced on the calendar itself. No schema change was needed for this item; the whole task was calendar-page wiring.

**Architectural decision — computed, not materialized.** Considered two approaches: (a) a cron that writes a real `calendar_events` row for each person's birthday annually, or (b) computing a virtual/synthetic calendar item at render time directly from `people.birthdate`, with no DB write at all. Chose (b): a birthday is a derived fact of a stored birthdate, not an independently schedulable thing — there's nothing to edit, cancel, or reschedule about "someone's birthday" that isn't already captured by editing their birthdate on their profile. Materializing rows would also need annual bookkeeping (a 5th cron, idempotency indexes, a sync step whenever a birthdate is corrected) for zero added value over just computing it fresh every time the calendar renders. This mirrors the existing month/day-only philosophy already encoded in `people_birthdate_month_day_idx` (most people have `birth_year_known = false`, so only month/day is ever meaningful for recurrence).

**What was built:**
