1. **`lib/calendar/birthdays.ts`** (new, pure, unit-tested) — `birthdaysInRange(people, rangeStart, rangeEnd)` walks every calendar day in the range and matches it against each person's `birthdate` month/day, returning one `BirthdayCalendarItem` (`personId`, `personName` — nickname if set else full name, `date`, `age` — `null` when `birth_year_known` is false) per occurrence. Handles Feb 29 birthdates by observing them on Feb 28 in non-leap years (same rule `lib/gifts/occasions.ts` already applies, kept consistent rather than reinvented) and correctly returns multiple occurrences when the range spans more than a year. Archived people and people with no birthdate are skipped. Deliberately does **not** exclude `relationship_type === 'self'` the way the gift-occasion scanner does — that exclusion exists because the gift engine has no reason to suggest a gift for yourself, but seeing your own birthday on your own calendar is exactly the expected behavior, so `self` is included here.
2. **`app/(app)/calendar/page.tsx`** — computes `birthdayItems` via `birthdaysInRange(people, gridStart, gridEnd)` (the same `people` list and grid-range bounds already loaded for the page) and merges them into the existing `DayItem[]` pipeline alongside real events and custody blocks, so they get the month-grid dot, day-span indexing, and selected-day card treatment for free. New `kind: "birthday"` on `DayItem`, all-day, distinct `bg-pink-500` dot (visually separate from the standard `bg-primary` event dot), title from `birthdayTitle()` (e.g. "Cal turns 4", or "Cal's birthday" when the birth year isn't known), and a "Birthday" chip via a new `OTHER_CHIP_LABELS` map feeding the existing `humanizeChipLabel()` helper. Excluded from the custody-only view (`view=custody`) — that view is specifically about who-has-the-kids, not general household occasions. No edit (pencil) or delete (×) control is rendered for birthday items — `DeleteCalendarItemButton` is only rendered when `item.kind !== "birthday"` — since there's no underlying row to edit or delete; the only way to change a birthday is to correct the person's birthdate on their profile.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (294/294 passing, 11 new) `&& pnpm build` all passed cleanly. No migration needed (no schema change). Deployed to production and live-verified via browser automation against real Smith Household data (Callan "Cal" Smith, `birthdate = 2022-08-27`):
- The August 2026 month grid showed a dot cluster on Aug 27 only — one distinct `bg-pink-500` dot plus the two `bg-primary` dots for that day's real events — with every other day in the month showing zero dots.
- Selecting Aug 27 showed "Cal turns 4" as the first item in the selected-day list, subtitle "All day", badge "Birthday", with no pencil or delete icon — directly contrasted against the two real "Edit Test Event"/"Edit Test Event UPDATED" items on the same day, which both correctly retained their pencil and delete icons.
- Aug 26 and Aug 28 both correctly showed "Nothing scheduled" — confirming the birthday item appears only on its exact date, not smeared across neighboring days.
- Switching to the custody-only view (`view=custody`) for Aug 27 correctly showed "Nothing scheduled" — confirming birthdays are excluded from that view as designed.
- Zero console errors or exceptions observed across all navigations (CDP `Runtime`/`Page` event draining, not just a visual check).

**Known gap (not a regression, disclosed by the verification pass):** the Smith Household's real July/August 2026 data contains no custody blocks, so custody-*block* item rendering alongside a birthday couldn't be exercised end-to-end this pass — only the custody view's shell (tabs, legend, empty state) was confirmed. Custody-block rendering itself is unchanged by this feature and was already verified in earlier D-0XX passes; noted here only for completeness.

## D-063 | 2026-08-28 | Preferred gift-shopping sites per person + save-site action

**Context:** Fifth item of the D-059-D-066 backlog. Gift suggestions (`lib/gifts/suggest.ts`) always linked to a generic Amazon search for every suggestion — never an actual per-person preference. The spec asked for a per-person list of preferred gift-shopping websites plus a "save site" action, wired so suggestions prefer a saved site over the Amazon default.

**What was built:**
1. **`person_gift_sites` table** (new migration, applied directly to production) — `id`, `person_id` (fk `people`, cascade delete), `label`, `url`, `created_at`/`updated_at` (`set_updated_at` trigger). RLS mirrors `person_interests` exactly: household-readable via `person_is_in_my_household(person_id)`; insert/update/delete restricted to `person_household_write_role_ok(person_id)` (owner/adult only — a child-role member can read but not write, verified via a new pglite RLS test pair mirroring the existing `person_interests` block). Unique index on `(person_id, url)` so re-saving the same URL for the same person upserts (updates the label) instead of erroring or duplicating — same D-032 rationale as `person_interests`.
2. **`lib/db/repositories/people.ts`** — `personGiftSitesRepo` (generic CRUD factory) plus `listGiftSitesForPerson()`, ordered by `created_at`.
3. **`lib/db/schemas.ts`** — `personGiftSiteInsertSchema` (`label` trimmed/length-capped at 60 chars, `url` validated via `z.url()`), with new unit tests.
4. **"Preferred gift sites" card** on `app/(app)/people/[id]/page.tsx`, positioned after "Gift budgets" and before "Get gift ideas" (per the standing bottom-nav-stays-at-6-items decision — reachable via the existing person page, no new route or nav item). Lists saved sites as clickable links with a delete ("x") icon per row; an inline add-form (`AddGiftSiteForm` in `person-forms.tsx`, mirroring `AddInterestForm`'s `useActionState` + manual `dispatch()` pattern per the D-031 native-form-action pitfall) is the "save-site action" the spec calls for — label + URL fields, "Save site" button.
5. **`app/(app)/people/[id]/actions.ts`** — `addGiftSiteAction` (upserts on `person_id,url` conflict, friendly-error-wrapped) and `deleteGiftSiteAction`, mirroring `addInterestAction`/`deleteInterestAction` exactly. The delete button uses the shared `ConfirmDeleteButton` two-click confirm pattern (KNOWN-ISSUES.md 1.5) already used everywhere else in the app — not a regression, just the existing UX.
6. **`lib/gifts/retailer-links.ts`** — new `buildPreferredSiteSearchLink(site, title)`. Rather than guessing or maintaining a per-domain adapter for each saved site's internal search syntax (unknowable in advance — could be Etsy, a small boutique's Shopify store, anything), it builds a Google search restricted to that site's domain via `site:` — a single robust approach that works uniformly for any URL a user saves. Not a network call, pure string building — same "autopilot ceiling" as `buildAmazonSearchLink` (Section 7.5/7.6): a curated deep link, never an automated purchase. New unit tests cover normal domains, a leading `www.` strip, and a malformed-URL fallback to a plain query.
7. **`lib/gifts/suggest.ts`** — now fetches `listGiftSitesForPerson` alongside the other person context. When generating each of the 3 suggestions, if the person has at least one saved site, round-robins across them (`giftSites[index % giftSites.length]`) for that suggestion's `product_url`/`retailer` via `buildPreferredSiteSearchLink`; falls back to `buildAmazonSearchLink` when none exist. The existing gifts page already rendered `retailer` generically ("Search on {retailer}"), so no page change was needed there — it now shows "Search on Etsy" (or whatever label was saved) automatically.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (305/305 passing, 11 new: 4 schema tests, 5 retailer-link tests, 2 RLS tests) `&& pnpm build` all passed cleanly. Migration applied directly to the production Supabase project (`moblcysnsaxohnslubym`). Committed (`8d8bfd1`), pushed to `origin/main`, deployed via `vercel deploy --prod`, aliased to `lifeos-seven-rho.vercel.app`.

Live-verified end to end via two browser-automation passes against real production data (Callan "Cal" Smith):
- **Add/persist**: added a site (label "Fat Brain Toys", URL `https://www.fatbraintoys.com`) via the new form — appeared immediately as a link in the list, and was still present after a full page reload (confirming server-side persistence, not just client state).
- **Validation**: a blank label was blocked by native required-field validation; a malformed URL (`not-a-url`) was blocked by the input's native `type="url"` validation (Chrome's "Please enter a URL" message) — no bad row was ever created in either case.
- **Suggestion wiring**: generated gift suggestions for the person after saving the site — all 6 generated suggestions on the Gifts page showed "Search on Fat Brain Toys" (not "Search on Amazon"), each linking to a Google search scoped to `site:fatbraintoys.com` for that specific gift title — confirming the preferred-site override works exactly as designed.
- **Delete**: the first verification pass initially flagged the two-click `ConfirmDeleteButton` arm/confirm behavior as a possible bug (single click appeared to "revert" after ~2s); a follow-up pass confirmed this is the app's existing, intentional two-click confirm pattern (KNOWN-ISSUES.md 1.5, same behavior already shipped for interests/budgets/gifts) — two clicks within the 4-second window deleted the row for real, and it stayed gone after a full page reload. Test data ("Fat Brain Toys") was cleaned up; no lingering test rows.
- Zero console errors observed across either verification pass.

## D-064 | 2026-08-28 | Work schedule + AI-driven time-off entry via Quick Capture

**Context:** Sixth item of the D-059-D-066 backlog. The app had no way to record a person's recurring work hours or one-off time off, which blocked two things the spec wanted: (1) a person's regular work schedule visible on their page and reflected on the calendar, and (2) letting the AI log a day off from a casual Quick Capture note like "I'm taking Friday off" without the user filling out a form.

**What was built:**
1. **Two separate tables**, deliberately not one: `work_schedules` (a recurring weekly pattern — `person_id`, `day_of_week` 0-6, `start_time`, `end_time`, `label`, no date) and `time_off_entries` (real dated rows — `person_id`, `start_date`, `end_date`, `reason`, `source` `manual`|`quick_capture`, `check (end_date >= start_date)`). A recurring pattern and a dated exception-to-that-pattern are different kinds of facts and don't belong in one table. Not reusing `calendar_events` despite its `type` union already including `"work"` — kept as dedicated tables, consistent with the `person_interests`/`person_gift_sites` precedent (D-032/D-063) of small per-person tables over overloading a general-purpose one.
2. RLS on both tables mirrors `person_interests`/`person_gift_sites` exactly: household-readable via `person_is_in_my_household(person_id)`; write restricted to `person_household_write_role_ok(person_id)` (owner/adult only).
3. **`lib/calendar/work-schedule.ts`** — `workShiftsInRange()` computes work-shift occurrences at render time from the weekly pattern (never materialized into dated rows, same computed-not-stored philosophy as D-062 birthdays), and `timeOffInRange()` expands dated time-off rows across a calendar range. `isCoveredByTimeOff()` suppresses a computed work shift on any day covered by an approved time-off entry for that same person — unit-tested.
4. **Person page** — new "Work schedule" and "Time off" cards, positioned after "Custody (next 14 days)" and before "Contact cadence." Gated on `relationship_type !== "child"` (broader than the `!== "self"` gate used for Contact cadence), since a work schedule applies to any working adult in the household, not just the logged-in user.
5. **Calendar page** — computed work-shift items and real time-off items merged into the day's item list (excluded from the custody-only view, like birthdays). Work-shift items get no delete control (computed, not a row); time-off items get the standard two-click `ConfirmDeleteButton`, wired to a new calendar-scoped `deleteTimeOffFromCalendarAction`.
6. **Quick Capture** — new `add_time_off` action type. `capture.ts` rule 7 lets the model skip asking who when no person is named ("I'm off Friday" is near-certainly about the speaker), but — see the fix below — the model itself never picks *which* person; it leaves `personId: null` and the server resolves it via the already-authenticated `selfPerson` from `requireHouseholdContext()`. `app/api/capture/route.ts`'s `add_time_off` case defaults `endDate` to `startDate` when omitted and rejects an end date before the start date.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (328/328 passing) `&& pnpm build` all passed cleanly. Migration applied directly to the production Supabase project (`moblcysnsaxohnslubym`). Committed and pushed to `origin/main`, deployed via `vercel deploy --prod`, aliased to `lifeos-seven-rho.vercel.app`.

**Bug found and fixed during live verification — Quick Capture misattributed an unnamed time-off entry to the wrong person.** The first production verification pass submitted "I'm taking Friday September 4th off, reason vacation" (no person named) and the entry landed on a person called "Invitee Three" instead of Richard Smith. Root cause: the Smith Household legitimately has *two* people with `relationship_type = "self"` — Richard Smith, and "Invitee Three," a second real Supabase auth user who accepted a household invite during earlier D-055 testing (confirmed via direct production query: both rows have `is_archived = false` and distinct, real `user_id` values — not a stray/orphaned test row, a genuine second household member). My original rule 7 wording told the model to "default personId to whichever household person has relationship_type 'self' in the list" — with two such rows, the model had no principled way to know which one was the person actually typing, and picked wrong. The fix (same day, before this feature was considered done): rewrote rule 7 so the model leaves `personId: null` unconditionally when no other person is named, and never attempts to resolve "self" itself; `route.ts`'s existing `action.personId ?? selfPerson.id` fallback (unchanged — it was already correct) then resolves it server-side against the actually-authenticated `user_id`, which is the only place that information reliably lives. This is a correctness issue that would recur in *any* household with more than one adult member, not a fluke specific to leftover test data — worth calling out because it's the kind of bug that's invisible in a single-person household and only surfaces with real multi-user data, exactly the case the live Smith Household happened to already have from D-055.

Re-verified end to end after the fix, twice, with two different phrasings ("I'm taking next Tuesday off for a doctor's appointment" and "taking Thursday off, going fishing"), both unnamed: both entries landed correctly on Richard Smith's page and were confirmed absent from every other person's page (Cal, Em, Grandma Test) after each submission. Also re-verified the full happy path from the first pass: manual work-shift add/render/no-delete-button on the calendar, manual time-off add/render/delete-button-present on the calendar, time-off correctly suppressing the computed work shift on an overlapping day and the shift correctly reappearing once that time-off is deleted, and the standard two-click confirm-delete pattern working on both the person page and the calendar page. All test data (the work shift, and all four time-off entries created across both verification passes) was deleted afterward; both August and September 2026 calendar views were confirmed clean of leftover chips.

## D-065 | 2026-08-28 | Calendar week and day views

**Context:** Seventh item of the D-059-D-066 backlog. The calendar page only ever rendered a full month grid plus a single selected-day item list below it; there was no way to see just the current week at a glance, or to drop into a focused single-day agenda. No dedicated spec document exists for this item (checked `docs/`, DECISIONS.md's own "Section X" references, and the project knowledge wiki — none described week/day view behavior beyond the backlog title itself), so the concrete behavior below is this build's own design, following the app's existing patterns.

**What was built:**
1. **A new `Month / Week / Day` segmented control**, deliberately separate from the existing `All / Custody` filter toggle rather than folded into it — the two are independent dimensions (granularity vs. filter) and compose freely (e.g. Week + Custody), so combining them into one control would have made some valid combinations unreachable. Selection is carried entirely in the URL via a new `range` query param (`week` | `day`; omitted/anything else means `month`), consistent with how `view=custody` already works.
2. **One shared data window, not three.** `gridStart`/`gridEnd` (previously always the full month-grid window) are now branched by `range`: month keeps its existing `startOfWeek(startOfMonth(...))..endOfWeek(endOfMonth(...))`; week narrows to `startOfWeek(selectedDay)..endOfWeek(selectedDay)`; day narrows to just `startOfDay(selectedDay)` on both ends. Every downstream consumer — the events/custody/work-schedule/time-off queries, `birthdaysInRange`, and the `byDay` grouping — already operated purely off these two variables, so narrowing them was the entire behavior change; nothing else in the data pipeline needed to know which range is active.
3. **Week view reuses the month grid's exact JSX unmodified.** `gridDays` (`eachDayOfInterval(gridStart, gridEnd)`) is just a 7-element array when `range === "week"`, and the existing `grid-cols-7` layout renders that as a single row for free — no separate week-grid component was written. One adjustment was needed: the existing "dim days outside the current month" logic (`isSameMonth(day, monthDate)`) would have incorrectly grayed out half a week that crosses a month boundary, since every day in a week view is equally in-range regardless of which month it falls in — fixed by short-circuiting `inMonth` to always `true` when `range === "week"`.
4. **Day view drops the grid card's `CardContent` entirely** (header with prev/next chevrons only) and relies on the unchanged selected-day item list below to do all the work — with only one day in the window, a grid adds nothing over the header. The redundant "day name, month day" paragraph that normally sits above that list is suppressed in day view specifically, since the header directly above it already shows the same full date.
5. **Prev/next semantics differ by granularity, matching each range's natural unit:** month nav shifts the month and lets `parseDayParam`'s existing fallback pick the day (unchanged from before this feature); week nav shifts the anchor day by exactly 7 days via `subWeeks`/`addWeeks`; day nav shifts it by exactly 1 day via `subDays`/`addDays`. Week/day nav links also carry an updated `month=` param tracking the new anchor day's month, so switching back to Month view mid-navigation lands somewhere sensible rather than on a stale month.

No schema or migration — this is a pure read/render feature over data that already existed and was already being queried.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (328/328 passing, unchanged — no new `lib/` module, so no new unit tests; consistent with the existing precedent that `parseMonthParam`/`parseDayParam`/the weekend-plan date math in this same page are pure presentational logic left untested inline) `&& pnpm build` all passed cleanly. Committed (`0205017`), pushed to `origin/main`, deployed via `vercel deploy --prod`, aliased to `lifeos-seven-rho.vercel.app`.

Live-verified end to end via browser automation against production: Month/Week/Day switching updates the URL's `range` param correctly in all three states; Week view's header shows a date range (e.g. "Aug 23 – Aug 29, 2026") and renders exactly one row of 7 day cells; Week prev/next chevrons shift the header and the URL's `day=` param by exactly 7 days each click; clicking different day cells within a week correctly updates the selected-day item list, including a day with real items (a birthday plus two events) and a day with none; Day view's header shows a single full date with no grid rendered beneath it, its chevrons shift the date by exactly 1 day each click, and the date is confirmed to appear exactly once on the page (no duplicate from the suppressed paragraph); returning to Month view restores the full grid with no error and keeps the last-selected day highlighted; and Week + Custody were confirmed active simultaneously (`range=week&view=custody` in the URL together, custody legend and week grid both rendering correctly). No console errors were observed at any point in the verification pass.

## D-066 | 2026-08-28 | AI brain-dump feature (voice/text → multi-item review → save)

**Context:** Eighth and final feature item of the D-059–D-066 backlog, per the user's earlier clarifying answer: "full voice recording + transcription." Quick Capture (D-048) already let the user speak or type one short note and get one action, with a clarifying question allowed if the AI was unsure. The gap this closes: a long, rambling brain-dump covering several unrelated things at once ("Cal wants a Lego set, had a nice call with Grandma, I'm off Friday, add the standup to the calendar") has no single "action" to resolve to, and there's no conversational turn available to ask a clarifying question against a one-shot transcript.

**What was built:**
1. **Shared write path, not a duplicated one.** Extracted the `executeAction`/`isKnownPersonId` switch logic that used to live inline in `app/api/capture/route.ts` into `lib/ai/capture-actions.ts`, so Quick Capture and Brain Dump call the exact same code to actually create/update a row for each of the seven action types (`add_interest`, `log_interaction`, `record_gift`, `create_calendar_event`, `append_person_note`, `add_gift_budget`, `add_time_off`). Pure refactor, confirmed behavior-neutral via `pnpm typecheck` immediately after.
2. **A new one-shot prompt** (`lib/ai/prompts/brain-dump.ts`, `BRAIN_DUMP_SYSTEM_PROMPT`) that never asks a clarifying question — there's no turn to answer one on — and instead splits the transcript into zero or more items, each carrying a `summary` field for display plus the same field union `CaptureAction` already uses. Ambiguous or unnamed people resolve to `personId: null` (rule 2: "never guess") and unrelated filler is simply omitted (rule 5), rather than forcing every sentence into an action or blocking on uncertainty.
3. **Two new routes**, deliberately split into a read-only parse step and a per-item write step: `/api/brain-dump/parse` (transcript → AI → candidate items, no database writes at all) and `/api/brain-dump/execute` (one possibly-edited item → re-validated via the same Zod schema minus `summary` → shared `executeAction`). Splitting these means a bad AI parse never touches the database, and each item can be saved, edited, or discarded independently without re-running the model.
4. **A full-page record-or-type UI** (`/brain-dump`) reusing the exact Web Speech API dictation pattern already shipped for Quick Capture (`components/capture/capture-button.tsx`, D-048), but appending finalized speech onto the existing transcript instead of overwriting it — a long rambling dump has pauses, and each pause shouldn't erase what came before. The review phase renders one card per item with a type-switcher dropdown (lets the user recategorize a misclassified item without losing already-filled fields), type-specific editable fields, and independent Save/Discard per card plus a "Save all" batch action (deliberately sequential, not `Promise.all`, for predictable one-at-a-time UI feedback).
5. **Two entry points, no new nav item:** a "Brain dump →" link inside the Quick Capture panel header, and a card on the Brief page — the bottom nav stays at exactly 6 items per the D-064 decision.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (328/328 passing — no new unit tests, consistent with the existing precedent that `app/api/capture/route.ts` itself has zero dedicated unit coverage; verification for both leans on the full build plus live browser verification instead) `&& pnpm build` all passed cleanly. Committed (`0c5f08e`), pushed to `origin/main`, deployed via `vercel deploy --prod`, aliased to `lifeos-seven-rho.vercel.app`.

**Two real bugs found during live verification, both fixed and re-verified same pass (commit `455f68f`):**

1. **Day view's date-range query was zero-width, so it could never show a single calendar event — a pre-existing D-065 bug, not new in this feature, that this feature's testing happened to be the first to surface.** `gridEnd` for Day view was `startOfDay(selectedDay)`, identical to `gridStart`. Every range query downstream (`listEventsInRange`'s `.gte(starts_at, gridStart).lt(starts_at, gridEnd)` in particular) filters a half-open `[gridStart, gridEnd)` window, so a zero-width window always matched zero rows. A brain-dump-created "Team Standup" event showed a "✓ Saved" badge (confirmed via direct database query — the row really was written, correctly, to the correct household, with the correct time) but never appeared on `/calendar?range=day` for the day it was created on. D-065's own live verification hadn't caught this because its "day with real items" check happened while navigating day cells *inside Week view* (a correctly wide window), not by directly loading Day view itself. Fixed by using `endOfDay(selectedDay)` for Day view's `gridEnd` — the same end-of-last-day convention `endOfWeek`/`endOfMonth` already use for Week/Month, rather than introducing a new pattern. Re-verified: a fresh manually-created test event now correctly appears in Day view; deleted afterward.
2. **Gift history never displayed a gift's `cost_cents`, for any gift regardless of entry method (manual form, Quick Capture, or brain-dump) — also pre-existing, not introduced by this feature.** Confirmed via direct query that the brain-dump gift idea's $50 cost was persisted correctly; it was simply never rendered on the person page. Added it to the existing "occasion · date · reaction" line as "· $50.00". Re-verified: a fresh manually-recorded $25 test gift now shows "$25.00" in the list; deleted afterward.

**Not a bug — expected model variance, with an existing graceful fallback:** twice across two separate verification passes, the brain-dump parser left a gift-idea/interest item's `personId` null even though the person was unambiguously named in the transcript ("Cal wants a Lego set...", "Em is really into dinosaurs") — while correctly resolving other people named in the very same transcript (Grandma Test's logged interaction, in the same request as the Cal miss). The prompt's rule 2 already says to leave `personId` null rather than guess when uncertain, and the review UI's existing "pick a person, Save stays disabled until you do" behavior already handles this correctly — the user just has to make one extra selection. Added a small hint ("I couldn't tell for sure who this is about — pick someone to save it") under the Who selector when this happens, to make that existing fallback less confusing, but did not change the prompt itself: this is the model occasionally erring on the side of caution on a case that happens to be resolvable, not a rule that's wrong, and rule 2 is exactly what prevents a *wrong* auto-guess on a case that's genuinely ambiguous (the far worse failure mode).

**Investigated and ruled out as a bug:** a verification pass reported being unable to delete a test interest chip after several attempts (each looked like it registered — hover/focus/red-highlight changes — but the chip persisted after reload). A focused follow-up pass, clicking the confirmed same DOM element twice with correct timing and inspecting the network/console via CDP throughout, found the two-click `ConfirmDeleteButton` pattern (D-036) working exactly as designed: the arm state auto-resets after inactivity (by design, `armMs`), so a second click made too late is treated as a fresh first click rather than a confirm. Deleting within the arm window worked immediately, persisted through a hard reload, and produced zero console errors — a browser-automation timing artifact, not an application bug. No code change made. The leftover test interest and gift/event rows created across both verification passes were deleted directly.

All test data created during this feature's build (the Team Standup event, the Cal gift idea, the Grandma Test interaction, the Em "dinosaurs" interest, the Day-view verification event, and the $25 verification gift) was deleted from production before this entry was written; the Smith Household's real data is unchanged from before this feature.

## D-067 | 2026-08-28 | Ingest Richard's real data (final backlog item) + activity multi-location display fix

**Context:** Final backlog item, per the user's original brain-dumped voice note (the same one that motivated D-066): a rambling real transcript describing the user's own activities, favorite locations, and both kids' shared interests, given explicitly so this data would be entered into the app rather than left as an example. This pass reconciled that transcript against what was already in production (most of it had in fact already been entered earlier in the build, at 2026-08-28 13:22–13:24 UTC) and filled in the remaining gaps, then cleaned up leftover test data that earlier D-0XX entries had explicitly deferred to this exact pass.

**What was already correctly ingested (confirmed, no changes needed):** Fishing (typical drive 45 min, big-trip max 90 min — matches the transcript's "shorter drive range, maybe around 45 minutes, but willing to drive up to an hour and a half" verbatim); Golf at Fiddlers Green (companions Cal + Em) and Golf at Oakway Golf Course; Daddy Daughter Dates (companion Em); Em's interests (golf, lagoon play cafe, defy trampoline park, "anything pre-teen girl related", fishing, swimming); Cal's interests (golf, monster trucks, "anything 4 year old boy related").

**What was missing and added this pass:**
1. **A "Shooting" activity for Richard** (enjoyment 7/10, matching the same enthusiasm level as Fishing/Golf-at-Oakway since the transcript names golfing/fishing/shooting together as things he's "a big fan of"), with its two real alternative locations from the transcript, corrected from voice-to-text garbling using the addresses already confirmed via web search earlier in the build: **Creswell Clay Target Sports**, 81078 N Pacific Hwy, Creswell, OR 97426 (garbled in the original dictation as "Cresswell Sporting Clays"), and **Izaak Walton League Eugene Chapter**, ~30930 Izaak Walton Rd, Eugene, OR (garbled as "Isaac Walton Shooting Range south of Eugene"; noted as members-only per the transcript).
2. **A "Little Man Dates" activity for Richard** with Cal as preferred companion (enjoyment 10/10, matching Daddy Daughter Dates' rank since the transcript names both in the same breath with equal enthusiasm) — the transcript explicitly says "I also like to do Daddy-Daughter dates with my daughter Emlyn, or do little man dates with my son Cal," but only the Daddy Daughter Dates half had been entered.
3. **"lagoon play cafe" and "defy trampoline park" added to Cal's interests** — the transcript says "the kids BOTH really enjoy" these two places, but only Em had them recorded; Cal was missing both.

**Real bug found and fixed this pass:** live verification of the new Shooting activity found that only the FIRST location ("Creswell Clay Target Sports") rendered on the Activities page — the second ("Izaak Walton League Eugene Chapter") was correctly saved in the database (confirmed via direct query) but never appeared anywhere in the UI, including the edit form and every other page searched. Root cause: `app/(app)/activities/page.tsx` rendered only `activity.locations[0]`, even though `activity_locations` is a one-to-many table and `listActivitiesWithLocations` already fetches the full array — this bug existed since D-059 shipped but had never been exercised before because every activity until now happened to have exactly zero or one location. Fixed by mapping over the full `activity.locations` array and rendering one badge per location instead of a single hardcoded `[0]`. Re-verified live: both "Creswell Clay Target Sports" and "Izaak Walton League Eugene Chapter" badges now show under Shooting, confirmed to survive a hard reload.

**Known scope limitation, not fixed this pass:** the Activities create/edit form (`activity-form.tsx`) still only has a single "Usual location" input, and `POST /api/activities` only ever creates one `activity_locations` row per activity — there is currently no UI path to add a *second* location to an activity (the Izaak Walton League location above was added directly via SQL, not through the form). This is a reasonable scope boundary rather than an oversight: the form's location-specific fields (USGS gauge ID, ODFW zone URL, NOAA tide station) are inherently single-location concepts for river/coastal condition-tracking, and multi-location activities without those condition fields (like Shooting) are the minority case. Extending the form to a dynamic add/remove list of locations, each with its own optional condition fields, would be the natural next step if the user wants to manage a second location through the UI rather than asking for one to be added directly.

**Test data cleanup, deferred from earlier passes to this one on purpose (per those entries' own notes):** deleted the "Grandma Test" childcare-provider person and their one accepted test childcare request (a leftover from D-060 verification); deleted 5 already-inactive test/QA activities (Astoria Bay Crabbing, QA Test Activity 2, Test Activity D038, Test Activity Delete Check, Willamette River Fishing) that were `is_active = false` leftovers from D-038/D-059 verification passes. None of these were visible to the user in the app before deletion (all soft-inactive or clearly test-named), but removing them now leaves production with only real Smith Household data.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (328/328 passing, no new tests — this is a one-line JSX rendering fix plus data-only changes, consistent with existing precedent that this list page has no dedicated unit coverage) `&& pnpm build` all passed cleanly. Committed (`46ba274`), pushed to `origin/main`, deployed via `vercel deploy --prod`, aliased to `lifeos-seven-rho.vercel.app`, confirmed status Ready. Live-verified via browser automation: Richard's Activities page shows Shooting (both locations) and Little Man Dates; Cal's person page shows all 5 interests including the 2 newly added; the People list no longer shows "Grandma Test"; none of the 5 deleted test activity names appear anywhere on the Activities page.

This closes the full D-059–D-066 backlog plus the real-data ingestion item. D-041 (email round-trip) and calendar sync (Google/Apple) remain explicitly deferred per the user's earlier instruction and were not built.

## D-068 | 2026-08-29 | Per-person work-schedule calendar visibility toggle + co-parent schedule and custody conflict detection

**Context:** The user reported a real bug he found by using the app: he added a work schedule (D-064) for his mother — a `parent`-type person record, added for other reasons (e.g. tracking her birthday) — and it started populating on his main `/calendar`, which he didn't want. He explicitly said he *might* want that behavior for someone else he adds in the future (e.g. a spouse or partner), so the fix needed to be an opt-in toggle, not a blanket "never show anyone but self." In the same message he asked for two related features to be built: coparent schedule and custody conflict integration, and showing coparent work schedules on the custody calendar.

**Root cause:** D-064 shipped `app/(app)/calendar/page.tsx` fetching work schedules/time-off for *every* household person unconditionally (`householdPersonIds = people.map(p => p.id)`, no filter) — there was no concept of per-person visibility at all, so any person with a work schedule always showed on the main calendar regardless of relationship type or whether the household member actually wanted that.

**Decision 1 — opt-in visibility toggle:** Added `people.show_work_schedule_on_calendar boolean not null default false` (migration `20260829000001_show_work_schedule_on_calendar.sql`), backfilled to `true` only for existing `self`-type person rows so the primary user's own experience doesn't regress. Every other existing person (including the mother's row) is now `false` by default — exactly reverses the reported bug. A checkbox on the person edit page (`edit-person-form.tsx`), gated to non-child people (self included), lets a household member opt any person in or out; `updatePersonAction` treats the checkbox's *absence* from the submitted form (which happens for children, who never render the checkbox) as "leave the existing value unchanged" rather than coercing to false. The main calendar page now filters to `people.filter(p => p.show_work_schedule_on_calendar)` before calling `listWorkSchedulesForPeople`/`listTimeOffForPeopleInRange`.

**Decision 2 — co-parent schedule on the custody calendar:** `/calendar/custody` now always fetches every household person's work schedules/time-off (via the same repo functions, but *without* the new visibility filter — deliberately bypassing it) and renders a "[Name]'s schedule" section listing each `co_parent`-type person's upcoming shifts and time off for the next 14 days. This is intentionally independent of the new toggle: the custody page's whole purpose is full visibility for custody planning, so a co-parent's shifts should show there even if the household chose not to show them on the shared main calendar.

**Decision 3 — custody/work-shift conflict detection:** New pure function `detectCustodyWorkConflicts()` (`lib/custody/conflicts.ts`, 6 unit tests) checks, for every upcoming custody block, whether the block's `responsible_person_id` has a work shift (expanded via the existing tested `workShiftsInRange`, honoring time-off suppression) whose time window overlaps the block's `[starts_at, ends_at)` window — computed at render time rather than persisted, mirroring the "computed, not materialized" philosophy already established for birthdays (D-062) and work shifts themselves (D-064), since there's nothing to individually edit about a detected conflict. Deliberately checks whichever person is `responsible_person_id` on each block regardless of relationship type (self, spouse, co-parent, etc.) — the conflict is "the person on duty is also scheduled to work," not specifically a co-parent concern. Surfaced as an amber warning card (icon + "`[Person]` is scheduled to work (`[shift]` `[overlap start]`–`[overlap end]`) during this custody block.") inline on each affected block in the custody page's "Next 14 days" list; the reported window is the actual overlap intersection, not the full shift, so it tells the household exactly how much of the block is double-booked.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (334/334 passing, +6 new for `detectCustodyWorkConflicts`) `&& pnpm build` all passed cleanly. Committed (`814c5f5`), pushed to `origin/main`, deployed via `vercel deploy --prod`, aliased to `lifeos-seven-rho.vercel.app`, confirmed status Ready. Migration applied directly to production Supabase; confirmed via query that both real `self` rows (Richard Smith, Invitee Three) came out `true` and every other real person (Jackie Smith/"Mom", the children, existing archived co-parent test fixtures) came out `false`.

Live-verified end-to-end via browser automation, using a temporary active `co_parent` test person + one work-schedule row + one custody block created for the purpose (the household's only pre-existing co-parent records were leftover *archived* fixtures from earlier verification passes, which correctly don't appear — `listPeopleForHousehold` excludes archived people by design, not a bug):
1. No "Jackie Smith: Work..." entries appear anywhere on the main `/calendar` (Week view, both current and next week) — confirms the reported bug is fixed.
2. Jackie Smith's edit page shows the new checkbox, confirmed unchecked via DOM inspection.
3. `/calendar/custody` showed a "ZZ D068 Verify CoParent's schedule" section listing the correct upcoming Mon 9 AM–5 PM shifts.
4. The "Next 14 days" list showed the test custody block with the correct responsible person (not "Unknown") and an amber warning card with the exact expected text, correctly reporting the overlap window (12–5 PM) rather than the full shift (9 AM–5 PM).

All test data (the temporary person, work schedule, and custody block, plus a stray work-schedule row mistakenly created against an already-archived co-parent fixture during the first verification attempt) was deleted from production afterward, confirmed via a zero-row follow-up query.

**Known limitation, not addressed this pass:** the household owner's own `self` person record doesn't appear in the `/people` list (by design — self is managed via Settings, not the People list), so the self-checkbox couldn't be visually confirmed via the People UI; it was instead confirmed directly via database query (both `self` rows read `true` post-migration) and via the existing D-064 precedent that self's own schedule already worked correctly on the calendar before this change.

## D-069 | 2026-08-30 | P0 fix batch: calendar delete, AI nickname roster, notifications Markdown, brain-dump review binding, canonical people list

**Context:** User-reported P0 spec covering five broken/missing behaviors: (1) calendar event delete did nothing, (2) AI features didn't resolve nicknames ("Cal"/"Em") the app itself displays, (3) notifications rendered raw Markdown as literal text, (4) Brain Dump review step showed empty fields but saved different values than what was shown, and (5) three disagreeing household rosters plus leaked test data ("Invitee Three") and a nameless person record.

**Fix 1 — Calendar delete:** `delete-item-button.tsx` wasn't actually wired to the delete server action. Replaced the old two-click `ConfirmDeleteButton` pattern with a shared dialog+toast component (`components/ui/dialog.tsx`, `components/ui/toast.tsx`, `lib/hooks/use-confirm-delete.ts`) that wraps the real server action, shows a confirmation dialog, and offers an undo toast after deletion. This pattern now supersedes the old confirm-button app-wide.

**Fix 2 — Nickname-aware AI roster:** Added `buildChildTokenMap(people)` (`lib/ai/context.ts`) as the canonical nickname/display-name/alias resolution boundary, applied consistently across every AI feature (Quick Capture, Brain Dump) so "Cal"/"Em" resolve case-insensitively to the same person records the People page shows, in one batch per request rather than per-item.

**Fix 3 — Notifications Markdown:** `app/(app)/notifications/page.tsx` now renders notification bodies as Markdown instead of literal text, and strips a duplicate leading "## " heading before rendering.

**Fix 4 — Brain Dump review binding:** `brain-dump-client.tsx`'s review form now binds parsed values into the visible inputs (previously the UI showed empty fields while silently saving different values underneath). "Save all" is now disabled while any item is incomplete (amber warning + amber card border via a new `incomplete` flag). Vague/guessed dates get a visible "Guessed — double-check" badge. Items with no stated time now default to an all-day event instead of a fabricated 7:00 AM/9:00 AM start.

Real regression found and fixed during live verification of Fix 4: `brainDumpItemSchema` requires `eventDateApproximate` as a present (nullable) key, but the client's `toExecutePayload` `base` object never set it. `JSON.stringify` silently drops `undefined`-valued keys, so the missing key failed server-side zod validation for every item type, not just calendar events, blocking all Brain Dump saves in production immediately after the Fix 4 code shipped. Fixed by adding the missing key as an explicit `null` literal. Lesson recorded for future schema changes: when a new required-but-nullable field is added to a schema shared between an AI-generated payload and a client-constructed literal object, grep for every place a literal matching that schema is built, not just the AI prompt — and always re-verify with a live save/execute round-trip, since this class of bug is invisible to typecheck/lint/test/build.

**Fix 5 — Canonical people list + test data cleanup:** `listPeopleForHousehold(client, householdId, {includeArchived?, excludeSelf?})` is now the single canonical roster query, applied consistently on the People page and both Add/Edit Event pages (`excludeSelf: true` — "self is who's using the app, not someone they're keeping track of," consistent with prior decisions). Deleted the leaked "Invitee Three" test person (cascaded 3 briefs rows + 11 notifications rows) and a stale duplicate cadence row. Added name-required validation to prevent future nameless person records; the one existing nameless record was found and fixed.

Investigated but determined NOT to be a bug: Settings > Household Members showing a different list than the People page. These are two intentionally distinct concepts — `household_members` (who has an actual login/invite-access account: owner/adult/child/viewer roles) vs. `people` (family/contacts tracked in-app, who may not have logins, e.g. children Cal/Em with no accounts, or non-account parent Jackie Smith). Confirmed via query that Smith Household's `household_members` table correctly contains only Richard Smith (owner) — no drift bug. Documented so the user understands the distinction: **Household Members = who can log into this household's data; People = family/contacts you're tracking, which may not all have logins.**

Found but deferred (informational, not fixed): an invite history entry (`rwsmith964+invitee3@gmail.com` — accepted) has no corresponding `household_members` row in Smith Household. The accepting user's account is confirmed to be the owner of a separate household instead, suggesting the accept flow let them land in their own household rather than joining Smith Household. Left as-is since it's a leftover test artifact, not affecting any real user; flagged for the user's awareness.

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (341/341 passing) `&& pnpm build` all passed cleanly both before and after the Fix-4 regression patch. Committed in two pushes (`db39d91` main P0 batch, `13e3343` the eventDateApproximate regression fix), deployed via `vercel deploy --prod`, aliased to `lifeos-seven-rho.vercel.app`, confirmed Ready after each deploy.

Live-verified all 5 checks via browser automation against production, post-regression-fix:
1. Calendar delete: created a test event, deleted it with the new confirmation dialog, confirmed gone after a hard reload.
2. AI nickname resolution: verified "Cal" and "Em" both resolve correctly in Quick Capture and Brain Dump.
3. Notifications render as formatted Markdown, no literal "##" or "**" visible.
4. Brain Dump: submitted a 4-item multi-type note (calendar event with a person, a person note, a time-off entry, and a vague-date event). All 4 fields were visibly populated in review (not empty), "Save all" succeeded with no errors, and all 4 were confirmed persisted via direct database query after reload — matching the review screen's shown values exactly (correct title capitalization, correct 1-hour duration, correct event type, correct person attendee link, all-day flag correctly set on the no-time-stated item, guessed-date badge correctly shown).
5. People list is consistent across People page and Add/Edit Event; "Invitee Three" confirmed gone (0 rows); Household Members vs. People distinction confirmed intentional via query.

**Test data cleanup:** Deleted 5 calendar events (a leftover "Test event from review", a leftover "Tee time at Fiddlers Green" Sep 5, an old pre-fix buggy "Trampoline park with Cal" Sep 1 artifact, and two events created by this pass's own live-verification runs), 1 time-off entry created by verification, and de-duplicated a person note on Emlyn Smith's profile that had accumulated a duplicate line across repeated Brain Dump test runs. No duplicate Callan Smith gift suggestions were found in the current database (the `gifts` table for this household is currently empty) — that earlier-reported item appears to have already been resolved or never materialized under this person_id.


## D-070 | 2026-08-30 | P1-6/7/8: unified scoring engine, real drive time, thresholded/deduped/grouped Opportunities

**Context:** User-reported P1 spec, items 6-8: (6) `/opportunities` returned everything it detected (24 open rows across 7 days, most scored 100/"Exceptional"), with Golf at Fiddlers Green and Golf at Oakway Golf Course showing as two separate cards for what the user experiences as one activity; (7) every location card showed 0 min drive time; (8) the weekend plan and Opportunities disagreed on score for the same activity/day because two different code paths computed it.

**Root cause (7):** `activity_locations.lat`/`lng` were null for every one of the 6 existing location rows — the activity form only exposes raw manual numeric lat/lng inputs that nobody had ever filled in. The travel-estimate code fell back to the household's home coordinates when a location had no lat/lng, making the haversine distance from home-to-home exactly 0.

**Root cause (8):** `lib/opportunities/detect.ts` (the cron that populates the `opportunities` table) computed and stored the *raw weather score* as `opportunities.score`. `lib/planner/generate.ts` (the weekend-plan generator) computed a full 5-factor composite via `scoreActivity()`. Same activity, same day, two different numbers, because the two code paths never shared a function.

**Root cause (6):** With `detect.ts` storing the raw weather score, nearly every reasonably-good-weather day scored 100 and got labeled "Exceptional" — there was no threshold, no cap, and no notion that two `user_activities` rows ("Golf at Fiddlers Green", "Golf at Oakway Golf Course") are the same *thing* to the user, just two locations for it.

**Decision — one scoring function, one presentation layer, both shared:**

1. **`lib/planner/score-candidate.ts`** — new `scoreActivityCandidate()` is now the single scoring entry point. It resolves the best-matching forecast period, resolves the best/nearest usable location (see below), and calls the existing `scoreActivity()` composite exactly once. Both `detect.ts` (opportunities cron) and `generate.ts` (weekend-plan cron) now call this same function — no more parallel scoring logic. Extracted alongside it: `lib/planner/forecast-period.ts` (`bestForecastPeriodForDate`, moved verbatim out of `detect.ts`) and `lib/planner/recency.ts` (`nextSaturdayFrom`, `listRecentlyProposedActivityTypes`, `weeksSinceLastProposed`, moved verbatim out of `generate.ts`) so both callers see identical recency/weekend-date logic too.

2. **`lib/planner/travel-estimate.ts`** — new `resolveTravelEstimate()` computes drive time from a real source in priority order: (a) haversine from home to a location's lat/lng when both are non-null and the location isn't home itself, (b) the activity's own `typical_drive_minutes` when set, (c) an explicit "unknown" sentinel — never a false 0. `pickBestLocation()` picks the nearest usable location when an activity has more than one (the Golf/Shooting multi-location case). Downstream, a real "unknown" no longer gets treated as "0 minutes, so skip this candidate for being too far" — only an actual computed 0 does.

3. **Geocode-on-save fallback** (`app/api/activities/route.ts` POST, `app/api/activities/[id]/route.ts` PATCH): since manual lat/lng entry was confirmed to be a dead field nobody uses, both routes now call the existing `geocodeAddress()` helper (Nominatim, already used by Settings for the home address) against the location address/name when no manual lat/lng was submitted. Additive only — a manually-entered lat/lng is never overwritten, and editing an activity without touching its location name doesn't re-fire a geocode call. Backfilled the 5 already-geocodable production location rows directly (Fiddlers Green, Oakway, Magpie Cafe, Creswell Clay Target Sports, plus the Shooting location) via Nominatim lookups; the 6th ("Izaak Walton League Eugene Chapter") returned no match under two phrasings and was left null — not a blocker now that "unknown" degrades gracefully instead of lying as 0.

4. **`lib/opportunities/present.ts`** — new presentation layer, `getPresentedOpportunities()`, is the single place threshold/dedupe/tiering/grouping happens, so the Opportunities page, the Brief card, and the Calendar weekend nudge can never disagree about what counts as a standout:
   - `STANDOUT_MIN_SCORE = 70` — anything below this is filtered out entirely (previously: no floor, so mediocre days still showed).
   - Family-key grouping (`familyKeyFor()`) collapses same-activity-different-location duplicates (Golf@Fiddlers/Golf@Oakway → one "golf" card, showing whichever location scored higher) using a normalized activity-type key, not the raw `user_activities.id` — a display-layer fix; the underlying DB rows stay separate two-`user_activities` records pending the real consolidation deferred to P3 item 2.
   - `MAX_PRESENTED_OPPORTUNITIES = 5` per day cap.
   - Tiering (`Exceptional` / `Great` / `Good`) is now relative to the *presented* set, not an absolute score cutoff — `Exceptional` is reserved for the actual top-scoring card per day, so it can't apply to a dozen cards simultaneously the way the old "everything ≥ 100 says Exceptional" logic did.
   - `byDay` groups the flat list by `for_date` for the Opportunities page's day-grouped rendering; `flat` (the ungrouped, already-thresholded/deduped/capped list) is what the Brief card (`.slice(0, 2)`) and Calendar weekend nudge consume.
   - `detect.ts`'s stored headline changed from `"Exceptional {type} weather {day}"` (a claim the raw detector had no business making) to a neutral `"{type} — good conditions {day}"` — the tier label itself is now applied only at presentation time, from the actually-relative tiering above, not baked into the stored row.

5. **Render surfaces updated** to consume the new joined-with-subject repository query (`listOpenOpportunitiesWithSubjectForHousehold` / its date-range variant, replacing the old un-joined `listOpenOpportunitiesForHousehold`) piped through `getPresentedOpportunities()`: `/opportunities` page (day-grouped, tier-badged cards, honest empty state when nothing clears the threshold even if raw rows exist), the home Brief card (top 2 from the shared `flat` list), and the Calendar page's weekend nudge (same shared pass, scoped to the upcoming Sat/Sun window).

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint && pnpm test -- --run` (341/341 passing, no regressions — the moved-not-rewritten `bestForecastPeriodForDate`/`forecast-period.test.ts` tests still pass under their new name/path) `&& pnpm build` all passed cleanly. Committed (`a39c9c5`), pushed to `origin/main`, deployed via `vercel deploy --prod`, aliased to `lifeos-seven-rho.vercel.app`, confirmed status Ready.

**Stale-data wrinkle found during live verification, and how it was handled:** the detector is deliberately idempotent per `(household, activity|trip_idea, for_date)` — it "never re-detects a day it already scored, dismissed or not." The first live-verification pass showed all 5 Opportunities cards still displaying the *old* pre-fix headlines ("Exceptional X weather Y"), all-100 scores, and "about 0 min drive each way" baked into their stored `reasoning` text — because those 30 rows (29 open + 1 dismissed) had been written by the cron *before* this fix shipped, and the idempotency guard meant the fixed code would never touch those same (activity, date) pairs again on its own. With the user's explicit approval, deleted all 30 stale rows for the Smith Household from production (`DELETE FROM opportunities WHERE household_id = '312f2e2e-63a8-4a61-88d7-4f1c29e23ef8'`, confirmed 0 remaining), then invoked `GET /api/cron/opportunities` directly, which regenerated 17 fresh rows under the new logic with real drive times (7/12/4/32/45 min instead of 0), varied realistic scores (74-87/100 instead of a flat 100), and neutral headlines.

**Live-verified via browser automation against production, post-refresh:**
1. `/opportunities`: 5 cards grouped under 3 day headers (Sun Aug 30, Mon Aug 31, Sat Sep 5); every card's description shows a real non-zero drive time (12 min for Golf at Fiddlers Green, 7 min for Daddy Daughter Dates); only one golf card per day (Fiddlers Green — Oakway never gets its own card, confirming the family-key dedupe); "Exceptional" tier applied to only 1 of 5 cards (the actual top scorer, 87/100), the rest Great/Good.
2. Home Brief card: shows the same top-2 (both 87/100, Sun Aug 30) cards as the Opportunities page.
3. Calendar's Sep 5 weekend nudge: "Golf at Fiddlers Green ties for the highest score this weekend at 83/100" with a 12-minute drive — an **exact match** to the Opportunities page's own Sep 5 Golf at Fiddlers Green card (83/100, 12 min) — confirming P1-8's unified scoring: the brief, Opportunities, and weekend plan now all agree on the same number for the same activity/day.

**Known deliberate gap, not a bug:** the weekend-plan nudge's own alternates list (e.g. "Oakway 77/100, 4 min") surfaces both golf locations for comparison in its plain-text recommendation, while the Opportunities page's family-key dedupe only ever shows the single best-scoring location as a card. This is correct per spec — item 6 explicitly asked to "show the best location" as the *displayed card*, not to stop scoring/considering the alternative location entirely; the weekend planner's job is a full comparative recommendation, the Opportunities page's job is a curated highlight list.


## D-071 | 2026-08-30 | P1-9: birthdays in the daily brief + gift-occasion default-to-nearest-occasion

**Problem (verbatim from the spec):** "Cal's birthday (Aug 27) wasn't surfaced in brief; gift suggestions defaulted to `just_because` instead of `birthday` — need lead-time surfacing (30/14/7/3/1/day-of), 'this weekend' logic including recent past days, gift-occasion default to nearest upcoming occasion."

**Root causes, two separate bugs:**
1. `lib/brief/generate.ts` never looked at birthdates at all — the daily brief's context gathering (events, gift order-by reminders, overdue contacts, prep obligations, weather, weekend-plan mention) simply had no birthday input, AI-generated or templated. A birthday could be the very next day and the brief would never mention it.
2. The manual "Get gift ideas" form (`GenerateSuggestionsForm` in `app/(app)/people/[id]/person-forms.tsx`) hardcoded `defaultValue="just_because"` on the occasion-type `<select>` and today's date on the occasion-date `<Input>`, regardless of whether the person actually had a real upcoming occasion. Since the automated overnight scan (`lib/gifts/scan.ts` → `scanUpcomingOccasions()`) already tags occasions correctly, this hardcoded default only bit when a household member manually clicked "Get gift ideas" before the automated scan had run for that occasion — which is exactly what happened for Cal.

**Fix:**
1. **New `birthdaysToSurfaceInBrief()` in `lib/calendar/birthdays.ts`** — surfaces a birthday only at specific lead-time milestones (30/14/7/3/1/day-of, i.e. `BIRTHDAY_LEAD_TIME_MILESTONE_DAYS = [30,14,7,3,1,0]`), **plus** a 3-day recent-past lookback (`BIRTHDAY_RECENT_PAST_LOOKBACK_DAYS = 3`) so a birthday that just happened is still caught — this is the literal "'this weekend' logic including recent past days" requirement, generalized to "recent past" rather than hardcoded to a calendar weekend, since the reported bug (Aug 27 birthday, Aug 30 brief) is a Thursday-to-Sunday gap, not a weekend-specific one. Deliberately milestone-based, not "every day for a month" — same anti-nagging reasoning as the gift order-by prompt-window buffer (`lib/gifts/leadtime.ts`'s `isPastPromptDate`). New `birthdayLeadTimeLabel()` renders the day-count as `"in 3 days"` / `"today"` / `"3 days ago"` — never a raw ISO date, per the spec's UI-facing rule.
2. **`lib/brief/generate.ts`** now computes `birthdaysToSurfaceInBrief(householdPeople, todayStart)` alongside the existing gift/contact/prep queries, maps each through the existing child-token redaction (`tokenMap.labelFor`), and threads it into a new `birthdays` field on `BriefContextInput` (`lib/ai/prompts/brief.ts`).
3. **AI prompt** (`BRIEF_SYSTEM_PROMPT`) gained rule 6: any birthday in context MUST appear in `headsUp`, using the exact given relative timing, and a past birthday must still appear (framed as a catch-up nudge) rather than being dropped — reusing the existing `headsUp`/`people` schema rather than adding a new output field, per "don't refactor beyond what each fix needs."
4. **Templated fallback** (`lib/brief/template-fallback.ts`) mirrors the same rule deterministically: birthdays map to `headsUp` items first (before gift reminders/prep), phrased as "Coming up {timing} — turning {age}." for upcoming/today or "Was {timing} — turned {age}." for past.
5. **New `nearestUpcomingOccasionForPerson()` in `lib/gifts/occasions.ts`** — checks for a birthday/anniversary within the last 3 days first (see live-verification note below for why this recent-past check was necessary), then falls back to the existing `scanUpcomingOccasions()` for a single person with a 366-day horizon (long enough to always find at least a Christmas candidate). `app/(app)/people/[id]/page.tsx` now computes this per-person and passes it as `defaultOccasionType`/`defaultOccasionDate` props into `GenerateSuggestionsForm`, which now defaults its form fields to the real relevant occasion instead of the hardcoded `just_because`/today. Falls back to `just_because`/today only when the person is excluded from occasion scanning entirely (archived or `relationship_type: 'self'`).

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint` both clean. `pnpm test -- --run` — **354/354 passing** (13 new: 6 in `birthdays.test.ts` covering the exact reported bug scenario, milestone boundaries, and the non-milestone/lookback-exceeded negative cases; 5 in `occasions.test.ts` for `nearestUpcomingOccasionForPerson`; 2 in `template-fallback.test.ts` for the upcoming/past headsUp phrasing) — no regressions. `pnpm build` passed, all routes compiled.

**Live-verification caught a second bug in this same fix, fixed before shipping:** the first cut of `nearestUpcomingOccasionForPerson` used only `scanUpcomingOccasions()` (strictly future-looking). Deployed and tested live against Cal (birthdate Aug 27, viewed Aug 30 — 3 days after), the "Get gift ideas" form defaulted to **Christmas (Dec 25, 2026)**, not birthday — because his birthday's *next* occurrence is a year away (Aug 27, 2027, 362 days out) while Christmas is only ~117 days out, so Christmas is the mathematically nearer *future* date. That's the literal opposite of the intended fix: the whole point was to default to "birthday" for someone whose birthday just passed uncelebrated. Fixed by adding `recentPastOccasionForPerson()`: a birthday/anniversary that occurred within the last 3 days (mirroring `BIRTHDAY_RECENT_PAST_LOOKBACK_DAYS`, duplicated as a local constant in `occasions.ts` rather than imported, to avoid a circular import since `birthdays.ts` already imports from `occasions.ts`) now takes priority over any future occasion. Re-verified live after redeploy: Cal's page now correctly defaults to occasionType="birthday", occasionDate=2026-08-27 (his actual birthday, not a re-projected future date).

**Live-verification results (production, Aug 30 2026, household Smith Household):**
- Deleted the stale pre-fix daily brief row for today (`briefs` table, household `312f2e2e-63a8-4a61-88d7-4f1c29e23ef8`, `brief_date = 2026-08-30`) via `confirm_action`-approved SQL, matching the D-070 stale-cache lesson — briefs are idempotent per `(person, date)` and the home page only generates one on-demand when missing.
- Home page brief regenerated on next page load. "Heads up" section now reads: **"Cal turned 4 — 3 days ago / Birthday was 3 days ago — if there's anything uncelebrated or unsent, now's the time to close that loop."** — confirms the lead-time-milestone + recent-past-lookback logic and the templated-fallback phrasing both work correctly in production.
- Cal's person page "Get gift ideas" form: after the `recentPastOccasionForPerson` fix, occasion dropdown correctly defaults to **birthday**, date field to his actual birthdate (not Christmas, not today's date).

## D-072 | 2026-08-30 | P1-10: gift order-by dates never render in the past

**Problem (verbatim from the spec):** "Gift order-by dates render already in the past — never show a past order-by date; show 'Needed now'/days-remaining instead."

**Root cause:** `app/(app)/gifts/page.tsx` rendered the raw stored `order_by_date` string unconditionally (`Order by {suggestion.order_by_date}`). `listActiveSuggestionsForHousehold()` intentionally does not filter suggestions out once their order-by date passes (a suggestion is still useful to browse — the gift can still be bought, just urgently), so any suggestion whose window had elapsed showed a calendar date visibly in the past, reading as broken/stale.

**Fix:** New `orderByStatusLabel(orderByDate, today)` in `lib/gifts/leadtime.ts` (co-located with the other pure order-by-date math per the existing single-source-of-truth pattern) replaces the raw date with a relative, always-actionable label: `"Needed now"` at or past the order-by date (styled destructive/urgent), `"1 day left to order"` / `"N days left to order"` while there's still time (styled as muted, non-alarming). `app/(app)/gifts/page.tsx` now calls this instead of interpolating the raw ISO string, so a past-tense date can never reach the screen — as a side effect this also removes one raw-ISO-date leak (the broader raw-enum/ISO-date sweep is P2 item 2 and intentionally out of scope here beyond this one line).

**Verification:** `pnpm typecheck && pnpm lint` clean. `pnpm test -- --run` — **358/358 passing** (4 new tests in `leadtime.test.ts`: past-due→"Needed now", exactly-due→"Needed now" not "0 days left", singular "1 day left", plural "N days left"). `pnpm build` passed.

**Live-verification (production, Aug 30 2026):** Existing production data already covered the past-due case without needing seeded test data — 8 active suggestions for Callan Smith with order-by dates of 2026-08-19/20/25 (all in the past relative to today). Confirmed on the live [Gifts page](https://lifeos-seven-rho.vercel.app/gifts): all 8 cards now show **"Needed now"**; zero occurrences of the literal string "Order by" or any raw `YYYY-MM-DD` deadline text anywhere on the page. (The household's only future-dated suggestions belonged to a different seed household and correctly did not appear — confirmed via Supabase that person ids `30000000-...-0003`/`...-0008` belong to household `20000000-...-0001`, not the Smith Household, so this was pre-existing correct RLS scoping, not a P1-10 concern.) The future "N days left to order" branch is covered by unit tests only, since no in-household suggestion currently has a future order-by date to observe live — same function, already exercised for the past-due branch in production.

## D-073 (P1-11): Duplicate gift suggestions + unstable list order

**Symptom:** Production data for Callan Smith showed 8 active gift suggestions, several near-identical pairs generated by separate calls to `generateGiftSuggestions` (e.g. "Little Tikes Totsports Easy Hit Golf Set" vs "Little Tikes TotSports Easy Score Golf Set"; "Hot Wheels Monster Trucks Arena Smashers Playset" vs "Hot Wheels Monster Truck Arena Smash Playset"). The AI is given previously-dismissed titles as a soft "avoid repeating" hint, but nothing blocked it from producing a near-duplicate of a title that's still *active* (suggested/saved) or already bought. Separately, `listActiveSuggestionsForHousehold` ordered only by `order_by_date`, with no tiebreaker — ties (or Postgres's unspecified order for equal sort keys) could reshuffle the list across reloads.

**Root cause:** (1) No dedup check against currently-active/converted suggestion titles at generation time — only against dismissed titles, and only as an AI-prompt hint, not a hard block. (2) `order_by_date`-only sort has no deterministic tiebreaker.

**Fix:**
- `lib/gifts/dedupe.ts` (new) — token-overlap fuzzy title similarity (handles word order and light suffix/plural variation like "trucks"/"truck" via prefix matching), `isFuzzyDuplicateTitle`, `dedupeFuzzyTitles` (generic), and `dedupeSuggestionsPerPerson` (domain-scoped: dedupes within a person's suggestions only — a shared idea across two different people is not a bug). Threshold `0.6`, chosen with margin below both real observed duplicate pairs (0.75 and 1.0 similarity) and above typical incidental word overlap between distinct titles.
- `lib/gifts/suggest.ts` — now also fetches `listActiveAndConvertedSuggestionTitlesForPerson` and hard-blocks (skips inserting) any new AI suggestion that fuzzy-duplicates an active/converted title for that person, checked cumulatively so the 3 suggestions in one AI call are also deduped against each other.
- `app/(app)/people/[id]/actions.ts` / `person-forms.tsx` — `generateSuggestionsAction` now returns a distinct message ("No new ideas this time — similar gifts were already suggested for them.") when every AI suggestion in a run got deduped away, instead of a generic "Done" that would otherwise silently show nothing changed.
- `lib/db/repositories/gifts.ts` — `listActiveSuggestionsForHousehold` now chains `.order("order_by_date").order("person_id").order("generated_at").order("id")` for a fully deterministic sort. Added `listActiveAndConvertedSuggestionTitlesForPerson`.
- `lib/gifts/group-suggestions.ts` (new) — pure `groupSuggestionsByPersonAndRun`: groups the stably-sorted, deduped list first by person (order of first/most-urgent appearance), then by occasion run (person + occasion_type + occasion_date — the natural key of one `generateGiftSuggestions` call, since it always returns exactly 3 suggestions for one occasion).
- `app/(app)/gifts/page.tsx` — applies `dedupeSuggestionsPerPerson` to the already-sorted list (defensive, catches duplicates already sitting in the database from before this fix, not just new ones), then renders via `groupSuggestionsByPersonAndRun` with a person heading and an occasion-run sub-heading (`occasionTypeDisplayLabel` + formatted month/day) per group, replacing the old flat card list and its redundant raw "For {person} · {occasion_type} {occasion_date}" line (which also leaked a raw enum and ISO date — both now gone from this view).
- `lib/gifts/occasions.ts` — added `OCCASION_TYPE_DISPLAY_LABELS` / `occasionTypeDisplayLabel` for the new group headers.

**Tests:** `lib/gifts/dedupe.test.ts` (13 tests) — similarity math including both real production duplicate pairs and a false-positive guard; `lib/gifts/group-suggestions.test.ts` (4 tests) — grouping shape including the exact 5+3 production split. 375/375 tests passing overall (18 new).

**Verification:** typecheck, lint, full test suite, and production build all clean.

## D-074 | 2026-08-30 | Custody builder: multi-child selection + per-day handover times ("Weekly" mode)

**User feedback (verbatim):** "one of the issues that I'm seeing is that it's a little bit confusing when building out a custody schedule... I have both of the kids at the same time and there's no option to be able to select both kids for the custody arrangement you can only select one at a time... My custody arrangement is to pick up the kids at 4:30 in the afternoon on a Friday and have them through 8:30 AM Monday morning and it's a little confusing to try to build that out... maybe each day has its own time frames with a all day button that you can pick clearly delineating who has what kids and what times on what days."

**Problem:** Two compounding usability gaps in `new-schedule-form.tsx`: (1) the child selector was a single `<select>`, forcing one full schedule-building pass per child even when siblings share the exact same arrangement; (2) the custody engine had exactly one global `handover_time` per schedule, so a real "Friday 4:30pm pickup, Monday 8:30am return" pattern (two different handover clock times) could not be represented at all — the old "Custom cycle" UI could only ever apply one time to every transition.

**Root cause:** `custody_schedules.handover_time` was a single column with no per-transition override, and the form's data model mirrored that 1:1.

**Fix:**
- **Schema** — new nullable `custom_handover_times jsonb` column on `custody_schedules` (migration `20260830000001_custody_per_day_handover_times.sql`), keyed by stringified cycle `dayIndex` → `"HH:MM"`. Fully additive: null/missing key falls back to the existing `handover_time`, so every pre-existing schedule is unaffected. Validated in `lib/db/schemas.ts` (HH:MM regex per value, `.refine()` that every key is `< cycle_length_days`).
- **Engine** (`lib/custody/schedule.ts`) — new exported `cycleDayIndexForDate(schedule, date)` (extracted from the day-index math already inlined in `cycleAssignmentForDate`) and `handoverTimeForDayIndex(schedule, dayIndex)` (override-or-fallback lookup). `lib/custody/materialize.ts` now resolves each run's handover time from its **start day's** dayIndex and applies that same resolved time to both `starts_at` and `ends_at` of that run — deliberately does NOT borrow the next run's time for `ends_at`, because that would (a) shift `ends_at`'s date and break the calendar day-grid's inclusive date-range rendering, and (b) risk `ends_at < starts_at` for single-day runs when the next day's time is clock-earlier, violating the `custody_blocks_end_after_start` check constraint. The precise "handover happens at 8:30am Monday" instant is still fully captured — it's simply the *next* run's own `starts_at`, not this run's `ends_at`; no information is lost.
- **New "Weekly (day-by-day)" builder mode** in `new-schedule-form.tsx`, alongside "Common pattern" and "Advanced" (renamed from "Custom cycle"): 7 rows (Sunday–Saturday), each with a person selector, an "All Day" toggle (checked by default = no override, uses the schedule's single default handover time), and — when unchecked — a time input that becomes that weekday's entry in `custom_handover_times`. `anchor_date` is auto-derived as the most recent Sunday on/before the start date, so the user only ever thinks in weekday names, never abstract cycle day-indices. Cycle length is fixed at 7 for this mode. This directly expresses the user's real arrangement: Friday and Saturday/Sunday → Dad, unchecked "All Day" with 16:30; Monday → Mom, unchecked "All Day" with 08:30; every other day left "All Day" (default handover time).
- **Multi-child selection** — the single child `<select>` became a multi-select checkbox-button list. On submit, the client loops the existing `POST /api/calendar/custody/schedules` once per selected child with otherwise-identical parameters; no API/schema change needed for this part. A mid-loop failure reports which child failed and warns that earlier children in the same submission were already saved (not auto-rolled-back), rather than silently reporting total success or total failure.
- **Display** — new `describeCustodyHandoverTimes(schedule)` in `schedule.ts` renders "Handover 5 PM" for the common single-time case, or a per-day breakdown like "Handover Fri 4:30 PM, Mon 8:30 AM" once overrides exist (falls back to "Day N" labels instead of weekday names if `cycle_length_days` isn't a multiple of 7, since dayIndex→weekday only holds for whole-week cycles). Replaces the old raw `schedule.handover_time.slice(0,5)` line (a raw 24-hour string) on both the custody list page and the schedule detail page — also fixes a pre-existing raw-time leak.
- New shared `formatHandoverTime` helper ("17:00"/"17:00:00" → "5 PM"/"4:30 PM") so no raw 24-hour or seconds-precision time string reaches the screen anywhere in the custody UI.

**Tests:** `lib/custody/schedule.test.ts` — 6 new tests: `cycleDayIndexForDate` (anchor, wrap, before-anchor positive-modulo) and `handoverTimeForDayIndex` (fallback with no overrides, fallback for an unlisted dayIndex, override applied for the exact Fri-pickup/Mon-return scenario from the user's real arrangement). 381/381 tests passing overall (23 in `schedule.test.ts`, up from 17).

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test -- --run` (381/381), `pnpm build` all clean.

**Not yet done at time of writing:** migration not yet applied to production Supabase; not yet deployed to Vercel; not yet live-verified with the user's real arrangement (both children, Fri 4:30pm–Mon 8:30am) on `/calendar` and `/calendar/custody/[id]`. AI-upload-a-custody-agreement-and-parse feature (explicitly flagged best-effort by the user) not started — deferred until after this fix is fully shipped and verified.

**Live-verification (production, Aug 30 2026):** Created a real schedule for both children (Callan and Emlyn Smith) via the Weekly builder matching the user's exact arrangement — Fri/Sat/Sun → Richard (Fri override 4:30 PM, Sat/Sun default), Mon-Thu → Melissa (Mon override 8:30 AM, Tue-Thu default). Both schedules created successfully in one submission ("Create schedule for 2 children"). List page shows `"Custom pattern · Handover Mon 8:30 AM, Fri 4:30 PM"` for both cards; detail page shows `"Handover Mon 8:30 AM, Fri 4:30 PM · from Aug 30, 2026 · ongoing"` — no raw time/ISO strings anywhere. `/calendar` in Month/Week/Day views correctly renders "Cal with Richard Smith" / "Em with Richard Smith" at 4:30–5:00 PM on Fri/Sat/Sun and "Cal with Mel" / "Em with Mel" at 8:30 AM on Monday, with no overlap or double-booking.

**Bug found and fixed during live-verification:** unchecking a day's "All Day" toggle inserted a new time-input element inline, reflowing every checkbox below it in the same list — a real user toggling several days in a row could easily misclick the wrong day's checkbox right after a row above it grows. Fixed by always rendering the time input (fixed-width, `disabled` when "All Day" is checked) instead of conditionally mounting it, so toggling any row never changes the vertical position of the rows below it. Re-verified: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test -- --run` (381/381), `pnpm build` all clean; committed and redeployed to production.

## D-075 | 2026-08-30 | AI custody-agreement upload → parse → verify → fill builder (best-effort)

**User feedback (verbatim, same message as D-074):** "it would be nice to have a feature in the pool where you can upload custody agreements and have the AI reading pool and then filled out the calendar and then you verify that it is correct before continuing."

**Scope, stated honestly:** This is the best-effort feature the user explicitly flagged as such. Two deliberate limitations, surfaced in the UI (not hidden):
