1. **Text only, no OCR.** `lib/ai/client.ts`'s `callAi` wraps a text-only Anthropic call with no vision/image support, and adding real PDF/image OCR was out of scope for this pass. The upload UI accepts pasted text or a plain-text `.txt` file only; it explicitly tells the user PDFs and scanned images aren't supported and to paste the relevant section instead.
2. **Single repeating week only.** The parser maps the agreement onto the same 7-day Weekly builder model from D-074 — it does not support true multi-week cycles (alternating weeks, 2-2-3, 2-2-5-5). For a non-weekly agreement, the model still returns its best single-week approximation but must set `confidence: "low"` and list the mismatch in `unresolved`, which the review UI surfaces as an explicit warning telling the user to use the Advanced cycle builder instead for the full rotation.

**Implementation:**
- `lib/ai/prompts/custody-agreement.ts` (new) — `CUSTODY_AGREEMENT_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, `buildCustodyAgreementUserPrompt(agreementText, adults, children)`, and Zod schema `custodyAgreementResponseSchema` validating `weeklyAssignments` (7 keys "0"-"6" → personId|null), `weeklyHandoverOverrides` (dayIndex → "HH:MM"), `defaultHandoverTime`, `handoverLocation`, `childPersonIds[]`, `confidence` (high/medium/low), `unresolved[]` warnings, and a plain-English `summary`.
- `app/api/calendar/custody/parse-agreement/route.ts` (new) — `requireHouseholdContext()`, returns `{status:"unavailable"}` immediately if `!isAiConfigured()`; splits the household roster into adults/children via `listPeopleForHousehold` + `relationship_type`; calls `callAi` with `feature: "custody_agreement_parse"`; validates the response with `parseAiJson` + the new Zod schema; catches `AiUnavailableError`/`AiBudgetExceededError` into the same friendly `{status:"unavailable", message}` shape used by `lib/gifts/suggest.ts` (never lets either propagate to an unhandled route error, matching the established Section 12.4 pattern). **Nothing is written to the database in this route** — it only returns a proposed pattern. Defensively re-validates every returned personId against the household's actual roster before returning (never trusts an AI-produced id as authorization-safe input on its own).
- `new-schedule-form.tsx` — new collapsible "Have a custody agreement? Let AI draft a starting point (beta)" section above the mode buttons: textarea for pasted text, optional `.txt` file picker (client-side `FileReader`), explicit PDF/image-not-supported note, "Parse agreement" button with pending/error states. On success, a **review card** (the "verify" step the user asked for) shows a per-weekday assignment table, handover-time overrides, the AI's plain-English summary, a confidence badge, and any `unresolved` warnings. "Apply to builder below" writes the parsed result into the existing Weekly-mode state (`mode`, `weeklyAssignments`, `weeklyAllDay`, `weeklyHandoverTime`, `handoverTime`, `handoverLocation`, `selectedChildIds`) — it does **not** submit anything. The user can then inspect/edit the now-populated Weekly grid exactly like a manually-built one, and must still click the pre-existing "Create schedule" button to actually save. This two-stage flow (review card → apply to builder → user can edit → explicit submit) is the "verify before continuing" gate the user asked for.

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test -- --run` (381/381, unchanged — no new unit tests added for this feature since it's a thin AI-prompt + UI-wiring layer over already-tested builder state, and the AI call itself isn't practical to unit test), `pnpm build` all clean.

**Not yet done at time of writing:** not yet committed/pushed/deployed to production; not yet live-verified end-to-end with a real agreement text matching the user's actual Fri 4:30pm–Mon 8:30am arrangement.

**Bug found and fixed during live-verification:** the "Household adults" roster handed to the AI initially included every non-child household member (any `relationship_type !== "child"`), which incorrectly included a grandparent tagged `relationship_type: "parent"` (per the documented semantics in `lib/custody/eligible-parents.ts`, `"parent"` means "the household self-person's own parent," not "a parent of the children"). Testing with a real Father/Mother agreement, the model matched "Mother" to that grandparent instead of the actual co-parent — and since the Weekly builder's own person `<select>` only offers `self`/`co_parent`/`spouse`/`partner` (via `filterEligibleResponsibleAdults`), that id had no matching `<option>` at all, silently rendering 4 of 7 days as "Unassigned" in the builder (and "Unknown person" in the review card) even though the review card's plain-English summary text still read correctly — a dangerous mismatch a user skimming only the summary could easily miss before clicking Create schedule.

**Fix:** `parse-agreement/route.ts` now builds the AI's adult roster from `filterEligibleResponsibleAdults(people)` — the exact same set the builder's dropdown uses — instead of "everyone except children," and sanitizes every returned `weeklyAssignments` id against that same eligible set (previously validated against the full household roster, which let the grandparent's id slip through as "valid" even though it wasn't renderable). Every id the AI can now return is guaranteed to have a selectable option in the builder. Also added an explicit `{status:"unavailable"}` guard for households with fewer than 2 eligible adults (previously would have silently sent an unusable 0-1-adult roster to the model).

**Re-verified live (production):** same Father/Mother agreement text now correctly resolves every day — review card shows "High confidence" with Richard Smith (Fri/Sat/Sun) and Mel (Mon-Thu), zero "Unknown person" entries, only advisory notes (no location stated; confirm Father/Mother role-matching) rather than error-level unresolved flags. Applied to the Weekly builder: all 7 days show a real assigned person (no "Unassigned"), Monday correctly shows All Day unchecked with 8:30 AM, default handover time 4:30 PM covers the Friday transition, and the 14-day preview alternates correctly with no gaps. `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test -- --run` (381/381), `pnpm build` all clean after the fix; committed, pushed, and redeployed to production.

**Still not done:** the minor UX surprise where applying a parsed result auto-selects every child the agreement seems to cover (overriding whatever child selection the user had before parsing) was observed but left as-is — arguably correct behavior when an agreement doesn't name specific children, but worth a P2-style follow-up if it proves confusing in practice. No unit tests added (thin AI-prompt/roster-scoping layer, not practical to unit test without mocking the AI call).

## D-076 | 2026-08-30 | P1-12: gift Save feedback + Saved gifts view + Dismiss undo

**Problem (verbatim from spec):** "Clicking Save produces no toast, no badge, no change to the card, and there's no saved-gifts list anywhere in the app. I have no idea whether it worked. Dismiss removes the card but with no undo and no way to review what I've dismissed." Fix: "add a Saved state on the card, a Saved / Shortlist view, and an undo on Dismiss."

**Implementation:**
- `lib/hooks/use-async-toast-action.ts` (new) — a lighter-weight sibling to the existing `useConfirmDelete` hook (D-069/P0-1), for async actions that need pending state + a success toast + an optional Undo action but don't need a confirm-dialog gate first (Save/Unsave aren't destructive; Dismiss already gets its safety net from Undo rather than a confirm click, consistent with the ground rule "every destructive or async action needs visible feedback ... and undo where possible" — undo satisfies that without adding an extra click to the common case).
- `app/(app)/gifts/gift-suggestion-actions.tsx` (rewritten) — now takes a `status: "suggested" | "saved"` prop.
  - `status === "suggested"`: Save button → `updateSuggestionStatusAction(id, "saved")` + success toast ("Saved to shortlist. Find it anytime in Saved gifts."); Dismiss button → status `"dismissed"` + toast with an Undo action that restores to `"suggested"`.
  - `status === "saved"`: renders a "✓ Saved" badge plus "Move back" (→ `"suggested"`, toast "Moved back to suggestions.") and "Dismiss" (→ `"dismissed"`, Undo restores to `"saved"` — the undo target is whatever `status` the card had before dismissing, not hardcoded).
  - Buttons show inline pending text ("Saving…" / "Dismissing…" / "Moving…") while their transition is in flight — the loading-state half of the ground rule.
- `app/(app)/gifts/gift-suggestion-groups.tsx` (new) — the card-rendering JSX (title, price tier badge, reasoning, order-by status, retailer link, actions) factored out of the page so the main Gifts page and the new Saved gifts page render literally the same markup and can never visually drift apart (the "one source of truth" ground rule) — takes `PersonGroup<GiftSuggestionRow & {person} & {status: "suggested"|"saved"}>[]`.
- `app/(app)/gifts/page.tsx` — now fetches the same household-scoped active-suggestions query as before (still both `suggested`+`saved`, since `listActiveSuggestionsForHousehold` is also used by the order-by-reminder cron in `lib/gifts/scan.ts` and a saved gift still needs its reminder), dedupes, then **filters the main list down to `status === "suggested"` only** — once a suggestion is Saved it now moves out of "to consider" the same way Dismiss already removed a card, so a decision in either direction visibly changes this list rather than leaving a saved card sitting here indistinguishable from an undecided one. Adds a header link "Saved gifts (N)" to the new view, and mentions the saved count in the empty state when relevant.
- `app/(app)/gifts/saved/page.tsx` (new) — same query + dedupe, filtered to `status === "saved"`, rendered through the shared `GiftSuggestionGroups`; its own empty state ("Nothing saved yet…") links back to the suggestions list.

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test -- --run` (381/381, no regressions — no new unit tests added since this is UI/action wiring over already-tested repo/dedupe/group functions, not new business logic), `pnpm build` (clean; confirms `/gifts/saved` is now a registered route). Live-verification against production pending in this same pass.

**Bug found and fixed during live-verification:** `updateSuggestionStatusAction` only called `revalidatePath("/gifts")`, not `/gifts/saved`. Save/Move back/Dismiss/Undo all route through this one action, and both pages read the same underlying query — so any change made while on `/gifts/saved` (most visibly: clicking Undo after Dismiss) left that page showing stale data (the empty state) until a manual reload, which looks exactly like "Undo doesn't work" to a user watching that screen. The main `/gifts` page's own Dismiss+Undo cycle worked correctly in the same test since it revalidates the route it's already on. **Fix:** revalidate both `/gifts` and `/gifts/saved` on every status write, since both are affected by the same underlying data. Re-verified via a second live-verification pass (see below).

**Re-verified live (production), second pass:** Saved a gift → toast "Saved to shortlist." → card left the main list → appeared on `/gifts/saved` with the Saved badge. Dismissed it from the Saved page → toast "Dismissed." with Undo → clicked Undo → the item reappeared on `/gifts/saved` immediately, without a reload, badge and Move back/Dismiss buttons intact. Repeated Dismiss+Undo on the main `/gifts` suggestions list → card reappeared immediately without reload. No console errors, no layout issues. `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test -- --run` (381/381), `pnpm build` all clean after the fix; committed, pushed, and redeployed to production.

**Second re-verification confirms the fix**: Saved a fresh item ("Hot Wheels Monster Trucks Arena Smashers Playset") → Saved gifts page → Dismiss → clicked Undo within ~1s → item reappeared in the Saved gifts list at every check across a 5-second observation window, with no manual reload. No console errors throughout. D-076 is complete.

## D-077 | 2026-08-30 | P1-13: brief timestamp + staleness detection

**Problem (verbatim from spec):** the daily brief has no timestamp and no way to tell it's gone stale after the calendar or household data changed underneath it.

**Implementation:**
- `lib/brief/staleness.ts` (new) — pure `isBriefStale(generatedAt, rows)` comparing `brief.generated_at` against the `created_at`/`updated_at` of a passed-in set of rows; no I/O of its own, so it's trivially unit-testable.
- `lib/brief/staleness.test.ts` (new, 5 tests).
- `app/(app)/page.tsx` — computes `isStale` by querying events + custody blocks in the same today..+2-day window `generateDailyBrief` itself reads (`listEventsInRange`, `listCustodyBlocksForHouseholdInRange`), reusing the already-fetched `householdPeople` rather than adding a redundant query. Renders "Updated X ago" (via `formatDistanceToNow`) under the headline, and an amber `AlertTriangle` staleness banner pointing at the existing `RegenerateBriefButton` when stale.

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test -- --run` (386/386, 5 new), `pnpm build` all clean.

**Verified live (production):** brief header showed "Updated about 5 hours ago" on login; an amber staleness banner ("Your calendar or household info has changed since this brief was written — tap Refresh brief above to update it.") was already visible before any test changes — correct behavior, since something in the household (events/people) had legitimately changed in the intervening 5 hours, not a false positive. Added a same-day calendar event ("Staleness test event") — banner remained (as expected, it was already correctly flagging staleness). Clicked Refresh brief → banner disappeared, timestamp updated to "Updated less than a minute ago," and the regenerated brief's Today section now listed the new event and an updated headline — confirming the refresh actually pulled in the new data rather than just re-stamping the old content. Cleaned up the test event (deleted via the calendar day view; confirmed via a 404 on its old edit URL). No console errors, no visual issues. `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test -- --run` (386/386), `pnpm build` all clean; committed, pushed, and deployed to production.

## D-078 | 2026-08-30 | P1-14: Quick Capture shares Brain Dump's parser

**Problem (verbatim from spec):** "Quick capture rejects sentences Brain dump handles fine — Typing 'Cal's shoe size is 10' into Quick capture returns 'Couldn't understand that — try rephrasing.' That's a person note... Fix: have Quick capture call the same parser as Brain dump. On failure, keep the text in the box and say specifically what was unclear (e.g. 'I got the note but not who it's about — who is this for?') rather than a generic rejection."

**Root cause:** Quick Capture (`app/api/capture/route.ts`) and Brain Dump (`app/api/brain-dump/parse/route.ts`) each had their own prompt/schema (`CAPTURE_SYSTEM_PROMPT`/`captureActionSchema` vs `BRAIN_DUMP_SYSTEM_PROMPT`/`brainDumpResponseSchema`) that had drifted apart despite covering the same action types — a classic "two parts of the app disagree" case the ground rules call out directly. Capture's own prompt failed to resolve "Cal's shoe size is 10" into a valid `append_person_note` action for this specific input, while Brain Dump's prompt (same underlying model, different prompt wording) succeeded — and Capture's route had no fallback beyond a flat generic rejection on any parse/validation failure.

**Fix — single source of truth:** `app/api/capture/route.ts` no longer maintains its own prompt/schema call. It now calls the exact same `BRAIN_DUMP_SYSTEM_PROMPT` / `brainDumpResponseSchema` / `buildBrainDumpUserPrompt` Brain Dump uses, joining the running conversation's user-turn text into one flat transcript (so a clarifying question's follow-up reply combines with the original text exactly as if the user had said it all in one breath). The resulting `items[]` is adapted back into Capture's single-action-per-turn conversational shape:
- 0 items → `unrecognized` with a specific "nothing to save" message.
- >1 items → a specific `error` ("That sounds like more than one thing — try sending them one at a time.") instead of a generic rejection, since Capture (unlike Brain Dump's multi-item review list) only executes one action per turn.
- 1 item whose type needs a person (`add_interest`, `log_interaction`, `record_gift`, `append_person_note`, `add_gift_budget`) but has no resolved `personId` → `needs_clarification` with a type-specific question, e.g. exactly the spec's own example for `append_person_note`: "I got the note but not who it's about — who is this for?"
- Otherwise → executes directly through the existing shared `executeAction`/`isKnownPersonId` (`lib/ai/capture-actions.ts`, already shared with Brain Dump's own execute route) and returns a `Saved — {summary}` confirmation.

`lib/ai/prompts/capture.ts` keeps `CAPTURE_SYSTEM_PROMPT`/`captureActionSchema`/`buildCaptureUserPrompt` in place (nothing else imports them) with a comment marking them superseded, rather than deleting — `CaptureAction`/`CaptureTurn`/`CapturePersonContext` in that same file remain the live shared type contract used by the route and by `capture-actions.ts`.

**Client fix (`components/capture/capture-button.tsx`):** on a genuine `error` (or a network failure) — as opposed to the normal `needs_clarification` follow-up-question flow, which is expected to clear the input for a fresh reply — the original typed text is now restored into the input box (`setInput(text)`) instead of being cleared and stranded, so the user can edit and resend rather than retyping from scratch, per the spec's "keep the text in the box" requirement.

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test -- --run` (386/386, unchanged — this is prompt/route wiring over already-tested `executeAction`, not new business logic), `pnpm build` all clean. Committed (`b45bf0c`), pushed to `origin/main`, deployed to production via Vercel.

**Live-verified (production):**
- The exact bug-report input, "Cal's shoe size is 10", now returns `Saved — Add shoe size 10 note to Cal's profile.` (not the old generic rejection), and the note verifiably persisted to Callan Smith's profile (`Shoe size: 10` appears in his Notes card).
- An ambiguous-person case, "log a call today" (no person named), returns the specific clarifying question `I got that as a call or visit to log, but not with whom — who was this with?`; replying "Mel" in the same panel resolves to Melissa Smith and returns `Saved — Log a call with Mel today.` — confirming the multi-turn transcript-joining approach works end-to-end.
- A genuine compound-sentence case, "Em wants a bike for her birthday and also remind me to call the dentist tomorrow", did **not** trigger the new `>1 items` message — the underlying model returned only 1 item (the bike gift idea) and silently dropped the dentist-reminder clause rather than splitting it into two. Checked whether this is a regression from this fix by running the *identical* text through Brain Dump's own review UI directly: **Brain Dump exhibits the exact same behavior** ("1 item to review", dentist clause dropped, nothing saved since the item was discarded during the check). This confirms it's a pre-existing weakness in the shared prompt's item-splitting rule (rule 1: "unrelated thoughts in the same transcript are separate items"), not something this fix introduced or worsened — Quick Capture now simply inherits Brain Dump's exact extraction quality, which is the intended "single source of truth" outcome. **Not fixed in this pass** (out of scope for P1-14's literal requirement, and improving compound-sentence splitting is a distinct prompt-engineering problem affecting both features equally) — flagged as a candidate follow-up if the user wants stronger multi-item detection; logged here rather than silently discovered and dropped.

**Follow-up (same day, before starting P2):** the compound-sentence splitting gap noted above was fixed rather than deferred, per explicit user request. Root cause: `BRAIN_DUMP_SYSTEM_PROMPT` rule 5's "omit non-actionable filler" language was broad enough that the model classified "remind me to call the dentist tomorrow" as an unrelated to-do rather than a `create_calendar_event`, and rule 1 didn't explicitly address facts joined by a conjunction within one sentence rather than separated by a period. **Fix (`lib/ai/prompts/brain-dump.ts`):** rule 1 now explicitly instructs splitting facts joined by "and"/"also"/"plus" within a single sentence into separate items, with the exact bike-gift + dentist-reminder sentence from live-verification as a worked example; rule 5 now explicitly classifies any "remind me to X" phrase carrying a day/time signal as an actionable `create_calendar_event`, only omitting a reminder with no day/time signal at all. Since Quick Capture and Brain Dump share this one prompt, the fix applies to both at once. `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test -- --run` (386/386, unchanged — prompt-text-only change), `pnpm build` all clean; committed, pushed, and redeployed to production.

**Re-verified live (production):** the identical sentence, "Em wants a bike for her birthday and also remind me to call the dentist tomorrow", now correctly produces **2 items** in Brain Dump's review list ("Record bike as a gift idea for Em's birthday" + "Add reminder to call the dentist tomorrow", resolved to 08/31/2026 all-day) — both discarded without saving to keep the check non-destructive. The same sentence sent through Quick Capture now correctly returns `That sounds like more than one thing — try sending them one at a time.` (instead of silently saving only the gift and dropping the reminder), with the original text preserved in the input box per the keep-text-on-failure behavior. D-078 is now fully complete with no known gaps.

## D-079 | 2026-08-30 | P2-1 through P2-5: capture button clears the nav, human-readable enum labels, calendar Add-Event prefill/Cancel, collapsible weekend plan, dark-theme-aware native controls

**Problem (verbatim from spec, P2 rough-edge items 1-5):**
1. "The floating capture button overlaps the bottom nav on some pages, hiding the last row of content."
2. "Several places show raw enum values or ISO dates instead of human-readable text" — invite status on Settings, gift reaction and occasion-type dropdowns on the person page.
3. "Add Event doesn't pick up the day you clicked, and there's no way to back out once the form is open."
4. "The weekend-plan card on Calendar is always expanded and takes up a lot of space; the custody view has a redundant Custody button and no obvious way to edit schedules."
5. "Native form controls (checkboxes, selects, date pickers) render with light-theme browser chrome even when the app itself is in dark mode, and required-field validation pops the browser's own tooltip instead of an in-app message."

**Implementation:**
- **P2-1** (`app/(app)/layout.tsx`, `components/capture/capture-button.tsx`): the page's bottom padding (`pb-20`→`pb-36`) and the capture button's own offset (`bottom-20`→`bottom-24`) both grew, so the button now sits in the gap above the nav bar on every page instead of overlapping the last row of content on pages with less vertical content.
- **P2-2** (`lib/gifts/occasions.ts`, `app/(app)/people/[id]/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/settings/household-members.tsx`, `app/(app)/people/[id]/person-forms.tsx`): added `GIFT_REACTION_DISPLAY_LABELS`/`giftReactionDisplayLabel()` alongside the existing `OCCASION_TYPE_DISPLAY_LABELS`/`occasionTypeDisplayLabel()` (P1-11) as the one place any gift enum gets a human label; gift-history reaction text and the reaction dropdown now use it, invite status on Settings is capitalized, and — found during this session's live-verification, not the original pass — the three `occasionType` `<select>` dropdowns in `person-forms.tsx` (Gift budgets, Gift history/Record a gift, Get gift ideas) were still rendering the raw enum (`birthday`, `just_because`, `default`, …) as visible `<option>` text even though `occasionTypeDisplayLabel()` already existed; switched all three to call it, same as the reaction dropdown already did.
- **P2-3** (`app/(app)/calendar/page.tsx`, `app/(app)/calendar/event-form.tsx`): the header "Add" link now points at `/calendar/new?date={selectedDayKey}` so the form's Date field opens pre-filled to whichever day is currently selected instead of always defaulting to today; `event-form.tsx` gained a Cancel button (`router.back()`) next to Save, 50/50 flex, so an accidental open can be backed out of without a wasted click into a blank/no-op form.
- **P2-4** (`app/(app)/calendar/page.tsx`): "This weekend" now renders inside a `<details>/<summary>` disclosure, collapsed by default, with a rotating `ChevronDown` — the AI narrative could run ~400px tall and previously always pushed everything else below it down the page. The custody view lost its redundant standalone header "Custody" button (the view toggle already exists elsewhere) and gained a "Manage schedules" link next to the Em/Cal legend, since there was previously no obvious way to reach the schedule editor from that view.
- **P2-5** (`app/globals.css`, `components/ui/timezone-combobox.tsx` (new), `lib/hooks/use-form-validity.ts` (new), applied across every form in the app): `color-scheme: light`/`dark` set per theme class fixes native control *chrome* (backgrounds/text) app-wide from one place. The plain native `<select>` timezone picker (holding ~400 IANA zone names) is replaced with a dependency-free searchable combobox (hidden input carries the IANA value, click-outside handler, browser-zone-detection fallback). `useFormValidity(formRef)` replaces every form's own `reportValidity()` call with a shared hook exposing `{invalid, checkValid, clearInvalid}`, so a blank required field shows an in-app inline message (e.g. "An occasion date is required.") instead of the browser's own unstyled validation tooltip — applied to all ~10 forms across settings, activities, people, and calendar/custody.
  - **Follow-up, found during this session's live-verification (D-081):** `color-scheme` does not set the checked-state fill browsers use for checkboxes/radios — that stayed OS-default blue regardless of theme, since `accent-color` is a separate CSS property `color-scheme` doesn't touch. One rule in `globals.css` (`input[type="checkbox"], input[type="radio"] { accent-color: var(--primary); }`) themes every raw checkbox/radio in the app from the existing `--primary` token, with no per-input changes needed across the 8 files that use raw `<input type="checkbox">`.

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint` (one `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` finding in the new timezone combobox, fixed with a justified `eslint-disable-next-line` — the effect syncs `Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone` post-mount and can't use a lazy `useState` initializer without baking in the wrong value during SSR), `pnpm test -- --run` (386/386, no regressions), `pnpm build` all clean at each stage. Committed across `2e2c685`, `8ea2e0f`, `7dbeafc`, `c9cd613`, and this session's `cd6f0b3` (occasion-dropdown + checkbox follow-ups); pushed and deployed to production after each.

**Live-verified (production), first pass:** capture button clears the bottom nav on `/activities` and `/people` with no hidden content when scrolled to the true bottom (PASS). Invite status and gift-reaction labels are human-readable (PASS). Calendar day-click → Add prefills the clicked date and Cancel discards without persisting (PASS). Weekend-plan card collapses/expands correctly, custody view has exactly one Custody control and a working "Manage schedules" link (PASS). Timezone combobox filters correctly on "Denver" (PASS); blank required field on `/people/new` shows an inline red message instead of a native tooltip (PASS, though the message was a single generic banner rather than per-field text directly under the field — accepted as meeting the spec's intent). **Gap found:** the occasion-type dropdown still rendered raw snake_case text, and checkboxes still rendered as unstyled default browser checkboxes (both fixed in the follow-up above).

**Live-verified (production), second pass (after the follow-up fix):** all three `occasionType` dropdowns (Gift budgets, Gift history, Get gift ideas) on Callan Smith's person page now show only human labels (Birthday/Christmas/Anniversary/Graduation/Just Because/Occasion) for every option — confirmed via direct DOM inspection of the `<option>` elements, not just the pre-selected value. Checkbox computed style now resolves `accentColor` to a near-black value matching the app's own button color (not browser-default blue) — confirmed via computed-style inspection and a zoomed screenshot; noted that the app itself was in light mode at test time, so this specifically confirms the checkbox's own theming rather than a dark-mode-wide screenshot. D-079 (P2-1 through P2-5) is now fully complete with no known gaps.

## D-080 | 2026-08-30 | P2-6: default gift budget showed $0-$150 instead of the household's real $25-$150

**Problem (verbatim from spec):** "Default gift budget shows two different numbers. Settings says $25–$150; person page shows default $0–$150. Make the person page read the household default."

**Investigation:** neither `settings-form.tsx` nor `people/[id]/page.tsx` contains a hardcoded "$0" — both only render real values, either the household's own `default_gift_budget_min_cents`/`max_cents` or a person's own `person_gift_budgets` row, via the shared `resolveGiftBudget()` (`lib/gifts/budget.ts`, already unit-tested, resolution order `person_occasion → person_default → household_default → fallback`). Queried the live Smith Household row directly: `default_gift_budget_min_cents=2500, max_cents=15000` — exactly the Settings page's "$25–$150". Queried `person_gift_budgets` for the household and found Callan Smith and Emlyn Smith each have an explicit `occasion_type="default"` row with `min_cents=0, max_cents=15000` — exactly the reported "$0–$150" bug, and NOT test data listed in this doc's "Test data to clean up" section, so it's a real product bug rather than leftover fixture data.

**Root cause:** `lib/ai/capture-actions.ts`'s `add_gift_budget` handler (used by both Quick Capture and Brain Dump) zero-filled whichever side of the range the natural-language capture didn't state: `min_cents: Math.round((action.budgetMinDollars ?? 0) * 100)`. A capture like "Cal's gift budget is up to $150" states only a max, so the handler silently wrote a permanent person-specific `min_cents: 0` override — and because `resolveGiftBudget()` correctly prioritizes a person-specific row over the household default *by design* (a deliberate per-person override should win), it faithfully returns that broken $0 floor everywhere the budget is used: on the person page display, and in the actual "Get gift ideas" AI-generated suggestions (meaning Callan and Emlyn's gift suggestions could have been budgeted as low as $0, below the household's intended $25 floor — a more consequential version of the same bug than the display alone suggests).

**Fix (single source of truth, `lib/ai/capture-actions.ts`):** when a captured budget only states one side of the range, the unspecified side now falls back to the household's own default (`household.default_gift_budget_min_cents`/`max_cents`, already available on the `household` object passed into `executeAction`) instead of `0` — so a person's stored default stays in sync with the household default unless the user genuinely states both sides. This prevents the bug from recurring for any future capture, for any person, in any household.

**Data backfill:** the two already-existing bad rows (Callan and Emlyn's `min_cents=0`) don't get fixed retroactively by the code change alone, since it only affects new captures. Confirmed with the user before writing to production data; backfilled both rows' `min_cents` from `0` to `2500` ($25, matching the household default) via a direct `UPDATE ... WHERE occasion_type = 'default' AND min_cents = 0 RETURNING ...`, scoped to exactly the two known-bad person_ids so it can't touch a legitimately-different override elsewhere. Confirmed the update returned both rows with `min_cents=2500, max_cents=15000`.

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test -- --run` (386/386, no regressions — this is a data-shape fix over an already-tested `resolveGiftBudget`, not new business logic), `pnpm build` all clean. Committed (`cd6f0b3`), pushed, deployed to production.

**Live-verified (production):** the live-verification pass that ran immediately after deploying the code fix (before the backfill was approved) correctly still showed "$0–$150" for both Callan and Emlyn — confirming the code fix alone, as expected, doesn't touch already-existing rows. After the backfill above, the underlying data now reads `min_cents=2500` for both — matching the household's $25–$150 default. D-080 is complete.

## D-082 | 2026-08-30 | P2-7: confirm/undo/loading feedback on destructive actions + "Get gift ideas" loading text and results navigation

**Problem (verbatim from spec):** "No confirmation, no undo, no loading state on key actions. 'Remove' on activity and 'Dismiss' on gift delete immediately with no confirm/undo. 'Get gift ideas' runs ~10s with no spinner, then a small 'Done' appears with no auto-navigation. Match Brain dump's pattern ('Reading through that…') everywhere, and navigate to results."

**Investigation — Remove/Dismiss already fixed:** checked `app/(app)/activities/deactivate-button.tsx` (the activity "Remove" button), `app/(app)/activities/delete-trip-idea-button.tsx` ("Dismiss" on a trip idea), and `app/(app)/people/[id]/person-forms.tsx`'s `DeleteGiftButton` ("Dismiss" on a gift) — all three already route through the shared `ConfirmDeleteButton` (`components/ui/confirm-delete-button.tsx`, backed by `lib/hooks/use-confirm-delete.ts`): a real confirm modal (not the discoverability-poor two-click pattern P0-1 replaced), a `pending` state disabling the button mid-request, and inline error feedback on failure. This was already addressed in an earlier segment (the D-076 gift Save/Dismiss/Undo work and its siblings elsewhere), so the literal "immediately with no confirm/undo" behavior described in the spec no longer matches the current code — verified rather than assumed, since the compacted session summary going into this segment had flagged these two files as still bypassing confirmation.

**Fix — "Get gift ideas" loading text and navigation (`app/(app)/people/[id]/person-forms.tsx`, `GenerateSuggestionsForm`):** the button already showed a spinner + "Thinking…" while pending (so "no spinner" specifically was already handled), but didn't match Brain Dump's own loading copy and left the user on the person page after a successful ~10s generation with only a small "Done" line, no path to the actual results. Changed the pending label to "Reading through that…" (matching Brain Dump's `brain-dump-client.tsx` exactly), and added a `useEffect` that navigates to `/gifts` on a successful run — but only when the run actually produced new suggestions (`state.success && !state.message`); when nothing new came back, `generateSuggestionsAction` sets an explanatory message ("No new ideas this time — similar gifts were already suggested for them.") and the form stays put so that message is seen rather than being navigated away from before it can be read.

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test -- --run` (386/386), `pnpm build` all clean. Committed (`cd6f0b3`), pushed, deployed to production.

**Live-verified (production):** clicked "Get gift ideas" on both Callan Smith's and Emlyn Smith's person pages; both runs (~11-13s) ended with the browser automatically landing on `/gifts` showing real itemized suggestions, with no manual click required. Confirmed the on-screen pending text is "Reading through that…" via the live-served compiled JS bundle (a repeat live click was blocked by the household's daily AI-suggestion quota being exhausted from earlier tests in the same pass) — searched the entire bundle for "Thinking" and found no remaining occurrence. D-082 is fully verified; D-079's "Remove"/"Dismiss" portion was already correct going in, so P2-7 as a whole is complete.

## D-083 | 2026-08-30 | P3-1: last-done tracking for activities

**Problem (verbatim from spec):** activities had no way to record when they were last actually done, so the weekend planner's recency scoring only knew about the last time an activity was *proposed*, not the last time it was genuinely done, and there was no way to log a past occurrence by hand.

**Fix — data model:** added `last_done_at date` (nullable) to `user_activities` via `supabase/migrations/20260830000002_activity_last_done.sql`, applied live to production. Added the column to `UserActivityRow`/`UserActivityInsert` (`lib/db/database.types.ts`) and to `userActivityBaseSchema` (`lib/db/schemas.ts`, `isoDate.nullable().optional()`).

**Fix — scoring (single source of truth):** new `weeksSinceLastDone(lastDoneAt, today)` helper (`lib/planner/recency.ts`, null-safe, floors to whole weeks, returns null for null input or a future date) and a new `combinedWeeksSinceRecent(weeksSinceLastProposed, weeksSinceLastDone)` helper (`lib/planner/scoring.ts`) that takes `Math.min` of the two signals rather than adding a second independent recency penalty — so "proposed recently" and "actually done recently" both suppress the same recency score instead of fighting each other. Wired through `score-candidate.ts`, `lib/opportunities/detect.ts`, and `lib/planner/generate.ts` (same call site pattern as the D-070 single-scoring-path precedent). The new field is optional everywhere it's threaded through, defaulting to null-equivalent behavior, so no existing caller or test needed mechanical updates.

**Fix — UI:** activities list (`app/(app)/activities/page.tsx`) shows "Last done \<Mon D\>" or "Not logged as done yet" per card (never a raw ISO date, per ground rule) via a new `MarkDoneButton` (`app/(app)/activities/mark-done-button.tsx`, calendar-check icon) that calls a new `markActivityDoneTodayAction` (`app/(app)/activities/actions.ts`, writes today's local date via `date-fns format`, matching the app's existing local-date convention rather than UTC `toISOString()`) and shows a success toast. The edit form (`activity-form.tsx` / `[id]/edit/page.tsx`) gained a "Last done (optional)" date input with helper text explaining it's normally set automatically. Both API routes (`app/api/activities/route.ts` POST, `app/api/activities/[id]/route.ts` PATCH) parse `lastDoneAt` into `last_done_at`.

**Fix — opportunity hook:** `updateOpportunityStatusAction` (`app/(app)/opportunities/actions.ts`) now writes `last_done_at = opportunity.for_date` onto the linked activity whenever a household marks an opportunity "acted on" — using the opportunity's own date rather than "today," so acting on a future-dated opportunity correctly back-dates (well, exact-dates) the activity instead of always stamping the click time. Best-effort (wrapped in try/catch so a failure here doesn't block the status update itself); revalidates `/activities`.

**Tests:** `lib/planner/recency.test.ts` (5 new tests for `weeksSinceLastDone`), `lib/planner/scoring.test.ts` (3 new tests: proposed-only vs done-only equivalence, combined-signal `Math.min` behavior, omitted-vs-null backward compatibility).

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm exec eslint .`, `pnpm exec vitest run` (394/394 across 41 files), `pnpm run build` all clean. Committed `a091a08`, pushed, deployed to production.

**Live-verified (production):** on `/activities`, every card showed "Not logged as done yet" before any action. Clicking "Mark done today" on "Little Man Dates" updated its card to "Last done Aug 30" (today) with no page reload needed. The edit form for that same activity showed the "Last done (optional)" field pre-filled `08/30/2026`, confirming the form reads the field back correctly. On `/opportunities`, clicking "Acted on" for the Monday, Aug 31 "Golf at Fiddlers Green" opportunity (a future-dated opportunity relative to today) updated that activity's card on `/activities` to "Last done Aug 31" — the opportunity's `for_date`, not "today" — confirming the acted-on hook uses the correct date rather than the click timestamp. No raw ISO dates or enum values appeared anywhere in the UI. D-083 is fully verified.

## D-085 | 2026-08-30 | P3-3: seasonality window + needs_daylight gating on activities

**Problem (verbatim from spec):** "Seasonality and daylight on activities. Add a season window and 'needs daylight' flag per activity; check proposed window against sunrise/sunset." Activities had no way to say "only in season March-October" or "only worth suggesting if there's enough daylight left" -- a winter-only or daylight-dependent activity could be proposed by the opportunity detector or weekend planner at any time of year, in any remaining light.

**Fix -- data model:** added `season_start_month smallint`, `season_end_month smallint` (both nullable; NULL/NULL = year-round), and `needs_daylight boolean not null default false` to `user_activities` via `supabase/migrations/20260830000003_activity_seasonality_daylight.sql`, applied live to production. Check constraints enforce 1-12 range and that the two month columns are null/non-null together (can't set only one end of the window). Added the three fields to `UserActivityRow`/`UserActivityInsert` (`lib/db/database.types.ts`) and to `userActivityBaseSchema` plus a new `withSeasonWindowRefinement()` helper (`lib/db/schemas.ts`, same pattern as the existing `withDriveTimeOrderRefinement`), composed into both the insert and update schemas.

**Fix -- gating logic (single source of truth):** new pure, unit-tested module `lib/planner/seasonality.ts`:
- `isActivityInSeason(activity, date)` -- month-range check, handles wrap-around (e.g. start=11 end=2 covers Nov-Feb); NULL/NULL always passes (year-round, so every pre-existing activity keeps behaving exactly as before).
- `computeDaylightWindow(date, lat, lng)` -- sunrise/sunset via `suncalc` (same library already used by `lib/external/solunar.ts`, no new dependency); handles the polar day/night edge case in the library's types defensively even though no supported household latitude can hit it.
- `daylightOverlapMinutes(block, daylight)` / `hasSufficientDaylight(activity, block, daylight)` -- minutes of overlap between a candidate open time block and the day's daylight window; a needs_daylight activity only passes if that overlap covers its typical duration. Non-needs_daylight activities always pass; a missing block (no open time at all) fails a needs_daylight activity.

Wired as a **hard pre-filter**, not a 6th weighted scoring component -- consistent with the existing hard gates in `detect.ts` (weather threshold, travel feasibility, available minutes) and "don't refactor beyond what each fix needs" (adding a weight would mean rebalancing `SCORING_WEIGHTS` and risking broader behavior shifts). Both `lib/opportunities/detect.ts` (per scanned day) and `lib/planner/generate.ts` (once for the weekend window) compute `daylight` once and gate each activity with `isActivityInSeason` + `hasSufficientDaylight` before scoring -- the identical rule shared via one module rather than duplicated logic. Scope is per-activity only, per spec wording; trip ideas (which have no season/daylight fields) are untouched.

**Fix -- weekend-plan empty-result messaging:** `generateWeekendPlan`'s `no_candidates` result gained a `noCandidatesReason: "missing_setup" | "seasonally_unavailable"` field, because the season/daylight gates can now filter out *every* activity even when the household has activities and a home address configured (e.g. every activity on file is winter-only and it's currently August). The calendar action (`app/(app)/calendar/actions.ts`) now shows an accurate "none of your activities are in season this weekend" message for that case instead of always misdirecting to "add an activity / set your home address."

**Fix -- UI:** the activity form (`activity-form.tsx`) gained a "Season window (optional)" section with From/Through month-name `<select>` dropdowns (blank option = "Year-round") and a "Needs daylight" checkbox with explanatory helper text; both API routes (`app/api/activities/route.ts` POST, `app/api/activities/[id]/route.ts` PATCH) parse the three new formData fields with `fieldMap` entries for validation-error routing; the edit page (`[id]/edit/page.tsx`) reads the three columns into form defaults. The activities list (`app/(app)/activities/page.tsx`) shows a "Season: Mon–Mon" badge and a separate "Needs daylight" badge when set -- never raw month integers (1-12) or the raw boolean, per the ground rule against showing raw enum values to the user. New shared `lib/planner/month-names.ts` (`MONTH_NAMES`, `monthName`, `seasonWindowLabel`) used by both the form's select options and the list badge, so the two never disagree on how a month number is spelled out.

**Tests:** `lib/planner/seasonality.test.ts` (12 new tests: year-round/normal/wrap-around/single-month season windows; full/partial/zero daylight overlap; sufficient/insufficient daylight scenarios, using Eugene, OR coordinates). Full suite: 406/406 across 42 files.

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm exec eslint`, `pnpm exec vitest run` (406 tests, 42 files), `pnpm run build` all clean. Committed `0220956`, pushed to `main`. Migration applied live to production Supabase. Deployed to production via Vercel, aliased to lifeos-seven-rho.vercel.app.

**Live-verified (production):** created a temporary "D085 Test Winter Activity" with season window December–February and "Needs daylight" checked. The `/activities` list correctly rendered a "Season: Dec–Feb" badge and a separate "Needs daylight" badge -- no raw month numbers or booleans anywhere. Opening its edit page confirmed the form correctly pre-filled the From/Through selects to December/February and the checkbox to checked, round-tripping the saved values exactly. Deleted the test activity afterward (confirmed gone from the list on reload) -- the household's real 6 activities (Golf, Fishing, Daddy Daughter Dates, Shooting, Little Man Dates, Defy Trampoline Park) were untouched throughout. Did not additionally trigger the AI-backed opportunity detector or weekend planner live against this test activity, to avoid burning the household's daily AI-suggestion budget on a throwaway record; the actual season/daylight gating logic itself is covered end-to-end by the 12 passing unit tests against `isActivityInSeason`/`hasSufficientDaylight`, which is what `detect.ts` and `generate.ts` call directly.

## D-086 | 2026-08-30 | P3-4: saved-gifts shortlist lifecycle (Saved -> Ordered -> Given)

**Problem (verbatim from spec):** "A saved-gifts shortlist with lifecycle states: Saved -> Ordered -> Given, writing 'Given' into Gift history." The Saved gifts view (D-076/P1-12) only ever had two states -- suggested and saved -- with no way to mark something as bought/ordered, and no way to close the loop into the recipient's actual Gift history once given. `suggestion_status` already had an unused `converted_to_gift` value and the historical `gifts` table already had a `given` status, but no code path ever connected the two.

**Fix -- data model:** added `'ordered'` to the `suggestion_status` enum, between `'saved'` and `'dismissed'`, via `supabase/migrations/20260830000004_gift_suggestion_ordered_status.sql` (`alter type ... add value`, additive and safe, applied live to production). Added `"ordered"` to `SuggestionStatus` (`lib/db/database.types.ts`) and `suggestionStatusSchema` (`lib/db/schemas.ts`). `listActiveSuggestionsForHousehold` and `listActiveAndConvertedSuggestionTitlesForPerson` (`lib/db/repositories/gifts.ts`) both now include `"ordered"` in their active-status filters, so an ordered suggestion keeps showing up on the shortlist and keeps suppressing duplicate re-suggestion. Deliberately left `listSuggestionsDueForOrder` filtering only `["suggested", "saved"]` -- once something is marked Ordered its order-by-date reminder should stop firing.

**Fix -- single source of truth for the conversion:** new pure, unit-tested `suggestionToGivenGiftInsert()` (`lib/gifts/convert.ts`) maps a `GiftSuggestionRow` to a `GiftInsert` (title -> description, occasion fields, category, estimated_cost_cents -> cost_cents, product_url, status always `"given"` regardless of the suggestion's own status, notes recording the retailer when known). Called from exactly one place: the new `markSuggestionGivenAction` server action (`app/(app)/gifts/actions.ts`), which fetches the suggestion, creates the `gifts` row via `giftsRepo.create`, flips the suggestion to `converted_to_gift` (the existing terminal state), and revalidates `/gifts`, `/gifts/saved`, and the recipient's `/people/[id]` so the new Gift history entry shows up immediately with no manual reload.

**Fix -- undo on the terminal step:** unlike the other status transitions (which are plain status flips with a status-revert undo), "Given" writes a real, permanent history row, so its undo (`undoMarkGivenAction`) also deletes that `gifts` row before reverting the suggestion back to `"ordered"` -- otherwise Undo would leave a phantom entry sitting in Gift history. The client (`gift-suggestion-actions.tsx`) tracks the created gift's id in a ref set by the action's return value, matching the existing `useAsyncToastAction` loading/success/undo pattern (D-076) used by every other action on the card.

**Fix -- UI, three-state card:** `GiftSuggestionActions` now branches on `"suggested" | "saved" | "ordered"`: suggested keeps Save/Dismiss; saved adds "Mark ordered" alongside the existing Move back/Dismiss; ordered shows an "Ordered" badge with a primary "Mark given" button plus Move back (to saved)/Dismiss. `SuggestionWithStatus` (`gift-suggestion-groups.tsx`) widened to the three-value union so the shared card renderer used by both `/gifts` and `/gifts/saved` can't drift. `/gifts/saved/page.tsx` now includes both `"saved"` and `"ordered"` suggestions (previously `"saved"` only) -- an ordered gift stays visible on the shortlist instead of silently disappearing. `/gifts/page.tsx`'s "Saved gifts (N)" badge count now sums both statuses so it doesn't undercount once something moves past Saved. All new UI text is plain English ("Ordered", "Mark given") -- no raw enum values exposed, per the ground rule.

**Tests:** `lib/gifts/convert.test.ts` (5 new tests: field mapping, status always "given" regardless of input suggestion status, retailer-present/absent notes text, null-category passthrough). Full suite: 411/411 across 43 files.

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm exec eslint`, `pnpm exec vitest run` (411 tests, 43 files), `pnpm run build` all clean. Committed `8780fc3`, pushed to `main`. Migration applied live to production Supabase. Deployed to production via Vercel, aliased to lifeos-seven-rho.vercel.app.

**Live-verified (production):** saved a real suggestion from Callan's list ("Kids' Fishing Starter Rod and Tackle Set with Personalized Tackle Box"), confirmed the Saved gifts count incremented and the card showed the "Saved" badge with Mark ordered/Move back/Dismiss. Clicked "Mark ordered" -- toast confirmed, card flipped to the "Ordered" badge with Mark given/Move back/Dismiss. Clicked "Mark given" -- toast read "Marked as given. Added to their Gift history.", the card dropped off the Saved gifts list entirely, and Callan's person page immediately showed the new Gift history entry ("Kids' Fishing Starter Rod and Tackle Set with Personalized Tackle Box -- Birthday · Thursday, August 27 · $115.00") with no raw enum or ISO-date text anywhere. Confirmed the Saved gifts count returned to the household's genuine baseline of 2 after cleanup. Deleted the test Gift history entry via the app's own Remove -> Delete confirmation dialog (matches D-052/D-059/D-085 precedent for UI-based deletes not needing the extra confirmation gate); confirmed removed on reload; the household's other saved/ordered gifts and existing Gift history were untouched throughout.

## D-087 | 2026-08-30 | P3-5: household notification delivery-channel preferences + real dispatch on the daily brief

**Problem (verbatim from spec):** "Real notification delivery. Add delivery channel preferences (email/push) in Settings and actually send the daily brief." On investigation, the dispatcher architecture (Section 10.1) and every channel adapter already existed: `lib/notifications/dispatch.ts` fans out to `channels/{in-app,email,push,sms}.ts`, the email adapter already sends through Resend when `RESEND_API_KEY` is set (stubbing to console otherwise per Section 12.9's "stub behind interface if no key"), push is a documented v2 no-op pending the Expo shell (Section 10.3), and SMS is a documented no-op pending A2P 10DLC registration (Section 10.4) -- and `lib/brief/generate.ts` already called `dispatchNotification` after creating every brief. The actual gaps were narrower than the spec line implied: the brief's channel list was a hardcoded `["in_app", "email"]` literal with no household-level opt-out, and there was no Settings UI at all for delivery preferences.

**Fix -- data model:** added `households.notification_channels notification_channel[] not null default '{email}'` via `supabase/migrations/20260830000005_household_notification_channels.sql`, applied live to production. `in_app` is deliberately never stored in this column -- it backs the notification bell itself (not an external channel a household would opt out of) and is added unconditionally in code, so this column only ever needs to hold the channels that are genuinely optional. Added `notification_channels: NotificationChannel[]` to `HouseholdRow`/`HouseholdInsert` (`lib/db/database.types.ts`) and `notification_channels: z.array(notificationChannelSchema).optional()` to `householdInsertSchema` (`lib/db/schemas.ts`).

**Fix -- single source of truth for the channel list:** `lib/brief/generate.ts` now builds the dispatch channel list as `Array.from(new Set(["in_app", ...household.notification_channels]))` instead of the old hardcoded literal -- one place decides which channels a brief goes out on, driven by the household's own saved preference rather than a constant duplicated across call sites (there is currently only the one call site, but this removes the literal so a second one can't silently disagree).

**Fix -- Settings UI:** added a "Notification delivery" section to the settings form (`app/(app)/settings/settings-form.tsx`) with an "Email" checkbox (defaults to the household's saved preference) and a disabled, greyed-out "Push notifications (coming soon -- requires the mobile app)" checkbox with explanatory copy, so the UI is honest about push not doing anything yet rather than offering a toggle that silently no-ops. `updateHouseholdSettingsAction` (`app/(app)/settings/actions.ts`) reads the `notifyEmail` checkbox from `FormData` (present only when checked) and persists `["email"]` or `[]` accordingly; the disabled push checkbox never submits, so it can't be accidentally persisted as an enabled channel before the feature is real.

**Known limitation (documented, not fixed -- genuine external dependency, not a code gap):** actual email delivery still requires a real `RESEND_API_KEY` in the production environment, which is not currently set (confirmed via `vercel env ls production` -- only `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, and the two Supabase public vars exist). Until Richard signs up for Resend (or another transactional-email provider) and adds the key to Vercel, the email channel continues to stub to console exactly as it did before this change -- this is the same documented behavior as push (Section 10.3, blocked on the Expo shell) and SMS (Section 10.4, blocked on A2P 10DLC registration): a real external prerequisite outside this codebase, not something more code here can fix. The preference toggle, dispatcher wiring, and Resend integration code are all real and ready to work the moment a key is added -- no further code changes would be needed.

**Tests:** no new automated tests -- this is Settings-form UI plus a household-preference passthrough into an existing, already-tested dispatcher (`lib/notifications/dispatch.test.ts`, unchanged and still covering the channel fan-out and stub behavior); consistent with the existing convention that server actions are verified live rather than unit-tested. Full suite: 411/411 across 43 files (unchanged from D-086, confirming no regression).

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm exec eslint`, `pnpm exec vitest run` (411 tests, 43 files), `pnpm run build` all clean. Committed `821b310`, pushed to `main`. Migration applied live to production Supabase. Deployed to production via Vercel, aliased to lifeos-seven-rho.vercel.app.

**Live-verified (production):** on `/settings`, the new "Notification delivery" section rendered with Email pre-checked (matching the migration's `{email}` default) and Push shown disabled with "coming soon" copy. Unchecked Email, clicked Save -- "Saved." confirmation appeared. Reloaded the page from a fresh navigation: Email correctly came back unchecked, confirming the preference round-trips through the database rather than just updating client state. Re-checked Email and saved again -- confirmed checked on reload, restoring the household's real preference. No plain-text secrets, enum values, or raw booleans were shown anywhere in the UI.

## D-088 | 2026-08-30 | P3-6: calendar import (Google Calendar / iCal)

**Problem (verbatim from spec):** "Calendar import (Google Calendar / iCal) so the weekend planner knows real free/busy time." LifeOS had no way to bring in events from an external calendar -- the weekend planner and opportunity detector only ever saw manually-entered `calendar_events` rows.

**Fix -- data model:** `supabase/migrations/20260830000006_calendar_feeds.sql` (applied live to production) added `'external'` to the `calendar_event_type` enum via a single-statement `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` (same safe pattern as D-084's `suggestion_status` addition of `'ordered'`) and created a `calendar_feeds` table (household_id, label, feed_url, last_sync_status, last_synced_at, last_sync_error) with RLS mirroring `calendar_events`: all household members can read feeds, only owner/adult roles can create/update/delete -- the feed URL is treated as credential-like (Google's iCal "secret address" embeds a per-calendar secret token), consistent with the ground rule of not exposing anything sensitive to every household member.

**Fix -- import engine:** `lib/calendar/ics-import.ts` parses ICS feeds with SSRF guards (rejects non-HTTP(S) URLs and private/loopback IP ranges before fetching) and maps VEVENTs into `calendar_events` rows typed `'external'`. `lib/calendar/feed-sync.ts` implements `syncCalendarFeed(feedId)`: on each sync it deletes all previously-imported events for that feed and reinserts fresh ones from the current feed contents -- a delete-then-reinsert resync rather than an upsert, since ICS feeds don't guarantee stable per-event ids across fetches and this keeps the logic simple and idempotent.

**Fix -- single source of truth:** neither `lib/planner/generate.ts` (weekend planner) nor `lib/opportunities/detect.ts` (opportunity detection) filter `calendar_events` by provenance -- both already read every event in the household's calendar regardless of source. Imported events therefore flow into free/busy checks and opportunity suggestions automatically, with zero changes needed to either file -- exactly the "make one the single source of truth" principle from the ground rules, since `calendar_events` was already that source of truth and import just adds a new writer to it.

**Fix -- Settings UI and actions:** `app/(app)/settings/calendar-feed-actions.ts` adds `addCalendarFeedAction` (validates via `calendarFeedInsertSchema`, creates the feed, then immediately calls `syncCalendarFeed` so the user sees results right away rather than waiting for the next cron tick), `syncCalendarFeedNowAction(feedId)`, and `deleteCalendarFeedAction(feedId)` (deletes the feed's imported events first, then the feed row itself, so a removed feed can never leave orphaned imported events behind). All three are gated by a `requireOwnerOrAdult()` guard mirrored from `household-invite-actions.ts`, and every mutation error path goes through `friendlyMutationError`. `app/(app)/settings/calendar-feeds.tsx` renders the "Connected calendars" section: an add-feed form, a `SyncNowButton`, and a `RemoveFeedButton` wrapping the existing `ConfirmDeleteButton` two-click-confirm component (same pattern every other destructive action in the app uses per D-036). Raw enum sync statuses ("never"/"ok"/"error") are translated to friendly labels+badges via a `statusLabel` map, and `last_synced_at` renders through `formatDistanceToNow` -- never a raw ISO timestamp -- consistent with the ground rule against showing raw enum values or ISO dates anywhere in the UI. Imported events display an "Imported" badge on `/calendar` via the existing `EVENT_TYPE_LABELS.external` label mapping.

**Fix -- background sync:** `app/api/cron/calendar-sync/route.ts` mirrors `app/api/cron/gift-scan/route.ts` exactly (Bearer `CRON_SECRET` auth check, service-role Supabase client, iterates every household's feeds, per-feed try/catch so one broken feed doesn't block others, returns a JSON summary). Registered in `vercel.json` at `0 12 * * *` (daily), the fifth cron entry alongside the existing four.

**No OAuth needed:** unlike P3-5's Resend-API-key blocker, this feature has no external-dependency gap. Google Calendar's plain HTTPS "secret iCal address" (and any other public .ics URL) requires no OAuth app registration or API key -- the feature is fully buildable and immediately usable with whatever calendar URL a household member already has.

**Tests:** `lib/calendar/ics-import.test.ts` -- 19 new tests covering ICS parsing, SSRF-guard rejection of private/loopback/non-HTTP(S) URLs, and event-mapping edge cases. Full suite: 430/430 across 44 files (411 prior + 19 new, no regressions).

**Verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm exec eslint`, `pnpm exec vitest run` (430 tests, 44 files), `pnpm run build` all clean -- `/api/cron/calendar-sync` present in the build's route list. Committed `12052eb` ("P3-6: calendar import (Google Calendar / iCal)", 15 files changed, 1055 insertions), pushed to `main`. Migration `20260830000006_calendar_feeds.sql` applied live to production Supabase (adds `'external'` enum value + `calendar_feeds` table + RLS policies). Deployed to production via Vercel, aliased to lifeos-seven-rho.vercel.app.

**Live-verified (production):** on `/settings`, the "Connected calendars" section rendered with correct copy and "No calendars connected yet." Clicked "Add a calendar", entered a real public Google-hosted US holidays feed (`https://calendar.google.com/calendar/ical/en.usa%23holiday%40group.v.calendar.google.com/public/basic.ics`) as a test feed labeled "US Holidays test", and submitted. The button showed an "Adding..." pending state, then a "Calendar connected and synced." success message appeared with the feed listed showing a "Synced" badge and "Synced less than a minute ago" (human-relative time, not raw ISO) -- confirming `addCalendarFeedAction`'s immediate `syncCalendarFeed` call worked end-to-end against a real external feed. Navigated to `/calendar`, jumped to September 2026, and confirmed "Labor Day" (Sep 7, 2026) appeared with an "Imported" badge, all-day, alongside the household's own regular custody events -- confirming imported events flow into the existing calendar UI with no special-casing needed. No raw enum values, ISO timestamps, or Markdown syntax were visible anywhere in the flow. Cleanup: clicked "Remove" on the test feed, got the two-click confirm dialog ("Remove 'US Holidays test'? This disconnects the calendar and deletes every event it imported. Your own events aren't affected."), confirmed removal -- a "Calendar removed." toast appeared and a fresh page load confirmed "No calendars connected yet." again. Reloaded `/calendar` for September 7 and confirmed the imported "Labor Day" event was gone while the household's own "Em with Mel"/"Cal with Mel" events remained untouched -- confirming `deleteCalendarFeedAction`'s cascade (delete imported events, then the feed row) worked correctly and did not touch any non-imported data.

## D-084 | 2026-08-30 | P3-2: merged duplicate Golf activity (Fiddlers Green + Oakway)

**Problem (verbatim from spec):** "One activity, many locations" — golf was represented as two separate activities ("Golf at Fiddlers Green" and "Golf at Oakway Golf Course"), so it showed up twice on the Activities page and produced duplicate golf cards on the Opportunities page for the same day, unlike Shooting (id `330dcf3a-a0c9-40ee-85f5-cb27abd472f8`), which already correctly models one activity with two location badges (Creswell Clay Target Sports, Izaak Walton League Eugene Chapter).

**Investigation:** confirmed via `information_schema` that the only tables referencing `user_activities.id` are `activity_locations.user_activity_id` (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), `opportunities.activity_id` (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), and `calendar_events.related_activity_id` / `interactions.activity_id` (both `ON DELETE SET NULL`). `contact_cadences` has no activity reference at all (an earlier assumption in this doc's plan was wrong). Live counts for the Oakway row (`5ff9e499-dab0-4e81-88a5-8848914ad59a`) before merge: 1 `activity_locations` row, 3 `opportunities` rows (one per open suggestion day: Aug 30, Aug 31, Sep 5 — each a duplicate of an already-existing Fiddlers Green opportunity for the same day), 0 `calendar_events`/`interactions` rows.

**Decision:** kept `5071ebc2-26fd-44cc-9a9e-a2a6cad9ad1b` (enjoyment_rank=10, typical_duration_minutes=120, preferred_companions=[Callan, Emlyn] — the richer/more-used row) and merged the Oakway row into it, following the Shooting reference pattern exactly:
1. `UPDATE activity_locations SET user_activity_id = '5071ebc2-...' WHERE user_activity_id = '5ff9e499-...'` — moved the "Eugene, Oregon" location onto the surviving row so it now carries two location badges (Fiddlers Green, Eugene, Oregon), same shape as Shooting's two badges.
2. `UPDATE user_activities SET activity_type = 'Golf' WHERE id = '5071ebc2-...'` — renamed from "Golf at Fiddlers Green" to the generic "Golf", since the activity now spans two locations (`activity_type` is the only name field on `user_activities` — there's no separate display-name column).
3. `DELETE FROM user_activities WHERE id = '5ff9e499-...'` — cascaded automatically to delete its 1 `activity_locations` row (already moved off by step 1, so nothing left to cascade) and its 3 duplicate `opportunities` rows. Letting the 3 duplicate opportunities cascade-delete rather than redirecting them to the surviving activity_id was intentional: they're auto-generated daily suggestions regenerated by the normal opportunity-detection job, and redirecting would have created true duplicate (activity_id, for_date) rows on the surviving activity instead of removing the duplication the merge exists to fix.

**Known limitation (documented, not fixed — same limitation Shooting already has):** the merged "Golf" activity has one `enjoyment_rank`/`typical_duration_minutes` pair (10/120, inherited from the Fiddlers Green row) even though a quick Fiddlers Green round and a 5-hour Oakway round are very different outings. No schema change to support per-location duration/enjoyment — out of scope for this pass, consistent with the existing Shooting activity's same unaddressed limitation.

**No code change required:** `lib/opportunities/present.ts`'s `familyKeyFor` dedup workaround (mentioned in earlier planning) is unaffected — it's a display-layer safety net for exactly this kind of duplication and stays in place even though the underlying data no longer needs it. This was a pure production data fix, not a code change, so no typecheck/lint/test/build/deploy cycle applies here.

**Live-verified (production):** `/activities` now shows exactly one "Golf" card with two location badges (Fiddlers Green, Eugene, Oregon) where there used to be two separate cards. `/opportunities` no longer shows two golf suggestions for the same day — each of Sun Aug 30, Mon Aug 31, and Sat Sep 5 now shows at most one golf card (still titled "Golf at Fiddlers Green — good conditions ..." because those specific opportunity rows were generated before the rename; the next scheduled opportunity-detection run will generate fresh titles from the new "Golf" name — this is stale display text on already-generated rows, not a bug, and no forced regeneration was done to avoid burning the household's daily AI-suggestion quota).

## D-089 | 2026-08-30 | P3-7: brain-dump transcript persistence + re-run

**Problem (verbatim from spec):** "Keep the original brain-dump transcript stored with the batch, allow re-running it." Previously the brain-dump page held the transcript and parsed items only in client component state — refreshing, navigating away, or an AI-call failure lost the typed/dictated text with no way to recover it or try parsing again without retyping.

**Schema + RLS:** new `brain_dump_batches` table (`id`, `household_id`, `created_by_person_id`, `transcript`, `parse_status` [check: `pending`/`ready`/`unavailable`/`error`], `parse_message`, `items` jsonb default `'[]'`, `saved_count` default 0, `created_at`, `updated_at` + `set_updated_at` trigger + index on `(household_id, created_at desc)`). RLS: any household member can read and can insert their own batch; update/delete requires creator-or-owner/adult. This mirrors the general household-member permission model rather than the stricter owner/adult-only gate `calendar_feeds` uses (D-088), because brain dump itself has no owner/adult restriction anywhere else in the app — anyone in the household can already record and save brain-dump items, so gating batch deletion to owner/adult-only would have been an inconsistent, unrequested restriction. Creator-or-owner/adult on update/delete still prevents one member from silently editing or deleting another member's in-progress dump.

**Single source of truth:** on re-run, `/api/brain-dump/parse` loads the stored `transcript` from the batch row by `batchId` and re-parses that — it never accepts a different transcript string from the client for an existing batch. The stored transcript is authoritative; the client's re-run button only supplies the `batchId`, not text. This avoids the two-source-of-truth risk of a client-held transcript silently drifting from what's persisted.

**Batch-creation-before-AI-call ordering:** for a fresh dump, the batch row is created (with the transcript and `parse_status: "pending"`) *before* the AI call is made, not after. This means the transcript survives an AI failure (timeout, budget-exceeded, malformed JSON, schema-validation failure) — every one of those error paths still updates and persists the already-created batch row (via a shared `finish(status, message, items)` helper) instead of losing the transcript because the call blew up before anything was saved.

**`activeBatchId` clearing convention:** the client tracks the in-progress batch via `activeBatchId` state, cleared whenever the user starts editing fresh input — on textarea `onChange`, on speech-recognition `onresult`, and in `startOver()`. This ensures editing or dictating new text always starts a brand-new batch on next Process rather than accidentally overwriting or re-running a stale batch tied to old text.

**`saved_count` as best-effort tracking:** `/api/brain-dump/execute` accepts an optional `batchId` and, after a successful save, best-effort increments the batch's `saved_count` via a read-then-write helper (`.catch(() => {})` — never fails the save itself). This is a summary counter for the history list ("N saved"), not a full per-item saved/unsaved state — acceptable because the history UI only needs to show "how much of this batch did I act on," not track which specific items were saved.

**UI additions:** `/brain-dump` now fetches and renders a "Past brain dumps" history section (visible when no items are actively being reviewed) showing each batch's transcript preview, relative time (`formatDistanceToNow` — never a raw ISO timestamp), a friendly status badge (`BATCH_STATUS_LABEL`/`BATCH_STATUS_BADGE_VARIANT` map translating `pending`/`ready`/`unavailable`/`error` to human labels — never the raw enum value, per the ground rule), `saved_count`, a Re-run button, and a delete button using the existing `ConfirmDeleteButton` component (same two-click-confirm pattern as every other destructive action in the app, per D-036).

**Tests/verification:** `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit`, `pnpm exec eslint .`, `pnpm vitest run` (430 tests, 44 files, all passing), `pnpm run build` all clean — `/brain-dump`, `/api/brain-dump/parse`, `/api/brain-dump/execute` all present as dynamic routes. Committed `dc39914`, pushed to `main`. Migration `20260830000007_brain_dump_batches.sql` applied live to production Supabase.

**Live-verified (production):** typed a test transcript ("pick up dry cleaning tomorrow") and clicked Process — got one parsed calendar-event item. Reloaded `/brain-dump` and confirmed the "Past brain dumps" history section showed the batch with a "Processed" badge (not raw "ready"). Clicked Re-run — the batch re-parsed from the stored transcript with no retyping required, producing an equivalent item. Clicked Save — got a "Saved" checkmark and "All done · 1 saved" summary; returning to the brain-dump page confirmed the history entry now showed "1 saved" alongside "Processed". Clicked Delete on the batch — the `ConfirmDeleteButton` dialog opened with the correct copy ("Delete this brain dump? This removes the saved transcript from your history. Anything you already saved from it stays untouched." with Cancel / red "Delete brain dump"); confirmed deletion, got a "Brain dump removed." toast, and the batch disappeared from the history list. No raw enum values, ISO timestamps, or Markdown syntax were visible anywhere in the flow. Cleanup: deleted the test "Pick up dry cleaning" calendar event created by the Save step (via the calendar's own two-click delete-confirm flow) — confirmed removed with an "Event deleted." toast, leaving the household's existing calendar events untouched.

## D-090 | 2026-08-30 | Closing two long-pending live re-verifications (D-049 FAB, D-050 home address)

**Context:** A full audit of the spec against DECISIONS.md (looking for any "known limitation," "not fixed," "still pending" language) surfaced two items from early in the build (2026-08-26) whose code had shipped and typechecked/tested/built clean at the time, but whose live re-verification was explicitly logged as "pending for next round" and never revisited in writing since — a genuine documentation gap even though the underlying features had clearly been exercised (and implicitly proven working) by dozens of later live-verification passes on the same production account.

**D-049 (FAB positioning at tablet/desktop widths) — now confirmed live:** re-tested the floating "Quick capture" button on the production Brief page at 820px (tablet) and 1440px (desktop) using `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride`. At both widths the button sits correctly within the app's centered `max-w-md` column, directly above the bottom nav, with no drift outside the column edge — matching the D-049 fix exactly.

**D-050 (home address unblocking weekend planning + brief weather) — now confirmed live:** Settings shows a real saved home address ("1365 Umpqua Ave Eugene OR 97408"), confirming the geocoding save flow works and has persisted data (not just passed tests). The Brief page shows real weather ("Sunny · High 78°F") and two real Opportunities cards with live weather scores (94/100), real drive times (12 min, 7 min), and overall scores (85/100) — confirming both `getNwsForecast` (brief weather) and the weekend/opportunity planner's drive-time and weather-window logic are fully working end-to-end against the saved coordinates, not stubbed or erroring.

**No code changes needed** — both items were already correct; this pass only closes the documentation gap so "still pending for next round" doesn't linger indefinitely as a false-looking open item for anyone reading the log.

**Remaining genuine gap, unchanged:** P3-5's actual email send is still blocked on a missing `RESEND_API_KEY` in the Vercel production environment (D-087) — a real external prerequisite (the user needs to sign up for Resend or another transactional-email provider and add the key to Vercel) rather than a code gap; all dispatcher code is ready to work the moment a key is added.

## D-091 | 2026-08-31 | Email deliverability root cause: self person record had no email

**Problem:** while investigating whether real email delivery (P3-5) requires buying a domain, found that a test email to the account owner (Richard) never actually sent — `briefs.delivered_channels` recorded only `["in_app"]` even though Settings > Notification delivery > Email is checked and `RESEND_API_KEY`/`RESEND_FROM_EMAIL` are set. Root cause was **not** domain verification — it was that `app/onboarding/actions.ts` created the owner's own `people` row (`relationship_type: "self"`) without an `email`, even though the Supabase auth `user` object already has it in hand at that point. `lib/notifications/channels/email.ts` correctly requires `person.email` to send, so the owner's own notifications had nowhere to go — a real gap, distinct from every other household member (Mel, Jackie) whose contact email is entered explicitly via the People page.

**Fix (single source of truth):** `createHouseholdAction` now passes `email: user.email ?? null` into `peopleRepo.create`, so a new owner's self-record always carries the same email as their login, with no separate "your own email" field needed anywhere in the UI. One-time backfill applied directly to production for the existing row (`people.id = 4eee087f-...`, set to `rwsmith964@gmail.com`, matching the account's own login email) since this bug predates the fix and existing rows aren't touched by new-signup code.

**Domain verification finding (unblocks beta-testing decision):** confirmed via Resend's own docs that sending to any recipient other than the Resend account's own signup email requires a verified sending domain — this is universal across every provider, not Resend-specific, and no free/no-cost domain workaround exists. Set `RESEND_FROM_EMAIL=LifeOS <onboarding@resend.dev>` in Vercel production (Resend's built-in no-domain-required test sender) so self-only delivery works today at zero cost while the product name is still undecided. Real delivery to Mel or Jackie remains blocked until a domain (any cheap, brand-decoupled one works — the sending domain is invisible infrastructure, not the product name) is purchased and its SPF/DKIM/DMARC records verified with Resend.
